Aariya Proactive Communication & Action System
The fundamental change is that Aariya will no longer depend entirely on you opening the app and starting a conversation.
She will be able to monitor authorized activities, recognize events, learn your preferences, contact you when appropriate, understand your reply, take authorized action, verify the result, and remember what happened.
THE CONTINUOUS LOOP
Observe
Monitor authorized systems, tasks, and events

Understand and decide
Context, urgency, confidence, impact, preferences

Communicate with you
Message, notification, or encrypted Internet voice

Understand your response
Continue the same conversation and context

Authorize, act, and verify
Use existing reasoning and execution pipelines

Remember and learn
Incident history, outcomes, feedback, preferences


The loop repeats while Aariya has authorization, available evidence, and a functioning monitoring path.

1. The architecture we should build

This separates the system into components with distinct responsibilities.
Component	Responsibility
Existing Aariya Brain	Reasoning, context, memory, dialogue and planning
Event interpreter	Determine what happened using evidence
Importance engine	Evaluate urgency, impact, confidence and appropriate response time
Policy engine	Enforce permissions, approval requirements and interruption rules
Escalation engine	Select messaging, notifications, voice calls and retries
Communication Gateway	Manage authenticated, encrypted real-time communication
Flutter application	Present calls, messages, evidence and action controls
Guardian	Independently detect core failures and trigger available alerts
Incident memory	Preserve event history and support controlled learning
The Guardian must not depend on the main AI Core to detect that the AI Core has failed. Its monitoring, alerting and deployment should be sufficiently independent to handle the failures it is designed to detect.
2. How Aariya should decide to contact you
Aariya should not use a single boolean such as is_emergency. Each event needs a structured assessment.
Evidence
What actually happened? Which source confirms it?


Severity and impact
What could be damaged, delayed, lost or affected?


Urgency
How long can this safely wait?


Confidence
How reliable is the evidence and interpretation?


Reversibility
Can a proposed response be safely undone?


Personal preferences
Has the user confirmed a preferred response for this event?


Response policy
What communication and action permissions apply?



These factors inform the decision; they should not be collapsed into a score that can override a mandatory safety rule.
For example:
- A routine task failure may produce a message.
- A consequential failure may require an urgent notification.
- A verified, time-critical incident may trigger an immediate voice call.
- An ambiguous event may prompt Aariya to explain the uncertainty rather than assert that an emergency has occurred.
- A resolved incident should cancel pending escalation where appropriate and send an update instead.
The escalation engine must also deduplicate events, combine simultaneous incidents, respect the user's interruption settings where possible, and stop retries when an incident is resolved or acknowledged.
3. The learning system
Your hybrid learning decision is important: Aariya should learn from experience without silently turning guesses into permanent instructions.

Observed preference
A single or repeated observation. It is a hypothesis, not an instruction.




Learned preference
A pattern supported by incident history, feedback and outcomes, with a recorded confidence and provenance.




Confirmed preference
An explicit choice from you, with scope and revision history. It remains subject to mandatory security and safety constraints.



Aariya can automatically adjust low-risk personalization, such as her conversational tone or whether ordinary updates are grouped together. If she wants to make a material change to escalation behavior, she should ask you and wait for confirmation.
If she learns that you prefer calls for database failures, that preference must not authorize her to make an unsafe database modification. Communication preferences and action permissions are different things.
Learning also needs to account for contradictory feedback, changing circumstances, outdated preferences and incorrect event classifications. Every material adjustment should be traceable to its supporting incidents or explicit instruction.
4. Voice calls, messages and conversational continuity
The call is a new way to communicate with the existing Aariya Brain, not a separate AI assistant.
When you answer a call:
1. The system authenticates the session and associates it with the incident.
2. Aariya loads the relevant context and the reason for escalation.
3. Speech recognition feeds your response into her existing conversation pipeline.
4. Her cognitive system interprets the response, considers the context and asks for clarification if necessary.
5. The authorization layer checks any requested action.
6. The existing execution pipeline acts and verifies the result.
7. Aariya explains what happened and updates the incident record.
Her voice pipeline should continue to use contextual understanding, internal emotional state, voice direction, natural timing and speech generation. Urgency should influence her delivery without turning every call into a robotic alert.
The Flutter application should also provide:
- A persistent conversation thread containing proactive messages and call history.
- A way to answer in text instead of voice.
- An incident details view with relevant logs, evidence and timestamps.
- A way to approve, reject or clarify proposed actions.
- A way to return to the conversation that was interrupted.
- Synchronization across authenticated devices without creating conflicting conversations.
When you say, "Show me what happened," Aariya should send or display the authorized evidence in the active app session, with suitable access controls and redaction for sensitive data.
5. Encryption and security boundaries
Your security requirement applies to messages, voice, files and conversation data. It needs to be designed into the protocol, not added as a final encryption wrapper.
The intended trust boundary is:
Your authenticated device
Device identity, protected keys, secure session

Encrypted communication
Authenticated real-time channel, replay protection, session lifecycle

Trusted Aariya endpoint
Authorized decryption and processing by the Brain


There is one technical distinction we must preserve: because Aariya's Brain needs to understand your messages and audio, it must receive usable plaintext at the trusted processing endpoint. Transport encryption alone does not make this end-to-end encryption.
The exact cryptographic design must define whether the Gateway is trusted with content or acts only as an encrypted relay. If the Gateway can decrypt messages, it is part of the trusted boundary. If you want only the device and AI Core to access content, the protocol must protect content from the Gateway too, while still allowing it to route calls.
We also need encrypted storage, strict service-to-service authentication, key rotation, access auditing, secure deletion policies, and a clear policy for any external speech or AI provider. No intermediate provider should receive conversation content unless explicitly authorized by the architecture and policy.
6. Autonomous action boundaries
Your instruction, "If Aariya cannot decide for herself, she should ask me and wait," should become an enforceable backend rule.
Action class	Expected behavior
Read-only, pre-authorized	Inspect permitted data and investigate
Low-risk, pre-authorized	Execute within defined limits and verify
Uncertain or underspecified	Ask a clarifying question and wait
Restricted or high-impact	Require explicit authorization and any required additional verification
Prohibited	Refuse to execute, explain why and offer a safe alternative
Voice recognition is not sufficient authentication for sensitive operations. Aariya should validate device/session identity, check permissions and request stronger confirmation when required. A spoken "yes" must not bypass authorization rules.
If you tell her to "fix it," she should investigate what she can safely investigate. If multiple materially different actions are possible, she should explain the options and wait for your decision.
7. Background operation and the Guardian
The system should be designed for the Flutter app to receive an incoming event when it is in the background or not actively open, subject to the operating system's notification and background-execution rules.
Internet voice calling will require suitable call signaling, media transport, app lifecycle handling and platform integration. A notification by itself does not guarantee that an app can ring like a native phone call.
The independent Guardian should monitor:
- AI Core and backend availability.
- Communication Gateway health.
- Critical dependencies and recovery attempts.
- Delivery and acknowledgement of urgent alerts.
- Security and integrity events.
- Its own monitoring and notification paths.
The Guardian needs an independent way to deliver alerts. It should not depend on the same failed process, credentials or network path for every recovery attempt.
For offline delivery, Aariya can use alternate channels only when they have been configured, authorized and are available. SMS or traditional phone calling would require a suitable provider and separate integration; neither can be guaranteed when the phone has no connectivity.

8. What Aariya becomes after implementation
This is the real evolution.

Today: Reactive companion
You initiate a conversation, provide context and request an action.




After implementation: Proactive companion
She monitors authorized tasks, detects relevant events and reaches you without waiting for you to open the app.




With action integration: Operational agent
She investigates, asks for decisions, executes permitted operations and verifies their outcomes.




With incident memory and learning: Adaptive system
She improves her personalization and future decisions using evidence, outcomes and your confirmed preferences.



This does not mean that Aariya becomes conscious or can guarantee that she will always understand the world correctly. It means her software gains persistent monitoring, proactive communication, controlled agency and a feedback loop that can improve her behavior over time.
9. Implementation roadmap
I recommend building this in the following order, integrating with your existing Flutter app and FastAPI backend.
1. Phase 1 — Inspect and baseline the existing system
   Map the actual backend modules, Flutter navigation and lifecycle, authentication, memory, voice pipeline and available execution tools. Identify what can be reused and what is missing.
2. Phase 2 — Event and incident infrastructure
   Implement structured events, durable incident records, evidence references, deduplication, event status and recovery tracking.
3. Phase 3 — Importance and policy engines
   Add severity, urgency, confidence, user preferences, interruption rules, capability permissions and explicit approval gates.
4. Phase 4 — Messaging and Flutter integration
   Build the persistent proactive conversation thread, incident details, acknowledgement, replies and device synchronization.
5. Phase 5 — Secure real-time voice
   Integrate authenticated call signaling, encrypted media, call lifecycle, contextual speech recognition and the existing natural voice pipeline.
6. Phase 6 — Guardian and delivery escalation
   Add independent health monitoring, retry policies, incident cancellation, fallback channels and tests for core or gateway failure.
7. Phase 7 — Conversational action execution
   Connect incoming voice and text responses to the existing reasoning, planning, authorization, execution and verification pipeline.
8. Phase 8 — Incident memory and hybrid learning
   Add observed, learned and confirmed preferences, feedback handling, provenance and safeguards against unapproved changes to policy.
9. Phase 9 — Multi-device and broader event sources
   Expand to desktop and web clients, additional authorized monitoring sources, and carefully scoped personal or physical-world integrations.
10. Phase 10 — Security and failure validation
    Test authentication, encryption, recovery, interrupted calls, duplicate alerts, offline devices, unauthorized commands, data leakage and Guardian independence.
Each phase should have measurable acceptance tests. We should not mark a capability as implemented just because its module or interface exists.
10. One important next step before writing code
I don't need more conceptual answers from you. Your requirements are sufficiently defined.
What I need now is the actual state of your existing Aariya codebase, because you have explicitly required real implementation without mock, synthetic or hardcoded operational data.
Please provide the current Aariya backend and Flutter project files, or connect the repository containing them. The useful starting points are:
- FastAPI entry point and backend module structure.
- Existing agent/controller, memory, context and Redis modules.
- Voice recognition, voice state, voice director and speech-generation modules.
- Flutter app structure, networking, authentication and existing conversation screens.
- Existing real execution tools and their authorization logic.
- Current configuration and deployment setup, with secrets removed.
Once those are available, I can map the specification to the actual code, identify reusable modules, establish the missing interfaces, and create the first implementation milestone with concrete file paths, code and tests.


# Aariya Proactive Communication & Action System
## Engineering Specification and Architecture — v1.0

**Project:** Aariya AI Companion  
**Purpose:** Extend the existing Aariya Brain and Flutter application with proactive event monitoring, secure Internet voice calls, persistent messaging, authorized action execution, independent health monitoring, incident memory and controlled learning.

**Status:** Architecture and requirements baseline  
**Implementation policy:** Existing-code-first, production-oriented, no fabricated operational data.

---

# 1. Engineering objectives

Aariya must be able to:

1. Monitor explicitly authorized systems, tasks, services and information sources.
2. Detect events using actual observations and distinguish evidence from inference.
3. Evaluate importance, urgency, confidence, impact and required response time.
4. Initiate messages, push notifications and Internet voice calls without waiting for the user to open the app.
5. Continue conversations through the existing Flutter application.
6. Understand spoken or written replies using the existing Aariya Brain.
7. Execute permitted actions through the existing reasoning and execution pipeline.
8. Ask for clarification or authorization when required and wait for a response.
9. Verify execution outcomes rather than assuming that a command succeeded.
10. Preserve incident history and learn personal preferences within defined boundaries.
11. Detect failures of the main Brain using an independently operated Guardian.
12. Protect messages, calls, files, credentials and stored information.
13. Maintain consistent incident state across devices, process restarts and communication failures.
14. Provide reviewable evidence for decisions, actions, delivery attempts and learning changes.

## 1.1 Non-goals

This system must not:

- Create a second, disconnected Aariya Brain.
- Replace the existing Flutter application.
- Treat a language model's opinion as verified operational evidence.
- Give an LLM unrestricted access to tools or credentials.
- Treat a missed call as user approval.
- Treat a successful API response as proof that the intended outcome occurred.
- Promise delivery when no communication channel is available.
- Silently change security policy through learning.
- Claim that Aariya is conscious or infallible.
- Use fabricated operational data to make the system appear functional.

---

# 2. Existing architecture to preserve

The existing project has a FastAPI cognitive backend, Flutter mobile client and an established natural voice and conversational architecture. Existing backend areas include agent/controller, retrieval, confidence, LLM and Redis-related components. Voice-related modules include state, conversation context, voice direction, continuity, prosody, timing, intonation and voice generation.

These are the known architectural areas, not a verified inventory of the current repository. Exact filenames, dependencies, APIs and interfaces must be inspected before implementation.

The new system must integrate with the existing components wherever they already perform the required function.

## 2.1 Target architecture

```mermaid
flowchart TB
    subgraph Sources["Authorized Event Sources"]
        SYS["System and Service Events"]
        TASK["Tasks and Workflows"]
        SEC["Security Monitoring"]
        EXT["Authorized External Sources"]
    end

    subgraph Backend["Aariya Backend"]
        ING["Event Ingestion"]
        BUS["Durable Event Processing"]
        CORR["Correlation and Evidence"]
        IMP["Importance and Confidence"]
        POLICY["Authorization and Policy"]
        ESC["Escalation and Delivery"]
        CORE["Existing Aariya Brain"]
        EXEC["Authorized Execution"]
        MEM["Incident Memory and Learning"]
    end

    subgraph Communication["Communication Infrastructure"]
        GW["Communication Gateway"]
        VOICE["Real-time Voice Session"]
        PUSH["Push and Messaging"]
    end

    subgraph Client["Existing Flutter Application"]
        UI["Conversation and Incident UI"]
        RESP["User Responses and Approvals"]
    end

    subgraph Independent["Independent Reliability Layer"]
        GUARD["Guardian / Watchdog"]
        ALERT["Independent Alert Path"]
    end

    SYS --> ING
    TASK --> ING
    SEC --> ING
    EXT --> ING
    ING --> BUS
    BUS --> CORR
    CORR --> IMP
    IMP --> POLICY
    POLICY --> ESC
    ESC --> GW
    GW --> VOICE
    GW --> PUSH
    VOICE <--> UI
    PUSH --> UI
    UI <--> RESP
    RESP --> CORE
    CORE --> POLICY
    POLICY --> EXEC
    EXEC --> MEM
    MEM --> CORE
    GUARD -. monitors .-> Backend
    GUARD --> ALERT
    ALERT --> GW
```

### Architectural rules

- The existing Brain remains responsible for reasoning, context and natural conversation.
- Event processing and policy enforcement must not depend solely on LLM output.
- The authorization engine must enforce permissions outside the model.
- The communication layer must not independently grant action permissions.
- The Guardian must be operationally independent of the failures it is expected to detect.
- All state-changing operations must be attributable to a verified identity, authorized service or explicitly recorded system policy.
- Durable incident state must survive process restarts.
- Monitoring must use real configured sources and report unavailable sources honestly.

---

# 3. Required subsystem specifications

## 3.1 Event ingestion and monitoring

### Responsibilities

- Receive real events from authorized integrations.
- Validate source identity and event schema.
- Record source timestamps and server receipt timestamps.
- Validate event freshness and integrity where supported.
- Reject malformed or unauthorized events.
- Associate events with the relevant monitored resource.
- Prevent unauthorized sources from triggering privileged actions.
- Persist accepted events before acknowledging durable ingestion.

### Required event envelope

```text
event_id
source_id
source_event_id
event_type
observed_at
received_at
resource_reference
evidence_references
schema_version
integrity_metadata
correlation_key
```

`event_id` must be generated using a secure identifier-generation method. Source identifiers must refer to actual registered integrations. Timestamps must reflect actual observations and receipt times, not fabricated event times.

Additional attributes must be typed according to the event category. Arbitrary external payloads must not become executable instructions.

### Source registration

Each monitoring source must have:

- An authenticated identity.
- A documented purpose.
- Explicit resource scope.
- Read permissions and, separately, action permissions.
- Health and last-successful-observation status.
- Credential and token lifecycle management.
- Data retention requirements.
- A revocation mechanism.

Aariya must never report a monitored system as healthy merely because no failure event has arrived. Absence of an event is not proof of health.

## 3.2 Durable event processing

Implement a durable event-processing mechanism using infrastructure appropriate to the existing deployment.

Requirements:

- Persist events before acknowledging successful ingestion.
- Retry transient failures with bounded backoff.
- Support idempotent processing.
- Deduplicate repeated source events.
- Record processing attempts and failure reasons.
- Support dead-letter handling for events that cannot be processed.
- Recover pending events after restarts.
- Preserve ordering where the source or workflow requires it.
- Expose queue age, processing latency and failure metrics.

At-least-once event delivery with idempotent processing is an acceptable baseline. Exactly-once effects must not be assumed across independent services.

For actions that may be repeated, use idempotency keys and inspect actual action state before retrying.

## 3.3 Event correlation and evidence

Multiple events may describe the same incident.

The correlation subsystem must:

- Associate events with an existing incident when evidence supports the relationship.
- Preserve each original event and its source.
- Distinguish confirmed facts, inferences and unresolved contradictions.
- Track whether the incident is active, recovering, resolved or unresolved.
- Reopen an incident if credible new evidence shows the problem persists.
- Avoid merging unrelated events solely because they occurred close together.
- Preserve the provenance of every important conclusion.

If a database monitor reports recovery but another trusted source reports continuing failures, Aariya must investigate the discrepancy rather than automatically cancel the alert.

## 3.4 Importance and confidence engine

Each incident assessment must include:

```text
severity
urgency
confidence
potential_impact
affected_resources
required_response_time
reversibility
evidence_quality
source_reliability
user_preferences
recommended_channel
decision_reason
assessment_version
```

These fields must be represented by validated types and defined semantics.

Severity, urgency and confidence are distinct:

- **Severity:** magnitude of the potential or observed consequence.
- **Urgency:** how quickly attention is required.
- **Confidence:** strength of the evidence supporting the interpretation.

A high-impact event with uncertain evidence requires a different response from a confirmed immediate threat.

### Decision requirements

- Preserve uncertainty instead of manufacturing confidence.
- Use deterministic rules for mandatory safety and permission boundaries.
- Use model-based reasoning only within defined limits.
- Require evidence for claims that an incident occurred or was resolved.
- Support policy overrides and versioned decision rules.
- Record the assessment that produced each escalation decision.
- Reassess incidents when new evidence arrives.

A numerical confidence score must not be treated as calibrated probability unless it has been evaluated against appropriate real-world outcomes.

## 3.5 Policy and authorization engine

Implement a backend-enforced policy layer with four capability classes.

| Class | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Read | Access explicitly permitted data and perform authorized investigation. |
| Pre-authorized action | Execute within a defined scope, risk level and limit. |
| Restricted action | Require explicit authorization and any additional authentication required by policy. |
| Prohibited action | Block execution and explain the restriction. |

### Required controls

- Per-user and per-device identity.
- Resource-level access control.
- Per-tool and per-action permissions.
- Scope, expiration and revocation of authorizations.
- Separation between communication authority and execution authority.
- Approval records linked to the exact proposed action.
- Stronger authentication for sensitive operations.
- Revalidation of permissions immediately before execution.
- Prevention of duplicate or replayed approvals.
- Audit logging for denied and permitted actions.

An approval must be bound to the operation, target resource and material parameters it authorizes. A changed operation requires a new approval if the previous approval no longer covers it.

An LLM cannot grant itself permission by reasoning that an action is necessary.

---

# 4. Proactive communication specification

## 4.1 Communication Gateway

The Gateway is responsible for:

- Authenticated client sessions.
- Message routing and delivery state.
- Call signaling and session coordination.
- Reconnection and session recovery.
- Device and conversation association.
- Rate limits and abuse prevention.
- Authorized routing of incident evidence.
- Communication health and delivery telemetry.

It must not become a second conversational brain.

The existing Aariya Brain supplies the conversational content, interpretation and action reasoning.

## 4.2 Communication channels

Support the following channels as separately configured capabilities:

1. Persistent in-app messaging.
2. Push notifications.
3. Internet voice calls.
4. Alternative messaging or telephony providers, when configured and authorized.

The escalation policy determines which channels to attempt. A channel is available only when its configuration, credentials, connectivity and provider status support the requested operation.

Do not hardcode a provider's credentials, phone number, device token or account identifier.

## 4.3 Delivery state machine

Every delivery attempt must have an explicit state.

```text
PENDING
   |
   v
QUEUED
   |
   v
SENT_TO_PROVIDER
   |
   +----> PROVIDER_REJECTED
   |
   v
PROVIDER_ACCEPTED
   |
   v
DEVICE_DELIVERED (when confirmed)
   |
   v
USER_ACKNOWLEDGED
```

For voice calls, use separate call states:

```text
CALL_REQUESTED
   |
   v
SIGNALING_STARTED
   |
   +----> CONNECTION_FAILED
   |
   v
RINGING (when confirmed)
   |
   +----> MISSED / DECLINED
   |
   v
CONNECTED
   |
   v
CONVERSATION_ACTIVE
   |
   v
ENDED
```

Persist actual state transitions, timestamps and provider references. Do not infer a state transition from a request merely being issued.

Provider acceptance does not mean device delivery. Device delivery does not mean that the user read or understood the message. A connected call does not automatically mean the incident was acknowledged.

## 4.4 Escalation engine

The escalation engine must:

- Apply the configured event policy.
- Respect acknowledgement deadlines and interruption preferences where possible.
- Consolidate correlated incidents.
- Avoid repeated alerts for the same unresolved condition.
- Cancel obsolete pending alerts after verified resolution.
- Increase urgency when credible evidence warrants it.
- Stop retrying when policy limits are reached.
- Preserve failed delivery attempts.
- Record why each communication was selected.
- Report when no channel is available.

Repeated escalation must be bounded by explicit policy. It must not become an infinite call loop.

## 4.5 User interruption modes

Support:

- Available.
- Busy.
- Do Not Disturb.
- Sleeping.
- Driving.
- Custom.

Modes affect delivery preferences, not mandatory authorization or truthfulness.

The application should explain which critical alerts may bypass ordinary interruption preferences and allow you to configure these rules. Actual behavior is subject to operating-system permissions and capabilities.

## 4.6 Emergency delivery fallback

The policy may consider:

```text
Internet voice
      |
      v
Internet messaging
      |
      v
Push notification
      |
      v
Configured SMS or telephone provider
```

This is a candidate fallback order, not a universal fixed sequence.

The policy engine must consider severity, delivery evidence, channel availability, user preferences, cost and provider restrictions. It must not claim successful delivery without supporting evidence.

---

# 5. Voice, conversation and action pipeline

## 5.1 Session continuity

Every proactive communication must reference an incident and an authenticated conversation session.

Required context includes:

- Existing conversation identifier.
- Incident identifier.
- Triggering event and evidence.
- Importance assessment.
- Reason for escalation.
- Current incident status.
- Relevant authorized history.
- Pending questions or approvals.
- Previous execution results.

The same context must be available whether the user replies by voice, text or another authenticated client.

## 5.2 Existing voice pipeline integration

Reuse the existing voice architecture after inspecting the actual code.

The intended flow is:

```text
Incoming audio
      |
      v
Speech recognition
      |
      v
Conversation understanding
      |
      v
Incident and session context
      |
      v
Existing Aariya Brain
      |
      v
Reasoning and planning
      |
      v
Authorization check
      |
      v
Execution or clarification
      |
      v
Verification and reporting
      |
      v
Natural speech generation
```

Sentiment, conversational warmth and voice delivery remain contextual presentation capabilities. They must not override incident evidence or policy decisions.

## 5.3 Ambiguous commands

For a command such as "fix it," Aariya must:

1. Identify the relevant incident.
2. Inspect the available authorized evidence.
3. Determine which operations are permitted.
4. Establish whether the instruction identifies a sufficiently clear action.
5. Ask for clarification if materially different actions are possible.
6. Obtain additional authorization if required.
7. Execute the approved operation.
8. Verify the actual result.
9. Report success, failure or unresolved status accurately.

## 5.4 Evidence delivery

When the user requests "show me what happened," Aariya must send the relevant evidence to an authenticated active device session.

Evidence access must be:

- Linked to the incident.
- Authorized for that user and device.
- Protected during transport and storage.
- Audited where appropriate.
- Redacted according to data sensitivity.
- Restricted to the necessary information.

If evidence cannot be retrieved, Aariya must explain that limitation rather than invent a report.

---

# 6. Cryptographic and security specification

## 6.1 Trust boundary

The required architecture is:

```text
Authenticated Flutter Device
           |
           | Protected communication
           v
Aariya Communication Gateway
           |
           | Authenticated internal channel
           v
Existing Aariya AI Core
           |
           v
Authorized processing and tools
```

The actual cryptographic design must define whether the Gateway is trusted to access content.

If end-to-end confidentiality is required between the device and AI Core, the Gateway must not be able to decrypt the application content. If it can decrypt content, it belongs to the trusted processing boundary.

The AI Core must have access to usable plaintext at the point where it processes the conversation.

## 6.2 Required protections

- Use established, reviewed cryptographic protocols rather than custom cryptography.
- Authenticate clients and services.
- Protect signaling and media.
- Define the Gateway's ability or inability to decrypt content.
- Protect application messages, voice, files and relevant session metadata.
- Use secure key generation, storage, rotation and revocation.
- Bind messages to authenticated sessions.
- Protect against replay, tampering and session confusion.
- Encrypt sensitive stored data.
- Apply least-privilege service credentials.
- Restrict logging of sensitive payloads.
- Define provider data handling and retention.
- Provide a documented key-compromise recovery procedure.

Transport encryption alone must not be described as complete end-to-end encryption.

For real-time voice, choose a reviewed media architecture with authenticated signaling and a clearly defined media trust boundary. Any recording, transcription or external processing must follow explicit policy.

## 6.3 Device and user authentication

Voice recognition is not proof of identity.

Sensitive operations require appropriate authenticated session state and additional verification where policy requires it.

The system must support:

- Device registration and revocation.
- Session expiration and renewal.
- Authentication recovery.
- Protection against replayed voice commands.
- Explicit approval of sensitive actions.
- Audit records connecting approvals to executed actions.

## 6.4 Untrusted content

External events, logs, retrieved documents and web content are data, not trusted instructions.

The system must separate:

- User instructions.
- Trusted application policy.
- Authenticated source evidence.
- Untrusted source content.
- Model-generated interpretations.

Instructions found in external content must never bypass the policy engine or authorize tool execution.

---

# 7. Independent Guardian specification

## 7.1 Deployment and responsibility

The Guardian must run independently enough that failure of the main Brain does not prevent detection and reporting of that failure.

It should not rely on the main AI process to declare itself healthy.

The deployment must document which failure classes the Guardian can detect and which it cannot.

## 7.2 Monitoring responsibilities

Monitor:

- AI Core availability.
- Backend dependency health.
- Communication Gateway health.
- Critical database and queue availability.
- Task execution and recovery outcomes.
- Alert delivery and acknowledgement.
- Security and integrity signals.
- Guardian heartbeat and monitoring health.

Use authenticated health checks and actual telemetry. A process being alive is not proof that its services are functioning correctly.

## 7.3 Guardian limitations and independent alerting

The Guardian must have its own health checks, operational monitoring and restricted alert credentials.

It should use an independently available delivery path where possible. If it shares a failed dependency with the main system, that shared failure must be documented.

It must not be allowed to bypass action authorization or perform unrestricted repairs.

---

# 8. Incident memory and hybrid learning

## 8.1 Incident lifecycle

Use an explicit incident lifecycle, such as:

```text
OPEN
  |
  v
ASSESSING
  |
  +----> AWAITING_USER
  |
  v
ACTION_IN_PROGRESS
  |
  v
VERIFYING
  |
  +----> RECOVERY_REQUIRED
  |
  v
RESOLVED
```

Additional states must be defined for failed delivery, cancellation, rejection and reopened incidents.

The incident lifecycle must reflect actual persisted state, not just conversational statements.

## 8.2 Incident record

The record must capture:

- Incident and event references.
- Evidence provenance.
- Assessments and decision versions.
- Communication attempts and delivery states.
- User responses and approval records.
- Executed actions and their results.
- Resolution evidence.
- Learning proposals and their sources.
- Audit metadata.

Store evidence references and appropriate metadata rather than copying all sensitive data into every incident record.

## 8.3 Learning record

Every material learned preference must have:

```text
preference_id
scope
preference_type
supporting_incident_references
evidence_summary
confidence
status
created_at
last_evaluated_at
confirmation_reference
policy_version
rollback_reference
```

The status must distinguish observed, learned, confirmed, rejected and superseded preferences.

## 8.4 Learning safeguards

- Low-risk personalization may adapt automatically.
- Material escalation changes require confirmation.
- Security policy and capability permissions cannot be learned around.
- Conflicting observations must be preserved and evaluated.
- Outdated preferences must be reconsidered.
- Learning changes must be auditable and reversible.
- The system must not invent supporting incidents or user feedback.

---

# 9. Independent user control and privacy

Add a user-controlled safety and control center.

Required controls:

- Pause or resume proactive monitoring.
- Disable autonomous actions.
- Change alert and interruption policies.
- Review registered sources and their permissions.
- Revoke a device or integration.
- Review pending approvals.
- Inspect recent incidents and communication attempts.
- Review material preference changes.
- Roll back eligible learned preferences.
- Export or delete eligible stored information.
- Review retention rules and processing providers.

The control center must not depend exclusively on the AI Brain to enforce changes. Critical controls should be enforced by authenticated backend policy and, where appropriate, an independent administrative path.

Changes must take effect only after successful authorization and persistence.

---

# 10. Reliability and operational observability

## 10.1 Required infrastructure behavior

The deployed system must support:

- Durable queues.
- Idempotent processing.
- Bounded retries.
- Recovery after restarts.
- Database backups and verified restoration.
- Versioned configuration.
- Service health checks.
- Provider failure handling.
- Structured logs, metrics and traces.
- Incident replay based on recorded real events.
- Monitoring of monitoring and delivery infrastructure.

Use actual configuration from the deployment environment. Missing configuration must produce an explicit unhealthy or unavailable status rather than silently activating a fake fallback.

## 10.2 Required operational metrics

Collect actual measurements for:

- Event ingestion delay.
- Event processing latency.
- Queue age and backlog.
- Incident assessment latency.
- Time to initiate escalation.
- Provider acceptance and confirmed delivery rates.
- Call connection and failure rates.
- Unacknowledged incident age.
- Action execution and verification failures.
- Guardian detection and notification latency.
- Unauthorized-action rejection.
- Recovery and restoration outcomes.

Do not publish invented performance numbers as system measurements.

Service-level objectives must be selected after examining the real deployment, its providers and its operating constraints.

---

# 11. API and data contract requirements

The following are logical interface contracts, not claims that these endpoints already exist. Exact routes and schemas must be aligned with the actual FastAPI application.

## 11.1 Event ingestion

**Purpose:** Accept authenticated events from registered sources.

Contract:

- Validate source identity and schema.
- Reject unauthorized events.
- Persist accepted events.
- Return an event reference and actual processing status.
- Prevent duplicate processing using source identity and idempotency information.

## 11.2 Incident retrieval

**Purpose:** Retrieve incident details and authorized evidence.

Contract:

- Require authentication.
- Enforce incident-level access control.
- Return current persisted status.
- Include provenance and timestamps.
- Exclude unauthorized evidence.

## 11.3 Communication session

**Purpose:** Establish and manage authenticated message or voice sessions.

Contract:

- Authenticate the device.
- Validate session and incident association.
- Protect signaling and content.
- Maintain explicit connection states.
- Record genuine delivery and call-state transitions.

## 11.4 User response and approval

**Purpose:** Associate a user's response with an incident or proposed action.

Contract:

- Authenticate the actor.
- Verify the session and incident.
- Validate the requested operation.
- Bind approval to the exact action and its parameters.
- Check authorization again before execution.
- Record the decision and result.

## 11.5 Guardian health

**Purpose:** Report actual subsystem health and alert-delivery status.

Contract:

- Use authenticated service identity.
- Report the monitored component and observation time.
- Distinguish healthy, degraded, unavailable and unknown.
- Avoid treating stale telemetry as current health.
- Keep sensitive internal diagnostics out of public endpoints.

---

# 12. Logical data model

Implement these as database entities or equivalent persisted records after inspecting the existing schema.

| Entity | Purpose |
|---|---|
| RegisteredSource | Source identity, scope, health and permissions |
| ObservedEvent | Actual event data and provenance |
| Incident | Aggregated event, assessment and lifecycle |
| EvidenceReference | Authorized reference to diagnostic evidence |
| Assessment | Severity, urgency, confidence and decision reason |
| PolicyVersion | Versioned escalation and action rules |
| AuthorizationGrant | Scoped and revocable permission |
| Approval | Authenticated decision bound to a proposed action |
| CommunicationAttempt | Message or call attempt and delivery state |
| ConversationSession | Device, user, incident and context association |
| ActionExecution | Authorized operation and execution status |
| VerificationResult | Evidence of actual outcome |
| GuardianObservation | Health measurements and detection state |
| LearningRecord | Preference observations and confirmed changes |
| AuditRecord | Security-sensitive decisions and state changes |

### Data integrity requirements

- Use durable identifiers and database constraints.
- Enforce uniqueness for applicable source event IDs and idempotency keys.
- Use transactions where related state changes must commit together.
- Record state transitions with actor, timestamp and reason.
- Define retention, deletion and access rules for each entity.
- Avoid storing secrets or unnecessary sensitive payloads in audit records.
- Protect concurrent approvals and action execution against race conditions.

---

# 13. No-fabricated-data implementation policy

This is a mandatory project constraint.

## 13.1 Production runtime

The application must never invent operational records to fill missing information.

Prohibited examples:

- Fabricating an incident when no source reported one.
- Reporting a successful call when no call was connected.
- Inventing user preferences or previous conversations.
- Returning fabricated health values when monitoring is unavailable.
- Creating pretend action results to satisfy an API response.
- Falling back to hardcoded credentials, identities or resource IDs.
- Treating example incident payloads as production data.

If the source is unavailable, return an explicit unknown or unavailable state.

If evidence is missing, preserve the uncertainty.

If execution cannot be verified, report that it could not be verified.

## 13.2 Configuration

Runtime behavior must derive from validated configuration and authorized records.

Use configuration management for:

- Provider endpoints and credentials.
- Database and queue connections.
- Encryption and key-management integrations.
- Notification and calling providers.
- Monitoring thresholds.
- Escalation policies.
- Authorization rules.
- Retention and privacy policies.

Do not embed environment-specific secrets or operational identities in source code.

## 13.3 Testing

Tests must not create fake operational history and then present it as genuine user data.

Use:

- Unit tests for deterministic logic.
- Cryptographic protocol test vectors from authoritative specifications.
- Sanitized real incident traces when testing realistic workflows.
- Controlled integration tests against actual isolated infrastructure.
- Failure injection to verify recovery behavior.
- Security tests for unauthorized access and invalid transitions.

Test-only inputs must be clearly isolated from production data and production credentials. No test-generated incident or execution result may leak into the live user's history.

---

# 14. Implementation phases and acceptance criteria

## Phase 1 — Existing-codebase audit

Inspect the actual FastAPI application, Flutter application, voice pipeline, memory, execution tools, database, Redis usage, authentication and deployment.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Actual modules and interfaces are documented.
- Reusable components are identified.
- Existing data models and authentication are understood.
- Missing capabilities are mapped to exact implementation locations.
- No replacement architecture is introduced without a demonstrated need.

## Phase 2 — Durable event and incident infrastructure

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Real authorized sources can submit validated events.
- Events survive process restarts.
- Duplicate delivery does not duplicate the intended effect.
- Incident states are persisted and recoverable.
- Evidence and event provenance remain available.

## Phase 3 — Importance, policy and authorization

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Assessments use real event evidence.
- Missing evidence remains unknown.
- Permissions are enforced server-side.
- Unauthorized and restricted actions are blocked.
- Each decision has a recorded reason and policy version.

## Phase 4 — Flutter messaging and incident interface

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The existing app receives and displays actual incident messages.
- Delivery and acknowledgement states reflect real observations.
- The user can inspect authorized evidence.
- Incident context survives reconnects and app restarts.
- No fabricated messages appear in the production conversation.

## Phase 5 — Secure real-time voice

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Authenticated clients can establish supported voice sessions.
- Media and signaling security match the documented trust model.
- Call states reflect actual connection events.
- The existing Aariya voice pipeline handles conversation.
- Unsupported background-call behavior is reported and handled honestly.

## Phase 6 — Guardian and escalation

**Acceptance criteria:**

- The Guardian detects tested failures independently of the affected component.
- Alerts use a functioning authorized delivery path.
- Failed and successful delivery states are recorded accurately.
- Retries are bounded and incidents are deduplicated.
- Guardian failure and shared dependencies are monitored.

## Phase 7 — Conversational action execution

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Voice and text instructions enter the existing reasoning pipeline.
- Actions pass explicit authorization checks.
- Ambiguous instructions result in clarification.
- Execution results are verified.
- Failed or unverified actions are reported accurately.

## Phase 8 — Incident memory and learning

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Incident history is persisted with provenance.
- Observed, learned and confirmed preferences are distinguishable.
- Material changes require confirmation.
- Learning cannot override safety policy.
- Eligible learning changes can be reviewed and rolled back.

## Phase 9 — Multi-device and broader sources

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Authenticated devices share consistent incident state.
- Concurrent responses do not cause duplicate execution.
- New monitoring sources require explicit registration and permissions.
- Access is revoked when a device or integration is removed.

## Phase 10 — Security and recovery validation

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Authentication and authorization boundaries are tested.
- Cryptographic behavior matches the documented design.
- Unauthorized content and actions are rejected.
- Backup restoration and restart recovery are verified.
- Offline and provider-failure behavior is tested.
- Alert delivery and Guardian limitations are documented.
- No fabricated operational data is introduced by failure-handling code.

---

# 15. Definition of done

The system is not complete merely because the code compiles or the Flutter interface displays an incoming-call screen.

A capability is complete only when:

1. It uses the actual configured service or integration.
2. It processes real authorized input.
3. It enforces access and action permissions.
4. It persists the required state.
5. It handles relevant failures.
6. It exposes genuine execution and delivery status.
7. It has appropriate automated tests.
8. It has been tested against its acceptance criteria.
9. It does not fabricate operational success.
10. Its limitations are documented.

---

# 16. Immediate next step

Before implementing these modules, inspect the existing Aariya repository and produce a codebase-specific implementation map.

For each requirement, record:

- Existing module, if any.
- Existing interface and data model.
- Reuse, extension or replacement decision.
- Exact files that need changes.
- New files that are genuinely necessary.
- Database migration requirements.
- Configuration and secret-management requirements.
- Tests and acceptance criteria.
- External integrations that need credentials or setup.

Only then should implementation begin.

**Final architectural principle:** Aariya remains one persistent cognitive system, with one consistent authorization model and shared conversational context. Proactive monitoring, messaging, voice, execution, Guardian monitoring and learning are coordinated capabilities around that existing Brain—not independent assistants with conflicting memories or permissions.

Aariya Proactive Event Foundation — integration overlay
This is an additive backend slice for the existing Aariya FastAPI application. It contains no demo events, fake provider, canned notification, default credentials, fabricated confidence, or synthetic runtime observations.
Included real behavior
- Authenticated event ingestion. source_id is derived from a configured source token, never trusted from the request body.
- Durable persistence to an explicitly configured SQLite database file.
- Per-source event-type allow-lists, so a task monitor cannot claim a security event unless that exact capability was explicitly granted.
- Event idempotency using (authenticated source ID, source_event_id).
- Incident creation and same-source correlation only when the real source supplies a correlation_key.
- Event history and evidence references retained with the incident.
- Authenticated list/detail endpoints plus audited acknowledge/resolve transitions.
- Strict input validation, bounded payload size, unique source tokens, UTC normalization for actual source timestamps, and failure-closed configuration.
Not yet included
This slice does not claim to deliver push messages or VoIP calls. It does not contain the existing Flutter source, authenticated mobile-session code, the current Aariya user identity system, the real event producers/watchers, the existing database abstraction, or provider credentials. Those must be connected to actual project code before dispatch, Guardian, or mobile call work can be safely integrated. It also does not execute corrective actions or estimate importance from an LLM.
Add to the actual project
Copy server/proactive/ into the existing repository's server/proactive/ directory. Preserve the repository's current files.

In the existing server/main.py, after the FastAPI app is created and before the module finishes loading, include this router:
from server.proactive.router import router as proactive_router
app.include_router(proactive_router)
Do not create a second FastAPI app. Apply the import/include only after checking the existing app factory/lifespan and import conventions. The source tree was not available when this overlay was prepared, so server/main.py is deliberately not overwritten by a guessed version.
Required configuration
Configure these variables using the project's real secret manager or local protected environment file. Do not commit their values.
- AARIYA_PROACTIVE_DB_PATH: absolute path for the persistent event database file.
- AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_TOKEN: a random token of at least 32 characters for the single configured user identity used by this initial slice.
- AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_ID: the real user/account identifier to be written in the audit trail.
- AARIYA_PROACTIVE_SOURCE_TOKENS_JSON: JSON object mapping each actual source ID to an object with exactly two properties: token (a unique random secret of at least 32 characters) and event_types (a non-empty explicit allow-list using the supported event-type names). Only configure sources that really exist and are controlled by Aariya. A source's event-type permission is denied by default unless configured here.
Generate secrets with the system cryptographic random generator, e.g. python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(48))". Generate a separate token for the user and for each source. Do not reuse authentication tokens from other services.

The application returns 503 for this feature while required configuration is missing; it does not fall back to open access or sample credentials.
Endpoints
All paths below are relative to /api/proactive/v1.
- POST /events — source-token authenticated. source_event_id, event_type, severity, title, and summary must come from a real, authorized source, and the source must be explicitly allow-listed for the submitted event type. Supported event types are system_failure, task_failure, critical_event, deadline, security_event, autonomous_discovery, recovery, and task_completed. occurred_at, confidence, correlation_key, evidence_refs, and attributes are optional source-supplied facts; the service does not invent them.
- GET /incidents?state=open&limit=50 — user-token authenticated; state is optional.
- GET /incidents/{incident_id} — includes persisted events and audit history.
- POST /incidents/{incident_id}/acknowledge — audited transition from open to acknowledged.
- POST /incidents/{incident_id}/resolve — requires a non-empty, user-supplied resolution reason; audited.
Send Authorization: Bearer <configured token>. Do not include the bearer token in a URL, event body, evidence reference, or log. Do not place passwords, API keys, session cookies, private keys, or full conversation transcripts in summary or attributes; send minimum diagnostic facts and opaque references to evidence stored under the existing access-control system.
Storage and deployment boundary
SQLite is appropriate here only as a first durable local/single-host slice. Its file must live in a protected application-data location with filesystem permissions and encrypted disk/storage appropriate to the device. This implementation intentionally does not claim multi-instance consistency, cloud replication, mobile push, end-to-end encryption, or Guardian independence. Before multiple backend instances are deployed, move the schema and transaction logic to Aariya's real shared database/migration system instead of sharing a SQLite file across hosts.
Next implementation step
Once the actual backend and Flutter source are available, wire existing system/task monitors into POST /events, replace the initial single-user token guard with Aariya's current session/authorization dependency, connect an outbox to configured real push/VoIP providers, and add the Flutter incident thread and evidence routes. Do not enable escalation until those provider delivery acknowledgements and policy checks are implemented.

"""Aariya proactive-event foundation.

This package only accepts authenticated observations and persists them. It does
not manufacture monitoring events, estimate confidence, execute actions, or
claim that a notification or call was delivered.
"""

from .router import router

__all__ = ["router"]



"""Environment-backed configuration for the proactive-event API.

No credentials or operational data have defaults. Missing configuration fails
closed rather than silently enabling unauthenticated event ingestion.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Mapping

from .schemas import EventType


class ConfigurationError(RuntimeError):
    """Required proactive-system configuration is absent or invalid."""


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class SourceAuthorization:
    token: str
    allowed_event_types: frozenset[EventType]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Settings:
    database_path: Path
    user_token: str
    user_id: str
    source_tokens: Mapping[str, SourceAuthorization]

    @classmethod
    def from_environment(cls) -> "Settings":
        raw_db_path = os.environ.get("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_DB_PATH", "").strip()
        raw_user_token = os.environ.get("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_TOKEN", "")
        user_id = os.environ.get("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_ID", "").strip()
        raw_sources = os.environ.get("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_SOURCE_TOKENS_JSON", "").strip()

        if not raw_db_path:
            raise ConfigurationError("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_DB_PATH is required")
        database_path = Path(raw_db_path).expanduser()
        if not database_path.is_absolute():
            raise ConfigurationError("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_DB_PATH must be an absolute path")
        if str(database_path) == ":memory:":
            raise ConfigurationError("An in-memory database is not permitted for durable incident storage")

        if len(raw_user_token) < 32:
            raise ConfigurationError("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_TOKEN must be a generated secret of at least 32 characters")
        if not user_id:
            raise ConfigurationError("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_ID is required")
        if len(user_id) > 160:
            raise ConfigurationError("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_ID must not exceed 160 characters")
        if not raw_sources:
            raise ConfigurationError("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_SOURCE_TOKENS_JSON is required")

        try:
            source_tokens = json.loads(raw_sources)
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            raise ConfigurationError("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_SOURCE_TOKENS_JSON must be valid JSON") from exc

        if not isinstance(source_tokens, dict) or not source_tokens:
            raise ConfigurationError("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_SOURCE_TOKENS_JSON must be a non-empty JSON object")
        validated_sources: dict[str, str] = {}
        seen_tokens: set[str] = set()
        allowed_keys = {"token", "event_types"}
        for source_id, entry in source_tokens.items():
            if not isinstance(source_id, str) or not source_id.strip() or len(source_id) > 160:
                raise ConfigurationError("Every configured source ID must be a non-empty string of at most 160 characters")
            if not isinstance(entry, dict) or set(entry) != allowed_keys:
                raise ConfigurationError("Each source must define only 'token' and 'event_types'")
            token = entry["token"]
            raw_event_types = entry["event_types"]
            if not isinstance(token, str) or len(token) < 32:
                raise ConfigurationError("Every configured source token must be a generated secret of at least 32 characters")
            if token == raw_user_token or token in seen_tokens:
                raise ConfigurationError("Source tokens must be unique and must not equal the user token")
            if not isinstance(raw_event_types, list) or not raw_event_types:
                raise ConfigurationError("Each source must have a non-empty explicit event_types allow-list")
            try:
                event_types = frozenset(EventType(item) for item in raw_event_types)
            except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
                raise ConfigurationError("A source event_types allow-list contains an unsupported event type") from exc
            if len(event_types) != len(raw_event_types):
                raise ConfigurationError("A source event_types allow-list must not contain duplicates")
            validated_sources[source_id] = SourceAuthorization(token=token, allowed_event_types=event_types)
            seen_tokens.add(token)

        return cls(
            database_path=database_path,
            user_token=raw_user_token,
            user_id=user_id,
            source_tokens=validated_sources,
        )


"""FastAPI routes for authenticated, durable proactive events and incidents."""

from __future__ import annotations

import hmac
import sqlite3
from functools import lru_cache
from typing import Annotated

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, Header, HTTPException, Query, status

from .config import ConfigurationError, Settings, SourceAuthorization
from .schemas import EventIn, EventIngestOut, IncidentOut, IncidentState, ResolveIn
from .store import DuplicateEventConflict, EventStore, IncidentConflict, IncidentNotFound

router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/proactive/v1", tags=["Aariya proactive communication"])


@lru_cache(maxsize=8)
def _store_for_path(path: str) -> EventStore:
    from pathlib import Path

    return EventStore(Path(path))


def _settings() -> Settings:
    try:
        return Settings.from_environment()
    except ConfigurationError as exc:
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
            detail="Proactive event service is not configured",
        ) from exc


def _bearer(authorization: str | None) -> str:
    if not authorization:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, detail="Bearer authentication required")
    scheme, separator, token = authorization.partition(" ")
    if not separator or scheme.lower() != "bearer" or not token:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, detail="Invalid authorization header")
    return token


def authenticated_source(authorization: Annotated[str | None, Header()] = None) -> tuple[str, SourceAuthorization]:
    settings = _settings()
    token = _bearer(authorization)
    for source_id, authorization_rule in settings.source_tokens.items():
        if hmac.compare_digest(token, authorization_rule.token):
            return source_id, authorization_rule
    raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, detail="Source authentication failed")


def authenticated_user(authorization: Annotated[str | None, Header()] = None) -> tuple[Settings, str]:
    settings = _settings()
    token = _bearer(authorization)
    if not hmac.compare_digest(token, settings.user_token):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, detail="User authentication failed")
    return settings, settings.user_id


def _store(settings: Settings) -> EventStore:
    try:
        return _store_for_path(str(settings.database_path))
    except (OSError, RuntimeError, ValueError, sqlite3.Error) as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, detail="Proactive storage is unavailable") from exc


@router.post("/events", response_model=EventIngestOut, status_code=status.HTTP_202_ACCEPTED)
def ingest_event(
    event: EventIn,
    source_auth: Annotated[tuple[str, SourceAuthorization], Depends(authenticated_source)],
    settings: Annotated[Settings, Depends(_settings)],
) -> EventIngestOut:
    source_id, authorization_rule = source_auth
    if event.event_type not in authorization_rule.allowed_event_types:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail="This source is not authorized to emit that event type")
    store = _store(settings)
    try:
        result, duplicate = store.ingest(source_id, event)
    except DuplicateEventConflict as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail=str(exc)) from exc
    except (OSError, RuntimeError, sqlite3.Error) as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, detail="Event could not be persisted") from exc
    return EventIngestOut(**result, duplicate=duplicate)


@router.get("/incidents", response_model=list[IncidentOut])
def list_incidents(
    auth: Annotated[tuple[Settings, str], Depends(authenticated_user)],
    state: IncidentState | None = Query(default=None),
    limit: int = Query(default=50, ge=1, le=100),
) -> list[IncidentOut]:
    settings, _actor_id = auth
    try:
        records = _store(settings).list_incidents(state, limit)
    except (OSError, RuntimeError, sqlite3.Error) as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, detail="Incident storage is unavailable") from exc
    return [IncidentOut(**record) for record in records]


@router.get("/incidents/{incident_id}", response_model=IncidentOut)
def get_incident(
    incident_id: str,
    auth: Annotated[tuple[Settings, str], Depends(authenticated_user)],
) -> IncidentOut:
    settings, _actor_id = auth
    try:
        record = _store(settings).get_incident(incident_id)
    except IncidentNotFound as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="Incident not found") from exc
    except (OSError, RuntimeError, sqlite3.Error) as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, detail="Incident storage is unavailable") from exc
    return IncidentOut(**record)


@router.post("/incidents/{incident_id}/acknowledge", response_model=IncidentOut)
def acknowledge_incident(
    incident_id: str,
    auth: Annotated[tuple[Settings, str], Depends(authenticated_user)],
) -> IncidentOut:
    settings, actor_id = auth
    store = _store(settings)
    try:
        store.transition(incident_id, IncidentState.ACKNOWLEDGED, actor_id)
        record = store.get_incident(incident_id)
    except IncidentNotFound as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="Incident not found") from exc
    except IncidentConflict as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail=str(exc)) from exc
    except (OSError, RuntimeError, sqlite3.Error) as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, detail="Incident state could not be persisted") from exc
    return IncidentOut(**record)


@router.post("/incidents/{incident_id}/resolve", response_model=IncidentOut)
def resolve_incident(
    incident_id: str,
    body: ResolveIn,
    auth: Annotated[tuple[Settings, str], Depends(authenticated_user)],
) -> IncidentOut:
    settings, actor_id = auth
    store = _store(settings)
    try:
        store.transition(incident_id, IncidentState.RESOLVED, actor_id, body.reason)
        record = store.get_incident(incident_id)
    except IncidentNotFound as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="Incident not found") from exc
    except IncidentConflict as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail=str(exc)) from exc
    except (OSError, RuntimeError, sqlite3.Error) as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, detail="Incident state could not be persisted") from exc
    return IncidentOut(**record)


"""Validated API contracts for real source observations and incident actions."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from datetime import datetime
from enum import StrEnum
from typing import Any

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, field_validator, model_validator


class EventType(StrEnum):
    SYSTEM_FAILURE = "system_failure"
    TASK_FAILURE = "task_failure"
    CRITICAL_EVENT = "critical_event"
    DEADLINE = "deadline"
    SECURITY_EVENT = "security_event"
    AUTONOMOUS_DISCOVERY = "autonomous_discovery"
    RECOVERY = "recovery"
    TASK_COMPLETED = "task_completed"


class Severity(StrEnum):
    INFO = "info"
    WARNING = "warning"
    HIGH = "high"
    CRITICAL = "critical"


class IncidentState(StrEnum):
    OPEN = "open"
    ACKNOWLEDGED = "acknowledged"
    RESOLVED = "resolved"


class EventIn(BaseModel):
    """A source-reported observation. Values are supplied by the real source."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", str_strip_whitespace=True)

    source_event_id: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=200)
    event_type: EventType
    severity: Severity
    title: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=200)
    summary: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=4_000)
    occurred_at: datetime | None = None
    correlation_key: str | None = Field(default=None, min_length=1, max_length=200)
    confidence: float | None = Field(default=None, ge=0.0, le=1.0)
    evidence_refs: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list, max_length=50)
    attributes: dict[str, Any] = Field(default_factory=dict)

    @field_validator("occurred_at")
    @classmethod
    def require_timezone_for_source_time(cls, value: datetime | None) -> datetime | None:
        if value is not None and (value.tzinfo is None or value.utcoffset() is None):
            raise ValueError("occurred_at must include a timezone offset")
        return value

    @field_validator("evidence_refs")
    @classmethod
    def validate_evidence_refs(cls, values: list[str]) -> list[str]:
        for item in values:
            if not item.strip() or len(item) > 512:
                raise ValueError("each evidence reference must be non-empty and at most 512 characters")
        if len(values) != len(set(values)):
            raise ValueError("evidence_refs must not contain duplicates")
        return values

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def validate_serializable_size(self) -> "EventIn":
        try:
            encoded = json.dumps(self.attributes, ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False, separators=(",", ":"))
        except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
            raise ValueError("attributes must contain JSON-compatible values only") from exc
        if len(encoded.encode("utf-8")) > 16_384:
            raise ValueError("attributes exceeds the 16 KiB limit")
        return self


class ResolveIn(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", str_strip_whitespace=True)
    reason: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=2_000)


class EventOut(BaseModel):
    event_id: str
    source_id: str
    source_event_id: str
    event_type: EventType
    severity: Severity
    title: str
    summary: str
    occurred_at: datetime | None
    received_at: datetime
    correlation_key: str | None
    confidence: float | None
    evidence_refs: list[str]
    attributes: dict[str, Any]


class AuditOut(BaseModel):
    audit_id: str
    actor_type: str
    actor_id: str
    action: str
    metadata: dict[str, Any]
    occurred_at: datetime


class IncidentOut(BaseModel):
    incident_id: str
    state: IncidentState
    severity: Severity
    title: str
    summary: str
    source_id: str
    correlation_key: str | None
    created_at: datetime
    updated_at: datetime
    acknowledged_at: datetime | None
    resolved_at: datetime | None
    resolution_reason: str | None
    events: list[EventOut] = Field(default_factory=list)
    audit: list[AuditOut] = Field(default_factory=list)


class EventIngestOut(BaseModel):
    event_id: str
    incident_id: str
    duplicate: bool
    incident_state: IncidentState

"""Durable SQLite event and incident store for a single-host Aariya runtime.

Use the project's shared production database instead when deploying multiple
backend instances. The database path is always explicitly configured.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import sqlite3
import uuid
from contextlib import closing
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from .schemas import EventIn, EventType, IncidentState, Severity

_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1
_SEVERITY_RANK = {
    Severity.INFO.value: 0,
    Severity.WARNING.value: 1,
    Severity.HIGH.value: 2,
    Severity.CRITICAL.value: 3,
}


class IncidentNotFound(LookupError):
    pass


class IncidentConflict(RuntimeError):
    pass


class DuplicateEventConflict(RuntimeError):
    """A source reused an event ID for different content."""


def utc_now() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="microseconds")


def _json(value: Any) -> str:
    return json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False, separators=(",", ":"))


def _parse_json(value: str) -> Any:
    return json.loads(value)


def _event_fingerprint(event: EventIn) -> str:
    payload = json.dumps(
        event.model_dump(mode="json"),
        ensure_ascii=False,
        allow_nan=False,
        separators=(",", ":"),
        sort_keys=True,
    ).encode("utf-8")
    return hashlib.sha256(payload).hexdigest()


class EventStore:
    """Persist events, incidents, and audit facts in a configured database."""

    def __init__(self, database_path: Path):
        if not database_path.is_absolute():
            raise ValueError("database_path must be absolute")
        self.database_path = database_path
        self.database_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self._initialize()

    def _connect(self) -> sqlite3.Connection:
        connection = sqlite3.connect(self.database_path, timeout=5.0, isolation_level=None)
        connection.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        connection.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
        connection.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000")
        connection.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL")
        return connection

    def _initialize(self) -> None:
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            db.executescript(
                """
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS proactive_schema_version (
                    version INTEGER NOT NULL
                );

                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS proactive_incidents (
                    incident_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
                    source_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    correlation_key TEXT,
                    state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('open', 'acknowledged', 'resolved')),
                    severity TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(severity IN ('info', 'warning', 'high', 'critical')),
                    title TEXT NOT NULL,
                    summary TEXT NOT NULL,
                    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    updated_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    acknowledged_at TEXT,
                    resolved_at TEXT,
                    resolution_reason TEXT,
                    first_event_id TEXT,
                    last_event_id TEXT
                );

                CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_proactive_incidents_state_updated
                    ON proactive_incidents(state, updated_at DESC, incident_id DESC);

                CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_proactive_active_source_correlation
                    ON proactive_incidents(source_id, correlation_key)
                    WHERE correlation_key IS NOT NULL AND state != 'resolved';

                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS proactive_events (
                    event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
                    incident_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES proactive_incidents(incident_id),
                    source_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    source_event_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    payload_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
                    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
                    severity TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(severity IN ('info', 'warning', 'high', 'critical')),
                    title TEXT NOT NULL,
                    summary TEXT NOT NULL,
                    occurred_at TEXT,
                    received_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    correlation_key TEXT,
                    confidence REAL CHECK(confidence IS NULL OR (confidence >= 0 AND confidence <= 1)),
                    evidence_refs_json TEXT NOT NULL,
                    attributes_json TEXT NOT NULL,
                    UNIQUE(source_id, source_event_id)
                );

                CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_proactive_events_incident_received
                    ON proactive_events(incident_id, received_at ASC);

                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS proactive_audit (
                    audit_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
                    incident_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES proactive_incidents(incident_id),
                    actor_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(actor_type IN ('source', 'user')),
                    actor_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    action TEXT NOT NULL,
                    metadata_json TEXT NOT NULL,
                    occurred_at TEXT NOT NULL
                );

                CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_proactive_audit_incident_time
                    ON proactive_audit(incident_id, occurred_at ASC);
                """
            )
            versions = db.execute("SELECT version FROM proactive_schema_version").fetchall()
            if not versions:
                db.execute("INSERT INTO proactive_schema_version(version) VALUES (?)", (_SCHEMA_VERSION,))
            elif len(versions) != 1 or versions[0]["version"] > _SCHEMA_VERSION:
                raise RuntimeError("The proactive database schema is newer or invalid; refusing to downgrade")
            elif versions[0]["version"] != _SCHEMA_VERSION:
                raise RuntimeError("A proactive database migration is required before this version can run")

    def ingest(self, source_id: str, event: EventIn) -> tuple[dict[str, Any], bool]:
        """Atomically persist a source event and its incident association.

        Idempotency is based on the authenticated source identity plus the source's
        own event ID; no generated/fabricated observation is introduced.
        """
        db = self._connect()
        payload_hash = _event_fingerprint(event)
        try:
            db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
            existing_event = db.execute(
                "SELECT event_id, incident_id, payload_hash FROM proactive_events WHERE source_id = ? AND source_event_id = ?",
                (source_id, event.source_event_id),
            ).fetchone()
            if existing_event:
                if existing_event["payload_hash"] != payload_hash:
                    raise DuplicateEventConflict("A source event ID was reused with different content")
                incident = db.execute(
                    "SELECT * FROM proactive_incidents WHERE incident_id = ?",
                    (existing_event["incident_id"],),
                ).fetchone()
                db.execute("COMMIT")
                return {
                    "event_id": existing_event["event_id"],
                    "incident_id": existing_event["incident_id"],
                    "incident_state": incident["state"],
                }, True

            incident = None
            if event.correlation_key is not None:
                incident = db.execute(
                    """SELECT * FROM proactive_incidents
                       WHERE source_id = ? AND correlation_key = ? AND state != 'resolved'
                       ORDER BY created_at ASC LIMIT 1""",
                    (source_id, event.correlation_key),
                ).fetchone()

            now = utc_now()
            if incident is None:
                incident_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
                db.execute(
                    """INSERT INTO proactive_incidents
                       (incident_id, source_id, correlation_key, state, severity, title, summary,
                        created_at, updated_at)
                       VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'open', ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)""",
                    (incident_id, source_id, event.correlation_key, event.severity.value, event.title, event.summary, now, now),
                )
                current_state = IncidentState.OPEN.value
                current_severity = event.severity.value
