                    or int(attempt["attempt_number"]) != int(job["attempts"])):
                raise OutboxConflict("Only the current in-flight attempt can be cancelled")
            db.execute(
                "UPDATE proactive_notification_outbox SET state = 'cancelled', lease_until = NULL, last_error_code = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE job_id = ?",
                (reason_code, now, job_id),
            )
            db.execute(
                "UPDATE proactive_notification_attempts SET state = 'cancelled', finished_at = ?, error_code = ? WHERE attempt_id = ?",
                (now, reason_code, attempt_id),
            )
            row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
            db.execute("COMMIT")
            return _decode(row)
        except Exception:
            if db.in_transaction:
                db.execute("ROLLBACK")
            raise
        finally:
            db.close()

    def get(self, job_id: str) -> NotificationJob:
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
        if row is None:
            raise OutboxNotFound(job_id)
        return _decode(row)

    def list_jobs(self, *, states: Iterable[str] | None = None, limit: int = 100) -> list[NotificationJob]:
        if not 1 <= limit <= 500:
            raise OutboxValidationError("limit must be between 1 and 500")
        state_list = tuple(dict.fromkeys(states or ()))
        valid_states = {"pending", "in_flight", "delivered", "dead", "cancelled"}
        if any(state not in valid_states for state in state_list):
            raise OutboxValidationError("states contains an unsupported state")
        query = "SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox"
        args: tuple[Any, ...] = ()
        if state_list:
            placeholders = ",".join("?" for _ in state_list)
            query += f" WHERE state IN ({placeholders})"
            args = state_list
        query += " ORDER BY created_at DESC, job_id DESC LIMIT ?"
        args += (limit,)
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            rows = db.execute(query, args).fetchall()
        return [_decode(row) for row in rows]


"""Provider-agnostic dispatcher for durable proactive notifications.

No provider credentials or provider implementation is included here. Integrate a
real push/VoIP/in-app provider that implements NotificationProvider, register only
providers whose credentials and destination configuration have been verified, and
supply an authoritative incident-actionability check before delivery.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Callable, Mapping, Protocol

from .outbox import ClaimedNotification, NotificationJob, NotificationOutbox


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class DeliveryReceipt:
    """Receipt returned by a real provider after it accepts a delivery request."""

    provider_message_id: str | None


class NotificationProvider(Protocol):
    """Adapter contract for a real provider such as push, in-app, or VoIP."""

    def deliver(self, job: NotificationJob, *, idempotency_key: str) -> DeliveryReceipt:
        """Send through the configured provider; never fabricate a receipt."""
        ...


class DispatchConfigurationError(RuntimeError):
    """Required dispatcher policy configuration is missing or invalid."""


class ProviderDeliveryError(RuntimeError):
    """A provider failure expressed as a safe code, never raw credential text."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        error_code: str,
        *,
        retryable: bool,
        retry_after_seconds: int | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        if not error_code.strip() or len(error_code) > 120:
            raise ValueError("error_code must be a non-empty safe code of at most 120 characters")
        if retry_after_seconds is not None and not 0 <= retry_after_seconds <= 31_536_000:
            raise ValueError("retry_after_seconds must be between 0 and one year")
        super().__init__(error_code)
        self.error_code = error_code
        self.retryable = retryable
        self.retry_after_seconds = retry_after_seconds


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class DispatchPolicy:
    batch_size: int
    lease_seconds: int
    base_retry_delay_seconds: int
    max_retry_delay_seconds: int

    @classmethod
    def from_environment(cls) -> "DispatchPolicy":
        """Load explicit retry/lease policy. Missing values never get defaults."""
        names = {
            "batch_size": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_BATCH_SIZE",
            "lease_seconds": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_LEASE_SECONDS",
            "base_retry_delay_seconds": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_BASE_SECONDS",
            "max_retry_delay_seconds": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_MAX_SECONDS",
        }
        parsed: dict[str, int] = {}
        for field, env_name in names.items():
            raw = os.environ.get(env_name, "").strip()
            if not raw:
                raise DispatchConfigurationError(f"{env_name} is required")
            try:
                parsed[field] = int(raw)
            except ValueError as exc:
                raise DispatchConfigurationError(f"{env_name} must be an integer") from exc
        try:
            return cls(**parsed)
        except ValueError as exc:
            raise DispatchConfigurationError("Notification dispatcher policy is outside its allowed bounds") from exc

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not 1 <= self.batch_size <= 500:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be between 1 and 500")
        if not 1 <= self.lease_seconds <= 86_400:
            raise ValueError("lease_seconds must be between 1 and 86400")
        if not 0 <= self.base_retry_delay_seconds <= 31_536_000:
            raise ValueError("base_retry_delay_seconds must be between 0 and one year")
        if not 0 <= self.max_retry_delay_seconds <= 31_536_000:
            raise ValueError("max_retry_delay_seconds must be between 0 and one year")
        if self.max_retry_delay_seconds < self.base_retry_delay_seconds:
            raise ValueError("max_retry_delay_seconds must be >= base_retry_delay_seconds")

    def retry_delay(self, attempt_number: int, provider_delay: int | None = None) -> int:
        if provider_delay is not None:
            return min(provider_delay, self.max_retry_delay_seconds)
        exponent = min(max(attempt_number - 1, 0), 30)
        return min(self.base_retry_delay_seconds * (2**exponent), self.max_retry_delay_seconds)


class NotificationDispatcher:
    """Dispatch due jobs via configured provider adapters.

    The actionability callback is mandatory so incident-linked alerts can be
    cancelled when their real incident is resolved or no longer requires contact.
    The callback must query authoritative application state, not infer it from the
    notification payload.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        outbox: NotificationOutbox,
        providers: Mapping[str, NotificationProvider],
        policy: DispatchPolicy,
    ) -> None:
        if any(not channel.strip() for channel in providers):
            raise ValueError("provider channel keys must be non-empty")
        self.outbox = outbox
        self.providers = dict(providers)
        self.policy = policy

    def dispatch_due(
        self,
        *,
        can_deliver: Callable[[NotificationJob], bool],
    ) -> dict[str, object]:
        """Attempt the due batch and return counts plus job IDs and safe states."""
        if not callable(can_deliver):
            raise ValueError("can_deliver must be an authoritative incident-state callback")

        claims = self.outbox.claim_due(
            channels=self.providers.keys(),
            limit=self.policy.batch_size,
            lease_seconds=self.policy.lease_seconds,
        )
        results: list[dict[str, str]] = []
        counts = {"delivered": 0, "retry_scheduled": 0, "dead": 0, "cancelled": 0}

        for claim in claims:
            job = claim.job
            try:
                actionable = can_deliver(job)
            except Exception:
                failed = self._record_failure(
                    claim, "eligibility_check_unavailable", retryable=True
                )
                state = "retry_scheduled" if failed.state == "pending" else "dead"
                counts[state] += 1
                results.append({"job_id": job.job_id, "state": state})
                continue

            if not actionable:
                cancelled = self.outbox.cancel_claimed(
                    job_id=job.job_id,
                    attempt_id=claim.attempt_id,
                    reason_code="incident_no_longer_actionable",
                )
                counts["cancelled"] += 1
                results.append({"job_id": cancelled.job_id, "state": cancelled.state})
                continue

            provider = self.providers[job.channel]
            try:
                receipt = provider.deliver(job, idempotency_key=job.job_id)
                if not isinstance(receipt, DeliveryReceipt):
                    raise TypeError("provider adapter did not return DeliveryReceipt")
            except ProviderDeliveryError as exc:
                failed = self._record_failure(
                    claim,
                    exc.error_code,
                    retryable=exc.retryable,
                    provider_delay=exc.retry_after_seconds,
                )
                state = "retry_scheduled" if failed.state == "pending" else "dead"
                counts[state] += 1
                results.append({"job_id": failed.job_id, "state": state})
                continue
            except Exception:
                # Never persist raw exception text; it may contain credentials or payload data.
                failed = self._record_failure(
                    claim, "provider_unexpected_error", retryable=True
                )
                state = "retry_scheduled" if failed.state == "pending" else "dead"
                counts[state] += 1
                results.append({"job_id": failed.job_id, "state": state})
                continue

            # Keep this outside the provider try/except. If persistence fails after a
            # provider accepted the request, leave the lease to expire and retry with
            # the same idempotency key instead of misclassifying a database error as
            # a provider failure. A provider should deduplicate that key when possible.
            delivered = self.outbox.mark_delivered(
                job_id=job.job_id,
                attempt_id=claim.attempt_id,
                provider_message_id=receipt.provider_message_id,
            )
            counts["delivered"] += 1
            results.append({"job_id": delivered.job_id, "state": delivered.state})

        return {"claimed": len(claims), "counts": counts, "results": results}

    def _record_failure(
        self,
        claim: ClaimedNotification,
        error_code: str,
        *,
        retryable: bool,
        provider_delay: int | None = None,
    ) -> NotificationJob:
        return self.outbox.mark_failed(
            job_id=claim.job.job_id,
            attempt_id=claim.attempt_id,
            error_code=error_code,
            retryable=retryable,
            retry_delay_seconds=self.policy.retry_delay(claim.attempt_number, provider_delay),
        )

"""Durable notification outbox primitives for Aariya.

This module queues notifications only. It deliberately contains no fake provider,
recipient, event, credential, or automatic notification policy. A real dispatcher
must supply channel, recipient, payload, and retry/lease policy from trusted runtime
configuration and real incident state.

Delivery semantics are at-least-once. Providers should honor ``job_id`` as an
idempotency key when they support it; a process crash after provider acceptance but
before recording the receipt can otherwise result in a duplicate delivery.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import uuid
from contextlib import closing
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Iterable


class OutboxConflict(RuntimeError):
    """A job transition is invalid or an idempotency key was reused differently."""


class OutboxNotFound(LookupError):
    """The requested outbox job or attempt does not exist."""


class OutboxValidationError(ValueError):
    """The supplied delivery data or timing policy is invalid."""


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class NotificationJob:
    job_id: str
    dedupe_key: str
    incident_id: str | None
    channel: str
    recipient_id: str
    payload: dict[str, Any]
    state: str
    attempts: int
    max_attempts: int
    available_at: str
    lease_until: str | None
    provider_message_id: str | None
    last_error_code: str | None
    created_at: str
    updated_at: str


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ClaimedNotification:
    job: NotificationJob
    attempt_id: str
    attempt_number: int


def utc_now() -> datetime:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc)


def _iso(value: datetime) -> str:
    return value.astimezone(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="microseconds")


def _json(value: Any) -> str:
    try:
        encoded = json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False, separators=(",", ":"), sort_keys=True)
    except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
        raise OutboxValidationError("payload must contain finite JSON-compatible values") from exc
    if len(encoded.encode("utf-8")) > 16_384:
        raise OutboxValidationError("payload must not exceed 16 KiB")
    return encoded


def _decode(row: sqlite3.Row) -> NotificationJob:
    return NotificationJob(
        job_id=row["job_id"],
        dedupe_key=row["dedupe_key"],
        incident_id=row["incident_id"],
        channel=row["channel"],
        recipient_id=row["recipient_id"],
        payload=json.loads(row["payload_json"]),
        state=row["state"],
        attempts=row["attempts"],
        max_attempts=row["max_attempts"],
        available_at=row["available_at"],
        lease_until=row["lease_until"],
        provider_message_id=row["provider_message_id"],
        last_error_code=row["last_error_code"],
        created_at=row["created_at"],
        updated_at=row["updated_at"],
    )


class NotificationOutbox:
    """SQLite outbox; use the same configured DB path as the proactive event store.

    This is a single-host implementation. For multiple backend instances, use a
    shared production database/queue with equivalent atomic-claim semantics.
    """

    def __init__(self, database_path: Path):
        if not database_path.is_absolute():
            raise OutboxValidationError("database_path must be absolute")
        if str(database_path) == ":memory:":
            raise OutboxValidationError("durable outbox requires a file-backed database")
        self.database_path = database_path
        self.database_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self._initialize()

    def _connect(self) -> sqlite3.Connection:
        connection = sqlite3.connect(self.database_path, timeout=5.0, isolation_level=None)
        connection.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        connection.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
        connection.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000")
        connection.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL")
        return connection

    def _initialize(self) -> None:
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            db.executescript(
                """
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS proactive_notification_outbox (
                    job_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
                    dedupe_key TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
                    incident_id TEXT,
                    channel TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(length(channel) BETWEEN 1 AND 80),
                    recipient_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(length(recipient_id) BETWEEN 1 AND 256),
                    payload_json TEXT NOT NULL,
                    state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('pending','in_flight','delivered','dead','cancelled')),
                    attempts INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(attempts >= 0),
                    max_attempts INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(max_attempts BETWEEN 1 AND 1000),
                    available_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    lease_until TEXT,
                    provider_message_id TEXT,
                    last_error_code TEXT,
                    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    updated_at TEXT NOT NULL
                );
                CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_proactive_outbox_due
                    ON proactive_notification_outbox(state, available_at, lease_until, created_at);
                CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_proactive_outbox_incident
                    ON proactive_notification_outbox(incident_id, state);
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS proactive_notification_attempts (
                    attempt_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
                    job_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES proactive_notification_outbox(job_id),
                    attempt_number INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(attempt_number > 0),
                    state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('started','delivered','retryable_failure','permanent_failure','cancelled')),
                    started_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    finished_at TEXT,
                    provider_message_id TEXT,
                    error_code TEXT,
                    UNIQUE(job_id, attempt_number)
                );
                CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_proactive_outbox_attempts_job
                    ON proactive_notification_attempts(job_id, attempt_number);
                """
            )

    def enqueue(
        self,
        *,
        dedupe_key: str,
        incident_id: str | None,
        channel: str,
        recipient_id: str,
        payload: dict[str, Any],
        max_attempts: int,
        available_at: datetime | None = None,
    ) -> tuple[NotificationJob, bool]:
        """Persist a notification request; return (job, duplicate)."""
        values = {
            "dedupe_key": dedupe_key,
            "incident_id": incident_id,
            "channel": channel,
            "recipient_id": recipient_id,
        }
        for key, value in values.items():
            if value is not None and (not isinstance(value, str) or not value.strip()):
                raise OutboxValidationError(f"{key} must be a non-empty string when provided")
        if len(dedupe_key) > 512:
            raise OutboxValidationError("dedupe_key must not exceed 512 characters")
        if incident_id is not None and len(incident_id) > 200:
            raise OutboxValidationError("incident_id must not exceed 200 characters")
        if len(channel) > 80 or len(recipient_id) > 256:
            raise OutboxValidationError("channel or recipient_id exceeds its maximum length")
        if not isinstance(payload, dict):
            raise OutboxValidationError("payload must be a JSON object")
        if not 1 <= max_attempts <= 1_000:
            raise OutboxValidationError("max_attempts must be between 1 and 1000")
        payload_json = _json(payload)
        now = utc_now()
        available = available_at or now
        if available.tzinfo is None or available.utcoffset() is None:
            raise OutboxValidationError("available_at must include a timezone")
        now_text, available_text = _iso(now), _iso(available)

        db = self._connect()
        try:
            db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
            existing = db.execute(
                "SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE dedupe_key = ?",
                (dedupe_key,),
            ).fetchone()
            if existing:
                expected = (incident_id, channel, recipient_id, payload_json, max_attempts)
                actual = (existing["incident_id"], existing["channel"], existing["recipient_id"], existing["payload_json"], existing["max_attempts"])
                if actual != expected:
                    raise OutboxConflict("dedupe_key was reused with different notification content")
                db.execute("COMMIT")
                return _decode(existing), True

            job_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
            db.execute(
                """INSERT INTO proactive_notification_outbox
                   (job_id, dedupe_key, incident_id, channel, recipient_id, payload_json,
                    state, attempts, max_attempts, available_at, lease_until, provider_message_id,
                    last_error_code, created_at, updated_at)
                   VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, 'pending', 0, ?, ?, NULL, NULL, NULL, ?, ?)""",
                (job_id, dedupe_key, incident_id, channel, recipient_id, payload_json, max_attempts, available_text, now_text, now_text),
            )
            row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
            db.execute("COMMIT")
            return _decode(row), False
        except Exception:
            if db.in_transaction:
                db.execute("ROLLBACK")
            raise
        finally:
            db.close()

    def claim_due(
        self,
        *,
        channels: Iterable[str],
        limit: int,
        lease_seconds: int,
        now: datetime | None = None,
    ) -> list[ClaimedNotification]:
        """Atomically lease due jobs only for channels with registered providers."""
        channel_list = tuple(dict.fromkeys(channels))
        if not channel_list:
            return []
        if any(not isinstance(ch, str) or not ch.strip() for ch in channel_list):
            raise OutboxValidationError("channels must contain non-empty names")
        if not 1 <= limit <= 500:
            raise OutboxValidationError("limit must be between 1 and 500")
        if not 1 <= lease_seconds <= 86_400:
            raise OutboxValidationError("lease_seconds must be between 1 and 86400")
        clock = now or utc_now()
        if clock.tzinfo is None or clock.utcoffset() is None:
            raise OutboxValidationError("now must include a timezone")
        now_text = _iso(clock)
        lease_until = _iso(clock + timedelta(seconds=lease_seconds))
        placeholders = ",".join("?" for _ in channel_list)
        db = self._connect()
        claimed: list[ClaimedNotification] = []
        try:
            db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
            exhausted = db.execute(
                """SELECT job_id FROM proactive_notification_outbox
                   WHERE state = 'in_flight' AND lease_until <= ? AND attempts >= max_attempts""",
                (now_text,),
            ).fetchall()
            for exhausted_row in exhausted:
                job_id = exhausted_row["job_id"]
                db.execute(
                    """UPDATE proactive_notification_attempts
                       SET state = 'permanent_failure', finished_at = ?, error_code = 'lease_expired_after_max_attempts'
                       WHERE job_id = ? AND state = 'started'""",
                    (now_text, job_id),
                )
                db.execute(
                    """UPDATE proactive_notification_outbox
                       SET state = 'dead', lease_until = NULL, last_error_code = 'lease_expired_after_max_attempts', updated_at = ?
                       WHERE job_id = ?""",
                    (now_text, job_id),
                )

            rows = db.execute(
                f"""SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox
                    WHERE channel IN ({placeholders})
                      AND attempts < max_attempts
                      AND available_at <= ?
                      AND (state = 'pending' OR (state = 'in_flight' AND lease_until <= ?))
                    ORDER BY available_at ASC, created_at ASC, job_id ASC
                    LIMIT ?""",
                (*channel_list, now_text, now_text, limit),
            ).fetchall()
            for row in rows:
                # Close the expired attempt before creating its successor.
                # The previous worker may still return later, so transition methods
                # also verify that the attempt is still the current attempt number.
                if row["state"] == "in_flight":
                    db.execute(
                        """UPDATE proactive_notification_attempts
                           SET state = 'retryable_failure', finished_at = ?, error_code = 'lease_expired'
                           WHERE job_id = ? AND attempt_number = ? AND state = 'started'""",
                        (now_text, row["job_id"], int(row["attempts"])),
                    )
                # Reclaiming expired leases is at-least-once. Provider idempotency should use job_id.
                attempt_number = int(row["attempts"]) + 1
                attempt_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
                db.execute(
                    """UPDATE proactive_notification_outbox
                       SET state = 'in_flight', attempts = ?, lease_until = ?, updated_at = ?
                       WHERE job_id = ?""",
                    (attempt_number, lease_until, now_text, row["job_id"]),
                )
                db.execute(
                    """INSERT INTO proactive_notification_attempts
                       (attempt_id, job_id, attempt_number, state, started_at)
                       VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'started', ?)""",
                    (attempt_id, row["job_id"], attempt_number, now_text),
                )
                updated = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (row["job_id"],)).fetchone()
                claimed.append(ClaimedNotification(_decode(updated), attempt_id, attempt_number))
            db.execute("COMMIT")
            return claimed
        except Exception:
            if db.in_transaction:
                db.execute("ROLLBACK")
            raise
        finally:
            db.close()

    def mark_delivered(self, *, job_id: str, attempt_id: str, provider_message_id: str | None) -> NotificationJob:
        now = _iso(utc_now())
        if provider_message_id is not None and (not provider_message_id.strip() or len(provider_message_id) > 512):
            raise OutboxValidationError("provider_message_id must be non-empty and at most 512 characters when provided")
        db = self._connect()
        try:
            db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
            job = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
            attempt = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_attempts WHERE attempt_id = ? AND job_id = ?", (attempt_id, job_id)).fetchone()
            if job is None or attempt is None:
                raise OutboxNotFound("Notification job or attempt not found")
            if job["state"] == "delivered":
                db.execute("COMMIT")
                return _decode(job)
            if (job["state"] != "in_flight" or attempt["state"] != "started"
                    or int(attempt["attempt_number"]) != int(job["attempts"])):
                raise OutboxConflict("Only the current in-flight attempt can be marked delivered")
            db.execute(
                """UPDATE proactive_notification_outbox
                   SET state = 'delivered', lease_until = NULL, provider_message_id = ?,
                       last_error_code = NULL, updated_at = ? WHERE job_id = ?""",
                (provider_message_id, now, job_id),
            )
            db.execute(
                """UPDATE proactive_notification_attempts
                   SET state = 'delivered', finished_at = ?, provider_message_id = ? WHERE attempt_id = ?""",
                (now, provider_message_id, attempt_id),
            )
            row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
            db.execute("COMMIT")
            return _decode(row)
        except Exception:
            if db.in_transaction:
                db.execute("ROLLBACK")
            raise
        finally:
            db.close()

    def mark_failed(
        self,
        *,
        job_id: str,
        attempt_id: str,
        error_code: str,
        retryable: bool,
        retry_delay_seconds: int,
    ) -> NotificationJob:
        """Record failure without persisting raw exception text or credential-bearing details."""
        if not error_code.strip() or len(error_code) > 120:
            raise OutboxValidationError("error_code must be a non-empty provider-safe code of at most 120 characters")
        if not 0 <= retry_delay_seconds <= 31_536_000:
            raise OutboxValidationError("retry_delay_seconds must be between 0 and one year")
        now_dt = utc_now()
        now_text = _iso(now_dt)
        db = self._connect()
        try:
            db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
            job = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
            attempt = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_attempts WHERE attempt_id = ? AND job_id = ?", (attempt_id, job_id)).fetchone()
            if job is None or attempt is None:
                raise OutboxNotFound("Notification job or attempt not found")
            if (job["state"] != "in_flight" or attempt["state"] != "started"
                    or int(attempt["attempt_number"]) != int(job["attempts"])):
                raise OutboxConflict("Only the current in-flight attempt can be marked failed")

            will_retry = retryable and int(job["attempts"]) < int(job["max_attempts"])
            next_state = "pending" if will_retry else "dead"
            attempt_state = "retryable_failure" if will_retry else "permanent_failure"
            next_available = _iso(now_dt + timedelta(seconds=retry_delay_seconds)) if will_retry else job["available_at"]
            db.execute(
                """UPDATE proactive_notification_outbox
                   SET state = ?, available_at = ?, lease_until = NULL, last_error_code = ?, updated_at = ?
                   WHERE job_id = ?""",
                (next_state, next_available, error_code, now_text, job_id),
            )
            db.execute(
                """UPDATE proactive_notification_attempts
                   SET state = ?, finished_at = ?, error_code = ? WHERE attempt_id = ?""",
                (attempt_state, now_text, error_code, attempt_id),
            )
            row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
            db.execute("COMMIT")
            return _decode(row)
        except Exception:
            if db.in_transaction:
                db.execute("ROLLBACK")
            raise
        finally:
            db.close()

    def cancel_claimed(self, *, job_id: str, attempt_id: str, reason_code: str) -> NotificationJob:
        """Cancel a claimed job when a caller confirms its incident is no longer actionable."""
        if not reason_code.strip() or len(reason_code) > 120:
            raise OutboxValidationError("reason_code must be a short non-empty code")
        now = _iso(utc_now())
        db = self._connect()
        try:
            db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
            job = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
            attempt = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_attempts WHERE attempt_id = ? AND job_id = ?", (attempt_id, job_id)).fetchone()
            if job is None or attempt is None:
                raise OutboxNotFound("Notification job or attempt not found")
            if (job["state"] != "in_flight" or attempt["state"] != "started"
                    or int(attempt["attempt_number"]) != int(job["attempts"])):
                raise OutboxConflict("Only the current in-flight attempt can be cancelled")
            db.execute(
                "UPDATE proactive_notification_outbox SET state = 'cancelled', lease_until = NULL, last_error_code = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE job_id = ?",
                (reason_code, now, job_id),
            )
            db.execute(
                "UPDATE proactive_notification_attempts SET state = 'cancelled', finished_at = ?, error_code = ? WHERE attempt_id = ?",
                (now, reason_code, attempt_id),
            )
            row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
            db.execute("COMMIT")
            return _decode(row)
        except Exception:
            if db.in_transaction:
                db.execute("ROLLBACK")
            raise
        finally:
            db.close()

    def get(self, job_id: str) -> NotificationJob:
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox WHERE job_id = ?", (job_id,)).fetchone()
        if row is None:
            raise OutboxNotFound(job_id)
        return _decode(row)

    def list_jobs(self, *, states: Iterable[str] | None = None, limit: int = 100) -> list[NotificationJob]:
        if not 1 <= limit <= 500:
            raise OutboxValidationError("limit must be between 1 and 500")
        state_list = tuple(dict.fromkeys(states or ()))
        valid_states = {"pending", "in_flight", "delivered", "dead", "cancelled"}
        if any(state not in valid_states for state in state_list):
            raise OutboxValidationError("states contains an unsupported state")
        query = "SELECT * FROM proactive_notification_outbox"
        args: tuple[Any, ...] = ()
        if state_list:
            placeholders = ",".join("?" for _ in state_list)
            query += f" WHERE state IN ({placeholders})"
            args = state_list
        query += " ORDER BY created_at DESC, job_id DESC LIMIT ?"
        args += (limit,)
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            rows = db.execute(query, args).fetchall()
        return [_decode(row) for row in rows]

"""Provider-agnostic dispatcher for durable proactive notifications.

No provider credentials or provider implementation is included here. Integrate a
real push/VoIP/in-app provider that implements NotificationProvider, register only
providers whose credentials and destination configuration have been verified, and
supply an authoritative incident-actionability check before delivery.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Callable, Mapping, Protocol

from .outbox import ClaimedNotification, NotificationJob, NotificationOutbox


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class DeliveryReceipt:
    """Receipt returned by a real provider after it accepts a delivery request."""

    provider_message_id: str | None


class NotificationProvider(Protocol):
    """Adapter contract for a real provider such as push, in-app, or VoIP."""

    def deliver(self, job: NotificationJob, *, idempotency_key: str) -> DeliveryReceipt:
        """Send through the configured provider; never fabricate a receipt."""
        ...


class DispatchConfigurationError(RuntimeError):
    """Required dispatcher policy configuration is missing or invalid."""


class ProviderDeliveryError(RuntimeError):
    """A provider failure expressed as a safe code, never raw credential text."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        error_code: str,
        *,
        retryable: bool,
        retry_after_seconds: int | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        if not error_code.strip() or len(error_code) > 120:
            raise ValueError("error_code must be a non-empty safe code of at most 120 characters")
        if retry_after_seconds is not None and not 0 <= retry_after_seconds <= 31_536_000:
            raise ValueError("retry_after_seconds must be between 0 and one year")
        super().__init__(error_code)
        self.error_code = error_code
        self.retryable = retryable
        self.retry_after_seconds = retry_after_seconds


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class DispatchPolicy:
    batch_size: int
    lease_seconds: int
    base_retry_delay_seconds: int
    max_retry_delay_seconds: int

    @classmethod
    def from_environment(cls) -> "DispatchPolicy":
        """Load explicit retry/lease policy. Missing values never get defaults."""
        names = {
            "batch_size": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_BATCH_SIZE",
            "lease_seconds": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_LEASE_SECONDS",
            "base_retry_delay_seconds": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_BASE_SECONDS",
            "max_retry_delay_seconds": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_MAX_SECONDS",
        }
        parsed: dict[str, int] = {}
        for field, env_name in names.items():
            raw = os.environ.get(env_name, "").strip()
            if not raw:
                raise DispatchConfigurationError(f"{env_name} is required")
            try:
                parsed[field] = int(raw)
            except ValueError as exc:
                raise DispatchConfigurationError(f"{env_name} must be an integer") from exc
        try:
            return cls(**parsed)
        except ValueError as exc:
            raise DispatchConfigurationError("Notification dispatcher policy is outside its allowed bounds") from exc

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not 1 <= self.batch_size <= 500:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be between 1 and 500")
        if not 1 <= self.lease_seconds <= 86_400:
            raise ValueError("lease_seconds must be between 1 and 86400")
        if not 0 <= self.base_retry_delay_seconds <= 31_536_000:
            raise ValueError("base_retry_delay_seconds must be between 0 and one year")
        if not 0 <= self.max_retry_delay_seconds <= 31_536_000:
            raise ValueError("max_retry_delay_seconds must be between 0 and one year")
        if self.max_retry_delay_seconds < self.base_retry_delay_seconds:
            raise ValueError("max_retry_delay_seconds must be >= base_retry_delay_seconds")

    def retry_delay(self, attempt_number: int, provider_delay: int | None = None) -> int:
        if provider_delay is not None:
            return min(provider_delay, self.max_retry_delay_seconds)
        exponent = min(max(attempt_number - 1, 0), 30)
        return min(self.base_retry_delay_seconds * (2**exponent), self.max_retry_delay_seconds)


class NotificationDispatcher:
    """Dispatch due jobs via configured provider adapters.

    The actionability callback is mandatory so incident-linked alerts can be
    cancelled when their real incident is resolved or no longer requires contact.
    The callback must query authoritative application state, not infer it from the
    notification payload.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        outbox: NotificationOutbox,
        providers: Mapping[str, NotificationProvider],
        policy: DispatchPolicy,
    ) -> None:
        if any(not channel.strip() for channel in providers):
            raise ValueError("provider channel keys must be non-empty")
        self.outbox = outbox
        self.providers = dict(providers)
        self.policy = policy

    def dispatch_due(
        self,
        *,
        can_deliver: Callable[[NotificationJob], bool],
    ) -> dict[str, object]:
        """Attempt the due batch and return counts plus job IDs and safe states."""
        if not callable(can_deliver):
            raise ValueError("can_deliver must be an authoritative incident-state callback")

        claims = self.outbox.claim_due(
            channels=self.providers.keys(),
            limit=self.policy.batch_size,
            lease_seconds=self.policy.lease_seconds,
        )
        results: list[dict[str, str]] = []
        counts = {"delivered": 0, "retry_scheduled": 0, "dead": 0, "cancelled": 0}

        for claim in claims:
            job = claim.job
            try:
                actionable = can_deliver(job)
            except Exception:
                failed = self._record_failure(
                    claim, "eligibility_check_unavailable", retryable=True
                )
                state = "retry_scheduled" if failed.state == "pending" else "dead"
                counts[state] += 1
                results.append({"job_id": job.job_id, "state": state})
                continue

            if not actionable:
                cancelled = self.outbox.cancel_claimed(
                    job_id=job.job_id,
                    attempt_id=claim.attempt_id,
                    reason_code="incident_no_longer_actionable",
                )
                counts["cancelled"] += 1
                results.append({"job_id": cancelled.job_id, "state": cancelled.state})
                continue

            provider = self.providers[job.channel]
            try:
                receipt = provider.deliver(job, idempotency_key=job.job_id)
                if not isinstance(receipt, DeliveryReceipt):
                    raise TypeError("provider adapter did not return DeliveryReceipt")
            except ProviderDeliveryError as exc:
                failed = self._record_failure(
                    claim,
                    exc.error_code,
                    retryable=exc.retryable,
                    provider_delay=exc.retry_after_seconds,
                )
                state = "retry_scheduled" if failed.state == "pending" else "dead"
                counts[state] += 1
                results.append({"job_id": failed.job_id, "state": state})
                continue
            except Exception:
                # Never persist raw exception text; it may contain credentials or payload data.
                failed = self._record_failure(
                    claim, "provider_unexpected_error", retryable=True
                )
                state = "retry_scheduled" if failed.state == "pending" else "dead"
                counts[state] += 1
                results.append({"job_id": failed.job_id, "state": state})
                continue

            # Keep this outside the provider try/except. If persistence fails after a
            # provider accepted the request, leave the lease to expire and retry with
            # the same idempotency key instead of misclassifying a database error as
            # a provider failure. A provider should deduplicate that key when possible.
            delivered = self.outbox.mark_delivered(
                job_id=job.job_id,
                attempt_id=claim.attempt_id,
                provider_message_id=receipt.provider_message_id,
            )
            counts["delivered"] += 1
            results.append({"job_id": delivered.job_id, "state": delivered.state})

        return {"claimed": len(claims), "counts": counts, "results": results}

    def _record_failure(
        self,
        claim: ClaimedNotification,
        error_code: str,
        *,
        retryable: bool,
        provider_delay: int | None = None,
    ) -> NotificationJob:
        return self.outbox.mark_failed(
            job_id=claim.job.job_id,
            attempt_id=claim.attempt_id,
            error_code=error_code,
            retryable=retryable,
            retry_delay_seconds=self.policy.retry_delay(claim.attempt_number, provider_delay),
        )

Aariya notification outbox and dispatcher integration
This adds durable delivery scheduling to the proactive-event foundation. It is an additive module: outbox.py stores notification jobs and attempts, while dispatcher.py sends them only through real provider adapters supplied by the application.
What is included
- A durable outbox stored in the same configured SQLite database path as the event store.
- A unique dedupe_key to prevent duplicate queueing of the same persisted notification decision.
- Atomic job claims and expiring leases so a crashed worker's work can be recovered.
- Attempt history, per-job maximum attempts, retry scheduling, and a terminal dead state.
- Provider idempotency key passed as the persistent job_id.
- Mandatory authoritative can_deliver callback, so resolved incidents can be cancelled before sending.
- Safe error codes rather than raw exception text, which could contain credentials or private content.
- Provider-agnostic interfaces for push, in-app messaging, and internet voice-call/VoIP adapters.
Add to Aariya
Copy these modules into the existing backend package:
- server/proactive/outbox.py
- server/proactive/dispatcher.py
Keep the existing server/proactive package and register no second FastAPI application. These modules do not change the existing event API routes.
Queue only after an actual policy decision
Do not enqueue every ingested event. Call NotificationOutbox.enqueue(...) only after Aariya's real event-assessment/escalation policy decides that a specific recipient should be contacted. Resolve all of these from persisted application data:
- incident_id: the real incident ID returned by the event store.
- channel: a channel allowed by the user's active notification preferences and the current escalation policy.
- recipient_id: a verified device/user endpoint from the device registry, never a guessed ID.
- payload: the minimum content required by the channel. Prefer a generic lock-screen notice and keep sensitive evidence behind authenticated app access.
- dedupe_key: a stable key derived from the persisted notification-decision/escalation record. Reuse that key for retries. Do not generate a new key merely because a network request failed.
- max_attempts: a value loaded from validated runtime policy/configuration.
The module intentionally does not invent an event, recipient, destination token, notification content, severity threshold, or escalation rule.
Connect a real provider
Implement NotificationProvider.deliver(job, idempotency_key=job.job_id) using the provider chosen by the project and real credentials delivered through secret management. Register a provider only after its credentials and channel are configured. The adapter must return DeliveryReceipt only after the provider confirms acceptance, and raise ProviderDeliveryError with a safe error code and retryability classification on provider failure. Never put raw HTTP bodies, access tokens, phone numbers, or payload text in error_code.

The dispatcher passes the stable job_id as the provider idempotency key. A provider that does not support idempotency may deliver twice if the provider accepts a request but the backend crashes before saving the receipt. Do not claim exactly-once delivery unless the selected provider explicitly supports it.
Run as a worker, not during every request
Create one supervised worker process/task that periodically invokes:
summary = dispatcher.dispatch_due(can_deliver=authoritative_incident_policy_check)
authoritative_incident_policy_check must read current incident state and escalation permissions from the real application store. It should return False for resolved/cancelled incidents or when the user's current communication settings prohibit that channel. If the check is unavailable, the dispatcher fails closed and schedules a retry instead of sending.

Load a validated DispatchPolicy from runtime configuration with DispatchPolicy.from_environment(). Its fields are mandatory and have no fallback defaults:
- batch_size
- lease_seconds
- base_retry_delay_seconds
- max_retry_delay_seconds
The corresponding empty fields are documented in .env.proactive.example. Populate them from a reviewed runtime policy before starting the worker.

Per-job max_attempts is set when the notification decision is persisted. The dispatcher should run under a process supervisor, expose operational metrics, and stop cleanly on shutdown.
Privacy, security, and deployment notes
- Do not store sensitive evidence or full conversation transcripts in the push payload. Store authorized evidence in the application's protected evidence store and send only a reference/notification hint.
- Protect the database file and backups with OS permissions and storage encryption appropriate to the deployment. This SQLite module does not itself encrypt payloads at rest.
- The database is a single-host starting point. For multiple workers/replicas, migrate the event store and outbox to the same shared production database or durable queue with equivalent atomic claims and uniqueness constraints.
- Voice calls are not enabled by this code. A real VoIP/provider adapter plus Flutter incoming-call handling, call permissions, acknowledgements, cancellation, and provider webhooks must be integrated separately.
- An outbox in the same database cannot report failure of that database itself. The independent Guardian must have a separate failure-detection and reporting path for core/database outages.
Tests
Run from the overlay root:
python -m pytest -q
python -m compileall -q server tests
The provider adapters in dispatcher tests are test-only implementations of the interface. They are not included as production providers and must not be registered in a running Aariya deployment.

from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from server.proactive.dispatcher import (
    DeliveryReceipt,
    DispatchConfigurationError,
    DispatchPolicy,
    NotificationDispatcher,
    ProviderDeliveryError,
)
from server.proactive.outbox import NotificationOutbox


class AcceptingProvider:
    """Test-only adapter exercising the provider contract, not a live provider."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.keys: list[str] = []

    def deliver(self, job, *, idempotency_key: str) -> DeliveryReceipt:
        self.keys.append(idempotency_key)
        return DeliveryReceipt(provider_message_id="test-receipt")


class RejectingProvider:
    def deliver(self, job, *, idempotency_key: str) -> DeliveryReceipt:
        raise ProviderDeliveryError("provider_rate_limited", retryable=True, retry_after_seconds=15)


def setup_job(tmp_path: Path, *, key: str = "incident:9:push"):
    outbox = NotificationOutbox((tmp_path / "database.sqlite3").resolve())
    job, _ = outbox.enqueue(
        dedupe_key=key,
        incident_id="incident-nine",
        channel="push",
        recipient_id="configured-device",
        payload={"incident_id": "incident-nine"},
        max_attempts=3,
    )
    return outbox, job


def policy() -> DispatchPolicy:
    return DispatchPolicy(batch_size=5, lease_seconds=30, base_retry_delay_seconds=10, max_retry_delay_seconds=300)


def test_dispatcher_uses_provider_and_idempotency_key(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox, queued = setup_job(tmp_path)
    provider = AcceptingProvider()
    dispatcher = NotificationDispatcher(outbox=outbox, providers={"push": provider}, policy=policy())
    result = dispatcher.dispatch_due(can_deliver=lambda job: job.incident_id == "incident-nine")
    assert result["counts"]["delivered"] == 1
    assert provider.keys == [queued.job_id]
    assert outbox.get(queued.job_id).state == "delivered"


def test_dispatcher_retries_provider_rate_limit_without_persisting_raw_error(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox, queued = setup_job(tmp_path)
    dispatcher = NotificationDispatcher(outbox=outbox, providers={"push": RejectingProvider()}, policy=policy())
    result = dispatcher.dispatch_due(can_deliver=lambda _job: True)
    assert result["counts"]["retry_scheduled"] == 1
    job = outbox.get(queued.job_id)
    assert job.state == "pending"
    assert job.last_error_code == "provider_rate_limited"


def test_dispatcher_cancels_when_authoritative_check_says_no_longer_actionable(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox, queued = setup_job(tmp_path)
    provider = AcceptingProvider()
    dispatcher = NotificationDispatcher(outbox=outbox, providers={"push": provider}, policy=policy())
    result = dispatcher.dispatch_due(can_deliver=lambda _job: False)
    assert result["counts"]["cancelled"] == 1
    assert provider.keys == []
    assert outbox.get(queued.job_id).state == "cancelled"


def test_unavailable_actionability_check_fails_closed_and_retries(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox, queued = setup_job(tmp_path)
    provider = AcceptingProvider()
    dispatcher = NotificationDispatcher(outbox=outbox, providers={"push": provider}, policy=policy())

    def unavailable(_job):
        raise RuntimeError("potentially secret error details must not be persisted")

    result = dispatcher.dispatch_due(can_deliver=unavailable)
    assert result["counts"]["retry_scheduled"] == 1
    assert provider.keys == []
    job = outbox.get(queued.job_id)
    assert job.last_error_code == "eligibility_check_unavailable"


def test_dispatch_policy_requires_explicit_environment_configuration(monkeypatch) -> None:
    for name in (
        "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_BATCH_SIZE",
        "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_LEASE_SECONDS",
        "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_BASE_SECONDS",
        "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_MAX_SECONDS",
    ):
        monkeypatch.delenv(name, raising=False)
    with pytest.raises(DispatchConfigurationError):
        DispatchPolicy.from_environment()


def test_dispatch_policy_loads_explicit_environment_configuration(monkeypatch) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setenv("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_BATCH_SIZE", "12")
    monkeypatch.setenv("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_LEASE_SECONDS", "45")
    monkeypatch.setenv("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_BASE_SECONDS", "5")
    monkeypatch.setenv("AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_MAX_SECONDS", "300")
    configured = DispatchPolicy.from_environment()
    assert configured.batch_size == 12
    assert configured.lease_seconds == 45
    assert configured.retry_delay(4) == 40

from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from server.proactive.outbox import (
    NotificationOutbox,
    OutboxConflict,
    OutboxValidationError,
)


def make_outbox(tmp_path: Path) -> NotificationOutbox:
    return NotificationOutbox((tmp_path / "proactive.sqlite3").resolve())


def enqueue(outbox: NotificationOutbox, *, dedupe_key: str = "incident:one:push"):
    return outbox.enqueue(
        dedupe_key=dedupe_key,
        incident_id="incident-one",
        channel="push",
        recipient_id="configured-device-id",
        payload={"incident_id": "incident-one", "notice": "A proactive incident needs attention"},
        max_attempts=4,
    )


def test_enqueue_persists_across_reopen_and_is_idempotent(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    first, duplicate = enqueue(outbox)
    assert not duplicate
    reopened = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    second, duplicate = enqueue(reopened)
    assert duplicate
    assert second.job_id == first.job_id
    assert second.state == "pending"


def test_same_dedupe_key_with_changed_payload_conflicts(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    enqueue(outbox)
    with pytest.raises(OutboxConflict):
        outbox.enqueue(
            dedupe_key="incident:one:push",
            incident_id="incident-one",
            channel="push",
            recipient_id="configured-device-id",
            payload={"incident_id": "incident-one", "notice": "Different content"},
            max_attempts=4,
        )


def test_claim_is_limited_to_channels_with_registered_providers(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    enqueue(outbox)
    assert outbox.claim_due(channels=[], limit=10, lease_seconds=30) == []
    assert outbox.claim_due(channels=["voice_call"], limit=10, lease_seconds=30) == []
    assert outbox.get(outbox.list_jobs()[0].job_id).state == "pending"


def test_claim_and_mark_delivered_records_attempt(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    queued, _ = enqueue(outbox)
    claims = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=5, lease_seconds=30)
    assert len(claims) == 1
    claim = claims[0]
    assert claim.job.job_id == queued.job_id
    assert claim.attempt_number == 1
    delivered = outbox.mark_delivered(job_id=claim.job.job_id, attempt_id=claim.attempt_id, provider_message_id="provider-receipt")
    assert delivered.state == "delivered"
    assert delivered.provider_message_id == "provider-receipt"


def test_retry_waits_until_available_at_and_then_can_be_claimed(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    queued, _ = outbox.enqueue(
        dedupe_key="retry-key", incident_id="incident-one", channel="push", recipient_id="device",
        payload={"incident_id": "incident-one"}, max_attempts=3, available_at=now - timedelta(seconds=1),
    )
    claim = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=30, now=now)[0]
    retried = outbox.mark_failed(
        job_id=queued.job_id, attempt_id=claim.attempt_id, error_code="provider_timeout",
        retryable=True, retry_delay_seconds=60,
    )
    assert retried.state == "pending"
    assert outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=30, now=now) == []
    later = now + timedelta(seconds=61)
    again = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=30, now=later)
    assert len(again) == 1
    assert again[0].attempt_number == 2


def test_permanent_failure_moves_job_to_dead(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    queued, _ = enqueue(outbox)
    claim = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=30)[0]
    dead = outbox.mark_failed(
        job_id=queued.job_id, attempt_id=claim.attempt_id, error_code="invalid_device_token",
        retryable=False, retry_delay_seconds=60,
    )
    assert dead.state == "dead"


def test_attempt_cannot_be_completed_twice_after_failure(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    queued, _ = enqueue(outbox)
    claim = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=30)[0]
    outbox.mark_failed(
        job_id=queued.job_id, attempt_id=claim.attempt_id, error_code="provider_timeout",
        retryable=True, retry_delay_seconds=0,
    )
    with pytest.raises(OutboxConflict):
        outbox.mark_delivered(job_id=queued.job_id, attempt_id=claim.attempt_id, provider_message_id=None)


def test_invalid_json_payload_is_rejected(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    with pytest.raises(OutboxValidationError):
        outbox.enqueue(
            dedupe_key="nan-key", incident_id=None, channel="push", recipient_id="device",
            payload={"invalid": float("nan")}, max_attempts=3,
        )


def test_expired_lease_is_reclaimed_with_new_attempt(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    queued, _ = enqueue(outbox)
    start = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    first = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=10, now=start)[0]
    after_expiry = start + timedelta(seconds=11)
    second = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=10, now=after_expiry)[0]
    assert second.job.job_id == queued.job_id
    assert second.attempt_id != first.attempt_id
    assert second.attempt_number == 2


def test_expired_last_attempt_becomes_dead_instead_of_retrying_forever(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    queued, _ = outbox.enqueue(
        dedupe_key="last-attempt", incident_id="incident-one", channel="push", recipient_id="device",
        payload={"incident_id": "incident-one"}, max_attempts=1,
    )
    start = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    claim = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=5, now=start)[0]
    after_expiry = start + timedelta(seconds=6)
    assert outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=5, now=after_expiry) == []
    job = outbox.get(queued.job_id)
    assert job.state == "dead"
    assert job.last_error_code == "lease_expired_after_max_attempts"


def test_expired_lease_invalidates_old_attempt_receipt(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    queued, _ = enqueue(outbox)
    start = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    old_claim = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=5, now=start)[0]
    new_claim = outbox.claim_due(
        channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=5, now=start + timedelta(seconds=6)
    )[0]
    assert new_claim.attempt_number == 2
    with pytest.raises(OutboxConflict):
        outbox.mark_delivered(
            job_id=queued.job_id, attempt_id=old_claim.attempt_id, provider_message_id="late-receipt"
        )


def test_expired_lease_invalidates_old_attempt_receipt(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    outbox = make_outbox(tmp_path)
    queued, _ = enqueue(outbox)
    start = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    old_claim = outbox.claim_due(channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=5, now=start)[0]
    new_claim = outbox.claim_due(
        channels=["push"], limit=1, lease_seconds=5, now=start + timedelta(seconds=6)
    )[0]
    assert new_claim.attempt_number == 2
    with pytest.raises(OutboxConflict):
        outbox.mark_delivered(
            job_id=queued.job_id, attempt_id=old_claim.attempt_id, provider_message_id="late-receipt"
        )

1. The complete requirements list
This checklist consolidates the requirements discussed in this conversation and the Aariya proactive-system specification.

A. Event monitoring and evidence

Detect real system failures, task failures, deadlines, critical events, security incidents, autonomous discoveries, recovery events, and task completions. Authenticate each source; validate, normalize, persist, deduplicate, correlate related events, preserve timestamps and evidence provenance, and reject replay or conflicting event IDs.




B. Importance assessment and escalation

Assess severity, urgency, confidence, evidence quality, impact, reversibility, response deadline, user preferences, current presence mode and safe recovery options. Decide whether to record, message, push, call, ask a question, or use an authorized fallback. A model recommendation never overrides deterministic policy.




C. Reliable proactive messaging

Use a persistent outbox, idempotency keys, retry/backoff, worker leases, delivery receipts and explicit delivery states. Cancel or downgrade pending messages when incidents resolve. Separate queued, provider-accepted, device-delivered, displayed, answered, acknowledged and resolved states.




D. Internet voice calls

Allow authorized proactive calls for urgent situations. Reuse Aariya’s existing conversation, memory, cognitive-state, voice-director and TTS pipeline. Support answer, decline, missed calls, retries, configured fallback and duplicate-call suppression across devices. Never claim a call connected unless the provider confirms it.




E. One ongoing conversation

Messages, calls, app sessions, desktop and web use the same authenticated user identity and relevant conversation context. A critical event can interrupt without destroying the previous conversation; the user can continue the incident thread and then return to the earlier conversation.




F. Authorization and real actions

Separate permission to contact you from permission to perform an action. Use a server-side capability/permission check for every execution; scope it to action, resource, user and expiry. Ask when ambiguous, require appropriate approval for high-impact operations, authenticate sensitive approval, verify the actual result, and preserve an audit trail.




G. Evidence and incident memory

“Show me” should open authorized evidence in the active device or session. Store real event details, evidence references, assessment/rationale, decisions, calls/messages, user response, authorization, action execution, verification, outcome and feedback. Preserve provenance and restrict evidence access.




H. Hybrid preference learning

Track observed, learned and explicitly confirmed preferences separately. Learn low-risk personalization from real outcomes and feedback. Ask before changing material escalation behavior. A preference cannot override security, authorization, or confirmed restrictions. Keep version history and provide correction/rollback.




I. Security and privacy

Authenticated devices and sources, TLS, short-lived sessions, secure key lifecycle, protected data at rest, replay protection, permission-aware logs, prompt-injection isolation, trusted-device revocation, privacy controls and minimized push previews. Decide explicitly whether the gateway is a trusted plaintext processor or an encrypted relay.




J. Independent Guardian/Watchdog

Detect AI Core, database, communication gateway, provider and delivery-path failures from an independently operated failure domain. Send alerts via separately configured channels where available. Keep Guardian permissions narrow and do not let it depend entirely on the component it is meant to monitor.




K. Existing Flutter app and background operation

Integrate secure incident views, push handling, call join/answer/decline, evidence viewing, approvals and conversation continuity into the existing app. Handle background/closed-app behavior within actual iOS/Android rules and permissions. Support trusted phone, tablet, desktop and web sessions without duplicate calls.




L. Operations and recovery

Health and readiness checks, structured logs with sensitive-data redaction, metrics, alerts, durable jobs, dead-letter/recovery procedures, database backups, migration handling, bounded retries, service shutdown/startup recovery, and tested failure scenarios. Do not start a service that requires missing critical configuration.



Required event-to-action lifecycle

2. Code map: which files implement which requirements?
The backend files below are the existing additive foundation from our previous steps. I ran the test suite again: 26 tests passed. That validates the event-store and notification-outbox slice, not the entire Aariya system.
File	Requirements covered
schemas.py	Event types, validation, incident states, evidence references
config.py	Explicit database path, source credentials, source event permissions
store.py	Durable events/incidents, deduplication, correlation, audit history
router.py	Authenticated API endpoints for events and incident state changes
source_client.py	Real monitoring-source integration
outbox.py	Durable notifications, idempotency, worker leases and retry states
dispatcher.py	Provider interface, retry policy, cancellation and delivery receipts
importance.py (next required module)	Versioned importance assessment and policy recommendations
action_authorization.py (next required module)	Capability grants, approvals, action-risk checks
preference_learning.py (next required module)	Observed/learned/confirmed preferences and guarded updates
guardian.py (next required module)	Independent health probes and authorized outage reporting
Flutter integration (project-specific)	Incident UI, device registration, push/call handling, shared conversations
Provider adapters (project-specific)	Real push/VoIP delivery, receipts, call lifecycle and webhook verification

The last five items are not implemented just because their names appear in this table. I’ll include their required contracts and starter code after the verified foundation. Real integrations still need your actual Aariya source tree and configured providers.
3. Foundation: source code directly in chat
Use the following layout for these additive backend files:
server/
  proactive/
    __init__.py
    schemas.py
    config.py
    store.py
    router.py
    source_client.py
    outbox.py
    dispatcher.py


The code uses Python 3.11+ syntax, Pydantic v2 and FastAPI. These modules should be merged into Aariya’s existing backend after checking its dependency versions and authentication design.
File 1 — server/proactive/schemas.py
Defines the validated event and incident contracts. Events originate from authenticated source systems; this file does not create operational events.
from __future__ import annotationsimport jsonfrom datetime import datetimefrom enum import StrEnumfrom typing import Anyfrom pydantic import (    BaseModel,    ConfigDict,    Field,    field_validator,    model_validator,)class EventType(StrEnum):    SYSTEM_FAILURE = "system_failure"    TASK_FAILURE = "task_failure"    CRITICAL_EVENT = "critical_event"    DEADLINE = "deadline"    SECURITY_EVENT = "security_event"    AUTONOMOUS_DISCOVERY = "autonomous_discovery"    RECOVERY = "recovery"    TASK_COMPLETED = "task_completed"class Severity(StrEnum):    INFO = "info"    WARNING = "warning"    HIGH = "high"    CRITICAL = "critical"class IncidentState(StrEnum):    OPEN = "open"    ACKNOWLEDGED = "acknowledged"    RESOLVED = "resolved"class EventIn(BaseModel):    """An observation supplied by an authenticated source."""    model_config = ConfigDict(        extra="forbid",        str_strip_whitespace=True,    )    source_event_id: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=200)    event_type: EventType    severity: Severity    title: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=200)    summary: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=4_000)



File 2 — server/proactive/config.py
There are no default operational credentials. The module refuses to start this feature unless required configuration is present.
from __future__ import annotationsimport jsonimport osfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom pathlib import Pathfrom typing import Mappingfrom .schemas import EventTypeclass ConfigurationError(RuntimeError):    """Required configuration is absent or invalid."""@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)class SourceAuthorization:    token: str    allowed_event_types: frozenset[EventType]@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)class Settings:    database_path: Path    user_token: str    user_id: str    source_tokens: Mapping[str, SourceAuthorization]    @classmethod    def from_environment(cls) -> "Settings":        raw_path = os.environ.get(            "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_DB_PATH", ""        ).strip()        user_token = os.environ.get(            "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_TOKEN", ""        )



Important integration note: this module currently uses a separately configured user token as a bootstrap authentication boundary. In the production Aariya app, connect incident endpoints to Aariya’s existing authenticated session and user authorization system. Do not make a static bearer token the permanent replacement for proper user sessions, revocation, and multi-device identity.
File 3 — server/proactive/store.py
This is the durable event and incident store. It uses parameterized SQL, SQLite transactions, source event IDs for deduplication, severity merging, and an audit record for each event ingestion and incident state change.

Scope note: this initial store correlates by source and correlation key. Cross-source correlation, source-signature verification, evidence blob storage and stronger append-only/external audit anchoring remain separate production features. Do not treat a hash or evidence reference as proof that the underlying claim is true.
File 4 — server/proactive/router.py
These endpoints ingest events and expose incident details. They are not the final multi-tenant authorization design; connect them to your existing Aariya identity and per-user/device permissions before enabling them in production.
