                continue
            except Exception:
                # Provider exceptions may contain URLs, credentials or response bodies.
                result = self.store.mark_failure(invitation_id=invite.invitation_id,
                    worker_id=worker_id, error_code="provider_internal_error", retryable=True, now=now)
                if result.status == "retry_wait":
                    retry_scheduled += 1
                else:
                    terminal += 1
                continue

            # A response may arrive after a lease has expired and another worker has
            # reclaimed it, or after the call was cancelled. Do not turn that race
            # into a second failure attempt or misreport it as a fresh acceptance.
            try:
                result = self.store.mark_provider_accepted(
                    invitation_id=invite.invitation_id, worker_id=worker_id,
                    provider_message_id=receipt.provider_message_id, now=now,
                )
            except InvitationConflict:
                skipped += 1
                continue
            if result.status in {"provider_accepted", "app_received"}:
                accepted += 1
            else:
                terminal += 1
        return DispatchSummary(len(claimed), accepted, retry_scheduled, terminal, skipped)



"""Production adapters for iOS PushKit/APNs and Android FCM call wake-ups.

These adapters do provider I/O only. Call eligibility, consent, authenticated
installation ownership and current device-token resolution remain the job of
injected repositories and the call-policy layer. Neither adapter sends
incident evidence or an AI answer in a push payload.

Optional dependencies (installed only when these transports are enabled):
  ``httpx[http2]`` and ``PyJWT[crypto]`` for APNs; ``firebase-admin>=7.5`` for FCM.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import os
import re
import threading
import time
import uuid
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Protocol

from .call_invitation_delivery import (
    InvitationDeliveryError,
    InvitationEnvelope,
    ProviderAcceptance,
)

_TOKEN_RE = re.compile(r"^[A-Fa-f0-9]{64,512}$")
_ID_RE = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,127}$")


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class VoipPushTarget:
    device_id: str
    user_id: str
    voip_device_token: str
    token_version: str
    active: bool
    trusted: bool
    call_enabled: bool
    call_consent: bool


class VoipTargetRepository(Protocol):
    def resolve_current_authorized_voip_target(
        self, *, user_id: str, device_id: str,
    ) -> VoipPushTarget | None:
        """Resolve current token and recheck owner, trust, app state and call consent."""
        ...

    def invalidate_voip_token_if_version_matches(
        self, *, device_id: str, token_version: str,
    ) -> bool:
        """Conditionally deactivate the failed token version, never a newer token."""
        ...


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ApnsVoipConfig:
    team_id: str
    key_id: str
    bundle_id: str
    private_key_pem: str
    use_sandbox: bool = False
    timeout_seconds: float = 4.0

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name, value in (("team_id", self.team_id), ("key_id", self.key_id), ("bundle_id", self.bundle_id)):
            if not isinstance(value, str) or not value.strip() or len(value) > 256:
                raise ValueError(f"APNs {name} is required")
        if "-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----" not in self.private_key_pem:
            raise ValueError("APNs signing key must be a PEM private key from a secret manager")
        if not 0.5 <= self.timeout_seconds <= 15:
            raise ValueError("APNs timeout_seconds must be between 0.5 and 15")

    @classmethod
    def from_environment(cls) -> "ApnsVoipConfig":
        key_path = os.environ.get("AARIYA_APNS_PRIVATE_KEY_FILE", "").strip()
        if not key_path:
            raise RuntimeError("AARIYA_APNS_PRIVATE_KEY_FILE must point to a protected secret mount")
        try:
            pem = Path(key_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        except OSError as exc:
            raise RuntimeError("APNs private key could not be read from the configured secret mount") from exc
        return cls(
            team_id=os.environ.get("AARIYA_APNS_TEAM_ID", "").strip(),
            key_id=os.environ.get("AARIYA_APNS_KEY_ID", "").strip(),
            bundle_id=os.environ.get("AARIYA_IOS_BUNDLE_ID", "").strip(),
            private_key_pem=pem,
            use_sandbox=os.environ.get("AARIYA_APNS_USE_SANDBOX", "false").strip().lower() in {"1", "true", "yes"},
        )


class ApnsVoipTransport:
    """APNs HTTP/2 provider for iOS PushKit call invitations."""

    def __init__(
        self, *, config: ApnsVoipConfig,
        target_repository: VoipTargetRepository,
        http_client: Any | None = None,
        jwt_module: Any | None = None,
        clock=time.time,
    ):
        self.config = config
        self.target_repository = target_repository
        self._clock = clock
        self._jwt = jwt_module
        self._jwt_cache: str | None = None
        self._jwt_created_at = 0.0
        self._jwt_lock = threading.Lock()
        if http_client is None:
            try:
                import httpx
            except ImportError as exc:
                raise RuntimeError("APNs transport requires httpx[http2]") from exc
            self.http_client = httpx.Client(http2=True, timeout=config.timeout_seconds)
            self._owns_client = True
        else:
            self.http_client = http_client
            self._owns_client = False

    @classmethod
    def from_environment(cls, *, target_repository: VoipTargetRepository) -> "ApnsVoipTransport":
        return cls(config=ApnsVoipConfig.from_environment(), target_repository=target_repository)

    def _bearer_token(self) -> str:
        with self._jwt_lock:
            now = int(self._clock())
            # Apple provider JWTs should be refreshed before their one-hour limit.
            if self._jwt_cache and now - self._jwt_created_at < 45 * 60:
                return self._jwt_cache
            jwt_module = self._jwt
            if jwt_module is None:
                try:
                    import jwt as jwt_module
                except ImportError as exc:
                    raise RuntimeError("APNs transport requires PyJWT[crypto]") from exc
            try:
                encoded = jwt_module.encode(
                    {"iss": self.config.team_id, "iat": now},
                    self.config.private_key_pem,
                    algorithm="ES256",
                    headers={"kid": self.config.key_id, "typ": "JWT"},
                )
            except Exception as exc:
                raise RuntimeError("APNs provider JWT could not be signed") from exc
            if isinstance(encoded, bytes):
                encoded = encoded.decode("ascii")
            if not isinstance(encoded, str) or encoded.count(".") != 2:
                raise RuntimeError("APNs JWT signer returned an invalid token")
            self._jwt_cache = encoded
            self._jwt_created_at = now
            return encoded

    def send(self, envelope: InvitationEnvelope, *, idempotency_key: str) -> ProviderAcceptance:
        if envelope.platform != "ios_pushkit":
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_wrong_platform", retryable=False)
        try:
            call_uuid = str(uuid.UUID(envelope.call_id))
            invitation_uuid = str(uuid.UUID(envelope.invitation_id))
        except (ValueError, AttributeError) as exc:
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_invalid_call_reference", retryable=False) from exc
        try:
            target = self.target_repository.resolve_current_authorized_voip_target(
                user_id=envelope.user_id, device_id=envelope.device_id,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_target_lookup_failed", retryable=True) from exc
        if target is None or (
            target.user_id != envelope.user_id
            or target.device_id != envelope.device_id
            or not target.active or not target.trusted
            or not target.call_enabled or not target.call_consent
            or not _TOKEN_RE.fullmatch(target.voip_device_token or "")
        ):
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_target_not_authorized", retryable=False)

        host = "https://api.sandbox.push.apple.com" if self.config.use_sandbox else "https://api.push.apple.com"
        url = f"{host}/3/device/{target.voip_device_token}"
        # PushKit VoIP notifications should be delivered immediately, not queued
        # for a later time. Expiry=0 follows Apple's guidance for call invitations.
        headers = {
            "authorization": f"bearer {self._bearer_token()}",
            "apns-push-type": "voip",
            "apns-topic": f"{self.config.bundle_id}.voip",
            "apns-priority": "10",
            "apns-expiration": "0",
            "content-type": "application/json",
        }
        payload = {
            "aps": {},
            "callUUID": call_uuid,
            "invitationID": invitation_uuid,
            "handle": "Aariya",
        }
        try:
            response = self.http_client.post(url, headers=headers, json=payload)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_network_unavailable", retryable=True) from exc
        status = int(response.status_code)
        if status == 200:
            provider_id = response.headers.get("apns-id")
            return ProviderAcceptance(provider_message_id=provider_id)
        if status in {429, 500, 502, 503, 504}:
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_provider_temporarily_unavailable", retryable=True)
        # Never persist or return the raw provider body; it can contain diagnostic data.
        if status == 410:
            try:
                self.target_repository.invalidate_voip_token_if_version_matches(
                    device_id=target.device_id,
                    token_version=target.token_version,
                )
            except Exception:
                # Do not mask the provider response; every attempt re-resolves the target.
                pass
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_voip_token_unregistered", retryable=False)
        if status in {401, 403}:
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_provider_auth_rejected", retryable=False)
        raise InvitationDeliveryError("apns_request_rejected", retryable=False)

    def close(self) -> None:
        if self._owns_client:
            self.http_client.close()


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class FcmCallTarget:
    device_id: str
    user_id: str
    firebase_installation_id: str
    installation_version: str
    active: bool
    trusted: bool
    call_enabled: bool
    call_consent: bool


class FcmCallTargetRepository(Protocol):
    def resolve_current_authorized_call_target(
        self, *, user_id: str, device_id: str,
    ) -> FcmCallTarget | None:
        """Resolve current FID with account, trusted-device and call-consent checks."""
        ...

    def invalidate_fid_if_version_matches(self, *, device_id: str, installation_version: str) -> bool:
        ...


class FcmCallWakeTransport:
    """High-priority FCM wake-up for Android; native app must present Telecom UI."""

    def __init__(self, *, project_id: str, firebase_app: Any,
                 target_repository: FcmCallTargetRepository, messaging_api: Any | None = None):
        if not isinstance(project_id, str) or not re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9-]{4,128}", project_id):
            raise ValueError("A valid Firebase project ID is required")
        if firebase_app is None:
            raise ValueError("A configured Firebase Admin app is required")
        self.project_id = project_id
        self.firebase_app = firebase_app
        self.target_repository = target_repository
        if messaging_api is None:
            try:
                from firebase_admin import messaging as messaging_api
            except ImportError as exc:
                raise RuntimeError("Android call wake requires firebase-admin>=7.5.0") from exc
        self.messaging = messaging_api

    @classmethod
    def from_environment(cls, *, target_repository: FcmCallTargetRepository) -> "FcmCallWakeTransport":
        project_id = os.environ.get("AARIYA_FCM_PROJECT_ID", "").strip()
        if not project_id:
            raise RuntimeError("AARIYA_FCM_PROJECT_ID is required")
        try:
            import firebase_admin
            from firebase_admin import credentials
        except ImportError as exc:
            raise RuntimeError("Android call wake requires firebase-admin>=7.5.0") from exc
        app_name = "aariya-call-wake"
        try:
            app = firebase_admin.get_app(app_name)
        except ValueError:
            app = firebase_admin.initialize_app(
                credential=credentials.ApplicationDefault(),
                options={"projectId": project_id}, name=app_name,
            )
        configured_project = getattr(app, "project_id", None)
        if configured_project and configured_project != project_id:
            raise RuntimeError("Firebase Admin app uses a different project ID")
        return cls(project_id=project_id, firebase_app=app, target_repository=target_repository)

    def send(self, envelope: InvitationEnvelope, *, idempotency_key: str) -> ProviderAcceptance:
        if envelope.platform != "android_fcm_wake":
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("fcm_wrong_platform", retryable=False)
        try:
            target = self.target_repository.resolve_current_authorized_call_target(
                user_id=envelope.user_id, device_id=envelope.device_id,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("fcm_call_target_lookup_failed", retryable=True) from exc
        if target is None or (
            target.user_id != envelope.user_id
            or target.device_id != envelope.device_id
            or not target.firebase_installation_id.strip()
            or not target.installation_version.strip()
            or not target.active or not target.trusted
            or not target.call_enabled or not target.call_consent
        ):
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("fcm_call_target_not_authorized", retryable=False)
        try:
            message = self.messaging.Message(
                data={
                    "type": "aariya_incoming_call",
                    "invitation_id": envelope.invitation_id,
                    "call_id": envelope.call_id,
                    "expires_at": envelope.expires_at,
                },
                fid=target.firebase_installation_id,
                android=self.messaging.AndroidConfig(
                    priority="high",
                    ttl=0,
                    collapse_key=f"aariya-call-{envelope.call_id[:48]}",
                ),
            )
            message_id = self.messaging.send(message, app=self.firebase_app)
        except Exception as exc:
            name = type(exc).__name__
            if name == "UnregisteredError":
                try:
                    self.target_repository.invalidate_fid_if_version_matches(
                        device_id=target.device_id,
                        installation_version=target.installation_version,
                    )
                except Exception:
                    pass
                raise InvitationDeliveryError("fcm_fid_unregistered", retryable=False) from exc
            if name in {"UnavailableError", "InternalError", "QuotaExceededError", "DeadlineExceededError"}:
                raise InvitationDeliveryError("fcm_provider_temporarily_unavailable", retryable=True) from exc
            if name in {"SenderIdMismatchError", "ThirdPartyAuthError", "InvalidArgumentError"}:
                raise InvitationDeliveryError("fcm_call_request_rejected", retryable=False) from exc
            raise InvitationDeliveryError("fcm_provider_error", retryable=True) from exc
        return ProviderAcceptance(provider_message_id=message_id)


"""Authenticated app-receipt/status endpoints for call invitations.

The caller must inject the same trusted user+device authentication dependency
used by the rest of Aariya. Do not expose these routes to unauthenticated
internet callers or infer device identity from request data.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Callable

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field

from .call_invitation_delivery import (
    InvitationConflict,
    InvitationNotFound,
    InvitationRecord,
    InvitationStore,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class AuthenticatedInvitationDevice:
    user_id: str
    device_id: str


class AppReceiptRequest(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")
    receipt_id: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=512)


class InvitationStatusResponse(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")
    invitation_id: str
    call_id: str
    status: str
    expires_at: str
    attempts: int


def _response(row: InvitationRecord) -> InvitationStatusResponse:
    return InvitationStatusResponse(
        invitation_id=row.invitation_id,
        call_id=row.call_id,
        status=row.status,
        expires_at=row.expires_at,
        attempts=row.attempts,
    )


def create_call_invitation_router(
    *, store: InvitationStore,
    authenticated_device_dependency: Callable,
) -> APIRouter:
    router = APIRouter(
        prefix="/api/proactive/v1/call-invitations",
        tags=["Aariya call invitation delivery"],
    )

    @router.post("/{invitation_id}/received", response_model=InvitationStatusResponse)
    def app_received(
        invitation_id: str,
        request: AppReceiptRequest,
        principal: AuthenticatedInvitationDevice = Depends(authenticated_device_dependency),
    ) -> InvitationStatusResponse:
        try:
            row, _duplicate = store.mark_app_received(
                invitation_id=invitation_id,
                user_id=principal.user_id,
                device_id=principal.device_id,
                receipt_id=request.receipt_id,
            )
            return _response(row)
        except InvitationNotFound as exc:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Call invitation not found") from exc
        except PermissionError as exc:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail="Call invitation access denied") from exc
        except InvitationConflict as exc:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=409, detail="Call invitation receipt rejected") from exc
        except ValueError as exc:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=422, detail="Invalid receipt") from exc

    @router.get("/{invitation_id}", response_model=InvitationStatusResponse)
    def get_status(
        invitation_id: str,
        principal: AuthenticatedInvitationDevice = Depends(authenticated_device_dependency),
    ) -> InvitationStatusResponse:
        try:
            row = store.get(invitation_id)
            if row.user_id != principal.user_id or row.device_id != principal.device_id:
                raise PermissionError("Wrong authenticated user/device")
            return _response(row)
        except InvitationNotFound as exc:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Call invitation not found") from exc
        except PermissionError as exc:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail="Call invitation access denied") from exc

    return router



1. Main implementation
Open the complete call_start_service.py source

The core workflow is:
result = call_start_service.start(    idempotency_key=approved_escalation.idempotency_key,    incident_id=approved_escalation.incident_id,    escalation_decision_id=approved_escalation.decision_id,    user_id=approved_escalation.user_id,    device_id=approved_escalation.device_id,    conversation_id=existing_aariya_conversation.id,    reason_code=approved_escalation.reason_code,    expires_at=approved_escalation.expires_at,)# The invitation is queued, not yet delivered.print(result.call.call_id)print(result.invitation.status)



approved_escalation and existing_aariya_conversation must come from Aariya’s real policy and conversation systems. They are integration points, not objects created by this overlay.
2. What the new module handles
Device and consent validation

Confirms that the registered user and device match, that the device is trusted and active, that incoming calls are enabled, and that the current consent allows the call. The existing call authorizer checks policy again before creating the request.


Crash recovery

CallStartRepairWorker finds unexpired calls with no invitation record and queues the missing invitation. The call and invitation stores must use the same database in this SQLite implementation.


Revoked-consent protection

If recovery discovers that the target is no longer authorized, it cancels the outstanding call instead of ringing the device. Temporary registry outages are retried.


Idempotency

Retries do not deliberately create a new call or invitation for the same operation. Existing delivery records remain authoritative.



3. Recovery worker
The worker can run under Aariya’s managed server or worker lifecycle:
await call_start_repair_worker.run_forever(    stop_event=shutdown_event,    poll_seconds=2.0,)



The worker needs to be instantiated with the real CallStore, InvitationStore, and trusted-device resolver. It should be supervised by the existing application lifecycle, with sanitized operational metrics and failure alerts.


Aariya call-start and invitation recovery
This is a source overlay intended to be integrated into the existing Aariya server. It is not a standalone application and must not create a separate account, consent, policy, conversation, or device registry.
Required shared services
- CallCoordinator backed by Aariya's call store and an authorizer that checks the current incident, escalation decision, consent, trusted device, and call policy.
- InvitationStore using the same file-backed SQLite database path as CallStore in this single-host overlay. For production multi-instance operation, migrate both tables to Aariya's shared DB and use a transactional outbox there.
- CallInvitationTargetResolver connected to the real trusted-device registry. It must return the current platform (ios_pushkit or android_fcm_wake) and current active/trusted/call-enabled/call-consent state. Never implement it with a hardcoded device or assumed consent.
- CallInvitationDispatcher with real APNs VoIP / FCM Android transports and a current call-state reader.
Start an approved call
result = call_start_service.start(
    idempotency_key=approved_escalation.idempotency_key,
    incident_id=approved_escalation.incident_id,
    escalation_decision_id=approved_escalation.decision_id,
    user_id=approved_escalation.user_id,
    device_id=approved_escalation.device_id,
    conversation_id=existing_aariya_conversation.id,
    reason_code=approved_escalation.reason_code,
    expires_at=approved_escalation.expires_at,
)
# `result.invitation` is queued. It is NOT proof of delivery.
# A supervised CallInvitationDispatcher sends it through the platform provider.
This method is an internal service, not an unauthenticated HTTP endpoint. The source of approved_escalation must be Aariya's actual policy/escalation decision store. It must not be created from a client-supplied incident payload alone.
Crash recovery worker
Construct CallStartRepairWorker with the same call/invitation DB and trusted-device resolver. Run await worker.run_forever(stop_event=shutdown_event) inside Aariya's managed worker lifecycle, or schedule run_once() under the existing supervisor. The worker finds only unexpired requested/ringing calls with no invitation row, resolves current consent/trust again, and enqueues the missing invitation. It does not recreate invitations for records already owned by the delivery outbox.

A device that is no longer authorized is not rung: the outstanding call is marked cancelled. A registry/storage outage is treated as transient so the worker retries. Call IDs, incident data, conversations, tokens, and arbitrary provider exception text are not logged by this module.
Delivery semantics
- pending / retry_wait means queued for transport.
- provider_accepted means APNs/FCM accepted the send request.
- app_received means the authenticated assigned device reported receipt.
- None of these states means a person saw, answered, or resolved anything.
- The accepted call still needs the existing Aariya voice runtime to join the LiveKit room through AariyaVoiceRuntimeCommandWorker. A separate model or TTS stack must not be created for the call.
Operations that remain project-specific
Register the routers in the existing FastAPI application lifespan, use the existing auth/session/ACL implementation, implement the target resolver from the real device registry, configure APNs and FCM, and ensure the supervised delivery and runtime workers start and stop with the service. Do not deploy with the isolated test authorizer or token issuer.

2. Connect it to Aariya’s server
The live server must construct these workers from its actual database, authorization system, delivery providers, and voice-runtime bridge. Once those dependencies exist, the integration looks like this:
from contextlib import asynccontextmanagerfrom fastapi import FastAPIfrom server.proactive.call_worker_specs import build_call_worker_specsfrom server.proactive.worker_supervisor import ProactiveWorkerSupervisor# These must be Aariya's real production dependencies.specs = build_call_worker_specs(    instance_id=deployment_instance_id,    call_store=real_call_store,    repair_worker=real_call_start_repair_worker,    invitation_dispatcher=real_call_invitation_dispatcher,    runtime_worker=real_voice_runtime_command_worker,)supervisor = ProactiveWorkerSupervisor(specs)@asynccontextmanagerasync def lifespan(app: FastAPI):    await supervisor.start()    app.state.proactive_worker_supervisor = supervisor    try:        yield    finally:        await supervisor.stop()app = FastAPI(lifespan=lifespan)



Important: merge this into Aariya’s existing lifespan instead of replacing it if the application already has startup and shutdown handlers.
3. What happens when a worker fails?
1. The supervisor records a sanitized failure status without exposing private exception details.
2. It retries using bounded exponential backoff rather than repeatedly hammering an unavailable service.
3. A worker becomes healthy again after a successful iteration. Its durable queue allows pending work to resume where supported.
4. On application shutdown, the supervisor signals the workers to stop and waits for them to exit.
A healthy worker loop does not prove that a push reached a phone or that an AI call is working. Those require separate delivery receipts and end-to-end checks.
What remains before Aariya is fully integrated
The overlay now has the call lifecycle, invitation delivery, call-start recovery, runtime command processing, and unified worker supervision. However, the live Aariya application is not yet fully connected to these components. Its actual server, Flutter source, authentication service, trusted-device registry, shared database/migrations, and existing voice-runtime implementation still need to be integrated and tested together.

The next critical step is to integrate this with the real Aariya repository and implement startup readiness checks that block unsafe startup when required capabilities, dependencies, or voice-runtime components are unavailable. That should connect to the existing startup commands rather than create a parallel launcher.


"""Structured supervision for Aariya proactive workers.

This module does not create repositories, credentials, user identities, or
provider adapters. The live application must construct each worker with its
real dependencies and supply the resulting callables here.

Synchronous workers run in a thread so provider/database operations do not
block the event loop. Failures are deliberately logged with sanitized error
codes only; raw exceptions may contain credentials, personal data, or payloads.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import inspect
import json
import logging
import re
import time
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Any, Awaitable, Callable, Mapping

logger = logging.getLogger("aariya.proactive.worker_supervisor")
_SAFE_NAME = re.compile(r"^[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]{0,63}$")

WorkerCallable = Callable[[], Any | Awaitable[Any]]


def _utc_now() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="milliseconds")


def _safe_log(event: str, **fields: object) -> None:
    logger.info(json.dumps(
        {"component": "proactive_worker_supervisor", "event": event,
         "at": _utc_now(), **fields},
        ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False, separators=(",", ":"),
    ))


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class WorkerSpec:
    name: str
    run_once: WorkerCallable
    interval_seconds: float = 1.0
    blocking: bool = True
    required: bool = True
    max_consecutive_failures: int = 5
    base_backoff_seconds: float = 0.5
    max_backoff_seconds: float = 30.0

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not _SAFE_NAME.fullmatch(self.name):
            raise ValueError("worker name must be a safe identifier")
        if not callable(self.run_once):
            raise ValueError("run_once must be callable")
        if not 0.05 <= self.interval_seconds <= 3600:
            raise ValueError("interval_seconds must be between 0.05 and 3600")
        if not 1 <= self.max_consecutive_failures <= 1000:
            raise ValueError("max_consecutive_failures must be between 1 and 1000")
        if not 0.05 <= self.base_backoff_seconds <= self.max_backoff_seconds <= 3600:
            raise ValueError("invalid worker backoff bounds")


@dataclass(slots=True)
class WorkerRuntimeState:
    name: str
    required: bool
    status: str = "starting"
    iterations: int = 0
    consecutive_failures: int = 0
    total_failures: int = 0
    last_started_at: str | None = None
    last_succeeded_at: str | None = None
    last_failed_at: str | None = None
    last_duration_ms: int | None = None
    last_error_code: str | None = None
    _last_result_type: str | None = field(default=None, repr=False)

    def public_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]:
        return {
            "name": self.name,
            "required": self.required,
            "status": self.status,
            "iterations": self.iterations,
            "consecutive_failures": self.consecutive_failures,
            "total_failures": self.total_failures,
            "last_started_at": self.last_started_at,
            "last_succeeded_at": self.last_succeeded_at,
            "last_failed_at": self.last_failed_at,
            "last_duration_ms": self.last_duration_ms,
            "last_error_code": self.last_error_code,
        }


class WorkerSupervisorError(RuntimeError):
    pass


class ProactiveWorkerSupervisor:
    """Run and supervise a fixed, trusted set of background workers.

    `ready` becomes true only after every required worker has completed at
    least one iteration successfully. After repeated failures the supervisor
    marks a worker failed but continues bounded-backoff retries, allowing
    transient infrastructure outages to recover without a process restart.
    """

    def __init__(self, specs: tuple[WorkerSpec, ...] | list[WorkerSpec], *,
                 shutdown_timeout_seconds: float = 10.0):
        if not specs:
            raise ValueError("at least one worker spec is required")
        names = [spec.name for spec in specs]
        if len(names) != len(set(names)):
            raise ValueError("worker names must be unique")
        if not 0.1 <= shutdown_timeout_seconds <= 300:
            raise ValueError("shutdown timeout must be between 0.1 and 300")
        self._specs = tuple(specs)
        self._states = {
            spec.name: WorkerRuntimeState(spec.name, spec.required)
            for spec in self._specs
        }
        self._shutdown_timeout_seconds = shutdown_timeout_seconds
        self._stop_event = asyncio.Event()
        self._tasks: dict[str, asyncio.Task[None]] = {}
        self._started = False
        self._stopping = False
        self._state_changed = asyncio.Condition()

    @property
    def ready(self) -> bool:
        if not self._started or self._stopping:
            return False
        required = [state for state in self._states.values() if state.required]
        return bool(required) and all(state.status == "healthy" for state in required)

    @property
    def status(self) -> str:
        if not self._started:
            return "stopped"
        if self._stopping:
            return "stopping"
        required = [state for state in self._states.values() if state.required]
        if any(state.status == "failed" for state in required):
            return "failed"
        if any(state.status in {"starting", "degraded"} for state in required):
            return "starting" if all(s.status == "starting" for s in required) else "degraded"
        if required and all(state.status == "healthy" for state in required):
            return "ready"
        return "degraded"

    def snapshot(self) -> dict[str, object]:
        """Return sanitized operational state; never includes worker results or errors."""
        return {
            "status": self.status,
            "ready": self.ready,
            "started": self._started,
            "stopping": self._stopping,
            "workers": {name: state.public_dict() for name, state in self._states.items()},
        }

    async def start(self) -> None:
        if self._started:
            raise WorkerSupervisorError("supervisor already started")
        self._started = True
        self._stopping = False
        self._stop_event.clear()
        _safe_log("supervisor_started", worker_count=len(self._specs))
        for spec in self._specs:
            self._tasks[spec.name] = asyncio.create_task(
                self._run_worker(spec), name=f"aariya-proactive:{spec.name}"
            )

    async def stop(self) -> None:
        if not self._started or self._stopping:
            return
        self._stopping = True
        self._stop_event.set()
        tasks = list(self._tasks.values())
        if tasks:
            done, pending = await asyncio.wait(
                tasks, timeout=self._shutdown_timeout_seconds,
            )
            for task in pending:
                task.cancel()
            if pending:
                await asyncio.gather(*pending, return_exceptions=True)
        self._tasks.clear()
        for state in self._states.values():
            state.status = "stopped"
        _safe_log("supervisor_stopped", worker_count=len(tasks))
        self._started = False
        self._stopping = False

    async def wait_until_ready(self, *, timeout_seconds: float = 10.0) -> bool:
        if not 0.1 <= timeout_seconds <= 300:
            raise ValueError("readiness timeout must be between 0.1 and 300")
        deadline = asyncio.get_running_loop().time() + timeout_seconds
        while not self.ready:
            if self.status in {"failed", "stopped"}:
                return False
            remaining = deadline - asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
            if remaining <= 0:
                return False
            await asyncio.sleep(min(0.05, remaining))
        return True

    async def _invoke(self, spec: WorkerSpec) -> Any:
        if spec.blocking:
            result = await asyncio.to_thread(spec.run_once)
        else:
            result = spec.run_once()
            if inspect.isawaitable(result):
                result = await result
        # A non-blocking adapter must not accidentally return a coroutine
        # without it being awaited (for example a callable returning coroutine).
        if inspect.isawaitable(result):
            result = await result
        return result

    async def _run_worker(self, spec: WorkerSpec) -> None:
        state = self._states[spec.name]
        while not self._stop_event.is_set():
            state.last_started_at = _utc_now()
            started = time.monotonic()
            try:
                result = await self._invoke(spec)
                state.iterations += 1
                state.consecutive_failures = 0
                state.last_error_code = None
                state.last_succeeded_at = _utc_now()
                state.last_duration_ms = max(0, int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000))
                state.status = "healthy"
                state._last_result_type = type(result).__name__ if result is not None else None
                _safe_log("worker_iteration_succeeded", worker=spec.name,
                          duration_ms=state.last_duration_ms, iteration=state.iterations)
                delay = spec.interval_seconds
            except asyncio.CancelledError:
                raise
            except Exception:
                state.iterations += 1
                state.consecutive_failures += 1
                state.total_failures += 1
                state.last_failed_at = _utc_now()
                state.last_duration_ms = max(0, int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000))
                state.last_error_code = "worker_iteration_failed"
                state.status = (
                    "failed" if state.consecutive_failures >= spec.max_consecutive_failures
                    else "degraded"
                )
                _safe_log(
                    "worker_iteration_failed", worker=spec.name,
                    duration_ms=state.last_duration_ms,
                    consecutive_failures=state.consecutive_failures,
                    status=state.status, error_code="worker_iteration_failed",
                )
                delay = min(
                    spec.max_backoff_seconds,
                    spec.base_backoff_seconds * (2 ** min(20, state.consecutive_failures - 1)),
                )
            try:
                await asyncio.wait_for(self._stop_event.wait(), timeout=delay)
            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                continue


@asynccontextmanager
async def supervised_proactive_workers(supervisor: ProactiveWorkerSupervisor):
    """Context manager suitable for use inside the existing FastAPI lifespan."""
    await supervisor.start()
    try:
        yield supervisor
    finally:
        await supervisor.stop()


"""Compose the call subsystem's durable workers for the common supervisor.

The caller constructs all dependencies from Aariya's real repositories,
configuration and providers. This helper does not load credentials or create
user/device/consent/policy records.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from .call_invitation_delivery import CallInvitationDispatcher
from .call_lifecycle import CallStore
from .call_runtime_worker import AariyaVoiceRuntimeCommandWorker
from .call_start_service import CallStartRepairWorker
from .worker_supervisor import WorkerSpec


def build_call_worker_specs(
    *,
    instance_id: str,
    call_store: CallStore,
    repair_worker: CallStartRepairWorker,
    invitation_dispatcher: CallInvitationDispatcher,
    runtime_worker: AariyaVoiceRuntimeCommandWorker,
    expiry_interval_seconds: float = 1.0,
    repair_interval_seconds: float = 2.0,
    delivery_interval_seconds: float = 0.5,
    runtime_interval_seconds: float = 0.5,
    batch_size: int = 50,
) -> tuple[WorkerSpec, ...]:
    """Create worker definitions for expiry, recovery, invitation and runtime.

    `instance_id` must be a unique stable process ID supplied by deployment
    configuration/hosting. It is used only for queue lease ownership.
    """
    if not instance_id.strip() or len(instance_id) > 128:
        raise ValueError("instance_id must be a non-empty deployment identifier")
    if not 1 <= batch_size <= 1000:
        raise ValueError("batch_size must be between 1 and 1000")

    def expire_due() -> int:
        return call_store.expire_due(limit=batch_size)

    def repair_due() -> Any:
        return repair_worker.run_once(limit=batch_size)

    def dispatch_invitations() -> Any:
        return invitation_dispatcher.dispatch_once(
            worker_id=f"{instance_id}:call-invitations", limit=min(batch_size, 100),
        )

    async def process_runtime_commands() -> Any:
        return await runtime_worker.run_once(
            worker_id=f"{instance_id}:call-runtime", limit=min(batch_size, 100),
        )

    return (
        WorkerSpec("call_expiry", expire_due, expiry_interval_seconds, blocking=True),
        WorkerSpec("call_start_repair", repair_due, repair_interval_seconds, blocking=True),
        WorkerSpec("call_invitation_delivery", dispatch_invitations, delivery_interval_seconds, blocking=True),
        WorkerSpec("call_voice_runtime", process_runtime_commands, runtime_interval_seconds, blocking=False),
    )


Aariya proactive call worker supervision
This module is an additive integration seam. It does not create a standalone app and must be connected inside the live Aariya FastAPI lifespan with Aariya's real database, device/consent resolver, APNs/FCM transports, CallAuthorizer, token issuer, and existing voice-runtime bridge.
Components supervised
build_call_worker_specs() composes four durable workers:
1. call_expiry: expires unanswered requests using the authoritative call store.
2. call_start_repair: repairs call records that have no invitation outbox record after a crash.
3. call_invitation_delivery: delivers due invitations through the configured platform provider and records provider acceptance separately from device receipt.
4. call_voice_runtime: processes accepted-call attach and terminal-call detach commands through the injected adapter to the existing Aariya voice/conversation runtime.
ProactiveWorkerSupervisor starts all loops together, keeps per-worker sanitized state, applies bounded exponential backoff after errors, and stops workers during application shutdown. It never logs raw exceptions, worker return values, tokens, call IDs, or payload contents. Readiness only becomes true after each required worker has completed one successful iteration; this confirms the worker loop itself is running, not that a phone, provider, or AI voice session has succeeded.
Integration snippet
The names below represent the real service objects that the live Aariya application must construct; this overlay cannot infer those implementations from the integration seam.
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from fastapi import FastAPI

from server.proactive.call_worker_specs import build_call_worker_specs
from server.proactive.worker_supervisor import ProactiveWorkerSupervisor

# Build these from Aariya's real production repositories and configuration.
# Do not use an isolated test authorizer, test token issuer, or fake registry.
call_worker_specs = build_call_worker_specs(
    instance_id=deployment_instance_id,  # unique per process/instance
    call_store=real_call_store,
    repair_worker=real_call_start_repair_worker,
    invitation_dispatcher=real_call_invitation_dispatcher,
    runtime_worker=real_voice_runtime_command_worker,
)
worker_supervisor = ProactiveWorkerSupervisor(call_worker_specs)

@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app: FastAPI):
    await worker_supervisor.start()
    app.state.proactive_worker_supervisor = worker_supervisor
    try:
        yield
    finally:
        await worker_supervisor.stop()

app = FastAPI(lifespan=lifespan)
If Aariya already has a FastAPI lifespan, merge the start() and stop() calls into it instead of replacing the current lifecycle. Do not create two competing lifespans. Only expose the supervisor snapshot through a health/admin endpoint if that endpoint is protected using the existing operational authorization policy.
Deployment cautions
- The call store, invitation outbox, and runtime-command outbox must use the same durable/shared database and migrations for the SQLite overlay; SQLite is a single-host foundation, not a multi-instance production database.
- instance_id must be unique across concurrently running processes to avoid lease-ownership ambiguity.
- Provider SDK/network operations need explicit connection/read timeouts; cancelling a Python to_thread() task cannot forcibly terminate its underlying thread.
- All call-start requests must still pass the real current policy/incident/device/consent authorization. Client input must never directly manufacture an approved escalation.
- Worker readiness is not a notification receipt, person acknowledgement, call answer, LiveKit connection, or proof that the AI runtime is operational.
- Run the native iOS/Android and existing Aariya voice-runtime integration tests before enabling real autonomous calls.

1. The new readiness API
The main file is:

Open readiness_api.py

It provides:

GET /health/proactive/ready

The endpoint returns HTTP 200 only when all required readiness checks pass and the actual worker supervisor reports ready. Otherwise, it returns HTTP 503.

The required contracts cover:
Check	What it must verify
event_store	Real incident/event storage is accessible
source_auth	Real monitoring-source authorization is available
policy_store	The approved policy service can be accessed
device_registry	The real trusted-device registry is available
notification_transport	The configured notification transport is ready
call_store	The actual call lifecycle store is accessible
invitation_transport	The configured incoming-call transport is ready
voice_runtime	Aariya’s existing conversation/voice runtime is available
workers	Required background workers have completed a successful iteration

These checks must use actual production adapters. The implementation deliberately refuses to treat missing probes, arbitrary truthy return values, timeouts, or exception messages as evidence of readiness.
Connect it to Aariya’s existing FastAPI app
Once the real adapters exist, register the router inside the existing app. Do not create a second FastAPI application.

2. Make readiness a startup requirement
I extended the separate startup-integrity patch to support three settings:
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_HEALTH_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000/health/proactive/ready
AARIYA_REQUIRE_PROACTIVE_HEALTH=1
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_READY_TIMEOUT_MS=60000


The URL is an example for a local backend on port 8000. Use it only after you have registered the readiness route and connected its real health checks.

With AARIYA_REQUIRE_PROACTIVE_HEALTH=1, the startup behavior is:
Preflight

Check configuration, launch ownership, source integrity, and known capability contracts.


Required endpoint configured

Check it during preflight when it is already expected to be available.


Backend being launched

Defer the local health probe until the backend is listening, rather than falsely passing it early.




Post-start verification

Wait for an actual HTTP 2xx response. Stop or report failure if required readiness cannot be established.


The updated launch paths enforce this policy for the combined development launcher, brain-only launcher, persistent backend start, and the applicable UI/GUI preflight paths. The runtime monitor also tracks the configured proactive health endpoint.
