"""Fail-closed readiness contract for Aariya's proactive subsystem.

This endpoint is intentionally dependency-injected. It does not infer that a
module is ready because it exists or imports successfully. The live application
must provide health probes backed by its real stores, authorization services,
provider adapters, and existing Aariya voice runtime.

The public response contains no credentials, user IDs, incident data, raw
exception messages, or provider payloads. Configure dependency-level timeouts:
Python cannot forcibly stop a synchronous function already running in a thread.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import inspect
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Any, Callable, Iterable
from collections.abc import Mapping

from fastapi import APIRouter
from fastapi.responses import JSONResponse

_SAFE_CHECK_ID = re.compile(r"^[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]{0,63}$")

# Required contracts for the full proactive event + contact capability.
# These are interface names, not default/fabricated health results.
REQUIRED_COMPONENTS = frozenset({
    "event_store",
    "source_auth",
    "policy_store",
    "device_registry",
    "notification_transport",
    "call_store",
    "invitation_transport",
    "voice_runtime",
})

HealthCheck = Callable[[], Any]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ProbeSpec:
    """A single real health probe supplied by the running application."""

    name: str
    check: HealthCheck
    timeout_seconds: float = 2.0
    required: bool = True

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not _SAFE_CHECK_ID.fullmatch(self.name):
            raise ValueError("probe name must be a safe lowercase identifier")
        if not callable(self.check):
            raise ValueError("probe check must be callable")
        if not 0.05 <= self.timeout_seconds <= 30.0:
            raise ValueError("probe timeout must be from 0.05 to 30 seconds")


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ProbeResult:
    name: str
    required: bool
    ready: bool
    status: str

    def public_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]:
        return {
            "name": self.name,
            "required": self.required,
            "status": self.status,
        }


class ProactiveReadinessGate:
    """Evaluate real subsystem contracts and the supervised worker bundle.

    All `REQUIRED_COMPONENTS` must have a configured probe. A missing probe is
    a construction error rather than being treated as healthy. A required
    probe passes only when its actual runtime check returns True or a mapping
    with `ready: True`. Truthy arbitrary values do not count as readiness.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        probes: Iterable[ProbeSpec],
        *,
        worker_supervisor: object,
        include_optional_probes: bool = True,
    ) -> None:
        probe_list = tuple(probes)
        names = [probe.name for probe in probe_list]
        if len(names) != len(set(names)):
            raise ValueError("probe names must be unique")
        missing = REQUIRED_COMPONENTS.difference(names)
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(
                "missing required proactive readiness probes: "
                + ", ".join(sorted(missing))
            )
        if worker_supervisor is None:
            raise ValueError("the real proactive worker supervisor is required")
        self._probes = tuple(
            probe for probe in probe_list
            if include_optional_probes or probe.required
        )
        self._worker_supervisor = worker_supervisor

    async def _call_probe(self, probe: ProbeSpec) -> bool:
        async def invoke() -> Any:
            if inspect.iscoroutinefunction(probe.check):
                return await probe.check()
            result = await asyncio.to_thread(probe.check)
            if inspect.isawaitable(result):
                return await result
            return result

        result = await asyncio.wait_for(invoke(), timeout=probe.timeout_seconds)
        if result is True:
            return True
        if isinstance(result, Mapping) and result.get("ready") is True:
            return True
        return False

    async def evaluate(self) -> dict[str, object]:
        async def evaluate_probe(probe: ProbeSpec) -> ProbeResult:
            try:
                ready = await self._call_probe(probe)
            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                ready = False
                status = "timeout"
            except Exception:
                # Do not disclose exception content; it can contain secrets.
                ready = False
                status = "error"
            else:
                status = "ready" if ready else "not_ready"
            return ProbeResult(probe.name, probe.required, ready, status)

        results = list(await asyncio.gather(
            *(evaluate_probe(probe) for probe in self._probes)
        ))
        worker_ready = getattr(self._worker_supervisor, "ready", False)
        try:
            if callable(worker_ready):
                worker_ready = worker_ready()
            if inspect.isawaitable(worker_ready):
                worker_ready = await worker_ready
            worker_ok = worker_ready is True
        except Exception:
            worker_ok = False

        results.append(ProbeResult(
            name="workers",
            required=True,
            ready=worker_ok,
            status="ready" if worker_ok else "not_ready",
        ))

        failed_required = sorted(
            result.name for result in results
            if result.required and not result.ready
        )
        has_optional_degradation = any(
            not result.required and not result.ready for result in results
        )
        ready = not failed_required
        return {
            "status": "ready" if ready else "not_ready",
            "checked_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
            "failed_checks": failed_required,
            "degraded": has_optional_degradation,
            "checks": [result.public_dict() for result in sorted(results, key=lambda r: r.name)],
        }


def create_proactive_readiness_router(gate: ProactiveReadinessGate) -> APIRouter:
    """Create `/health/proactive/ready`; return 503 until all required checks pass."""
    if not isinstance(gate, ProactiveReadinessGate):
        raise TypeError("gate must be a ProactiveReadinessGate configured with real adapters")
    router = APIRouter()

    @router.get("/health/proactive/ready", include_in_schema=False)
    async def proactive_readiness() -> JSONResponse:
        report = await gate.evaluate()
        code = 200 if report["status"] == "ready" else 503
        return JSONResponse(content=report, status_code=code, headers={"Cache-Control": "no-store"})

    return router


Aariya Proactive Event Foundation — integration overlay
This is an additive backend slice for the existing Aariya FastAPI application. It contains no demo events, fake provider, canned notification, default credentials, fabricated confidence, or synthetic runtime observations.
Included real behavior
- Authenticated event ingestion. source_id is derived from a configured source token, never trusted from the request body.
- Durable persistence to an explicitly configured SQLite database file.
- Per-source event-type allow-lists, so a task monitor cannot claim a security event unless that exact capability was explicitly granted.
- Event idempotency using (authenticated source ID, source_event_id).
- Incident creation and same-source correlation only when the real source supplies a correlation_key.
- Event history and evidence references retained with the incident.
- Authenticated list/detail endpoints plus audited acknowledge/resolve transitions.
- Strict input validation, bounded payload size, unique source tokens, UTC normalization for actual source timestamps, and failure-closed configuration.
Not yet included
Event ingestion itself does not send push messages or initiate calls; the wider additive overlay now has separate provider/call lifecycle modules, but they remain unconnected to the live service. This source snapshot does not contain the existing Flutter app source, authenticated mobile-session code, the current Aariya user identity system, the real event producers/watchers, the shared production database abstraction, or provider credentials. Those must be connected to actual project code before delivery and calling can be enabled. It also does not execute corrective actions or grant action authorization based on an LLM.
Add to the actual project
Copy server/proactive/ into the existing repository's server/proactive/ directory. Preserve the repository's current files.

In the existing server/main.py, after the FastAPI app is created and before the module finishes loading, include this router:
from server.proactive.router import router as proactive_router
app.include_router(proactive_router)
Do not create a second FastAPI app. Apply the import/include only after checking the existing app factory/lifespan and import conventions. The source tree was not available when this overlay was prepared, so server/main.py is deliberately not overwritten by a guessed version.
Proactive readiness endpoint for startup gating
server/proactive/readiness_api.py supplies a readiness router factory. Register it in the existing FastAPI app only after constructing the real health probes and the actual ProactiveWorkerSupervisor. It deliberately fails closed if any of these required contracts are omitted: event_store, source_auth, policy_store, device_registry, notification_transport, call_store, invitation_transport, and voice_runtime. The supervisor itself must report ready after the required workers successfully complete an iteration.

The probe lambdas must execute safe operations against the actual services (for example, a database connectivity query, an authorization-store read, provider credential/config validation, or a health check on the existing voice runtime). They must never enqueue a test event, send a real notification as a health check, call a user, create synthetic incidents, or infer readiness from an import. Use adapter-level network/database timeouts. The endpoint returns HTTP 503 until every required probe passes and does not disclose raw exception messages.

Example shape—the existing_* names are integration points, not claimed symbols from the unavailable live repository:
from server.proactive.readiness_api import (
    ProbeSpec, ProactiveReadinessGate, create_proactive_readiness_router,
)

readiness = ProactiveReadinessGate(
    probes=[
        ProbeSpec("event_store", existing_event_store.health_check),
        ProbeSpec("source_auth", existing_source_auth.health_check),
        ProbeSpec("policy_store", existing_policy_store.health_check),
        ProbeSpec("device_registry", existing_device_registry.health_check),
        ProbeSpec("notification_transport", existing_notification_transport.health_check),
        ProbeSpec("call_store", existing_call_store.health_check),
        ProbeSpec("invitation_transport", existing_invitation_transport.health_check),
        ProbeSpec("voice_runtime", existing_aariya_voice_runtime.health_check),
    ],
    worker_supervisor=existing_proactive_worker_supervisor,
)
app.include_router(create_proactive_readiness_router(readiness))
Only merge this into the existing app and lifespan. Once it is genuinely wired, configure AARIYA_PROACTIVE_HEALTH_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000/health/proactive/ready, then set AARIYA_REQUIRE_PROACTIVE_HEALTH=1 to make the startup-integrity overlay block unsafe launches. The capability manifest under capabilities/ registers the endpoint with startup discovery. Do not set the requirement flag before the endpoint returns real readiness results; that should intentionally block startup.
Required configuration
Configure these variables using the project's real secret manager or local protected environment file. Do not commit their values.
- AARIYA_PROACTIVE_DB_PATH: absolute path for the persistent event database file.
- AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_TOKEN: a random token of at least 32 characters for the single configured user identity used by this initial slice.
- AARIYA_PROACTIVE_USER_ID: the real user/account identifier to be written in the audit trail.
- AARIYA_PROACTIVE_SOURCE_TOKENS_JSON: JSON object mapping each actual source ID to an object with token (a unique random secret of at least 32 characters), event_types (a non-empty explicit allow-list), and optionally heartbeat_interval_seconds (an explicit integer interval for source liveness). Only configure sources that really exist and are controlled by Aariya. A source's event-type permission is denied by default unless configured here. The optional heartbeat_interval_seconds field is required to classify a source as healthy or stale; omit it only when its reporting cadence is intentionally not monitored.
Generate secrets with the system cryptographic random generator, e.g. python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(48))". Generate a separate token for the user and for each source. Do not reuse authentication tokens from other services.

The application returns 503 for this feature while required configuration is missing; it does not fall back to open access or sample credentials.
Endpoints
All paths below are relative to /api/proactive/v1.
- POST /events — source-token authenticated. source_event_id, event_type, severity, title, and summary must come from a real, authorized source, and the source must be explicitly allow-listed for the submitted event type. Supported event types are system_failure, task_failure, critical_event, deadline, security_event, autonomous_discovery, recovery, task_completed, and the internal monitoring_source_health type. Only the health-evaluator's internal event source should be able to emit monitoring_source_health; do not grant it to ordinary monitors. occurred_at, confidence, correlation_key, evidence_refs, and attributes are optional source-supplied facts; the service does not invent them.
- GET /incidents?state=open&limit=50 — user-token authenticated; state is optional.
- GET /incidents/{incident_id} — includes persisted events and audit history.
- POST /incidents/{incident_id}/acknowledge — audited transition from open to acknowledged.
- POST /incidents/{incident_id}/resolve — requires a non-empty, user-supplied resolution reason; audited.
Send Authorization: Bearer <configured token>. Do not include the bearer token in a URL, event body, evidence reference, or log. Do not place passwords, API keys, session cookies, private keys, or full conversation transcripts in summary or attributes; send minimum diagnostic facts and opaque references to evidence stored under the existing access-control system.
Storage and deployment boundary
SQLite is appropriate here only as a first durable local/single-host slice. Its file must live in a protected application-data location with filesystem permissions and encrypted disk/storage appropriate to the device. This implementation intentionally does not claim multi-instance consistency, cloud replication, mobile push, end-to-end encryption, or Guardian independence. Before multiple backend instances are deployed, move the schema and transaction logic to Aariya's real shared database/migration system instead of sharing a SQLite file across hosts.
Next implementation step
Once the actual backend and Flutter source are available, wire existing system/task monitors into POST /events, replace the initial single-user token guard with Aariya's current session/authorization dependency, connect an outbox to configured real push/VoIP providers, and add the Flutter incident thread and evidence routes. Do not enable escalation until those provider delivery acknowledgements and policy checks are implemented.
Optional real FCM HTTP v1 provider
The overlay includes server/proactive/fcm_http_v1.py and server/proactive/notification_worker.py. FCM HTTP v1 uses an authenticated request to https://fcm.googleapis.com/v1/projects/{project_id}/messages:send; this adapter uses the Google firebase.messaging OAuth scope and a device registration token resolved at dispatch time from a real repository. See the official Firebase send documentation and REST send reference.

Install the optional runtime dependency in the application's actual Python environment:
pip install -r requirements-proactive.txt
Supply the actual project ID and privacy-safe generic notification text via the protected runtime environment. Credentials should use Google Application Default Credentials; for a local/on-prem host, configure GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS to a protected service-account file path, and never commit that key file.

Required variables for python -m server.proactive.notification_worker:
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_DB_PATH=
AARIYA_FCM_PROJECT_ID=
AARIYA_FCM_NOTIFICATION_TITLE=
AARIYA_FCM_NOTIFICATION_BODY=
AARIYA_FCM_HTTP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=
AARIYA_PUSH_INSTALLATION_REPOSITORY_FACTORY=
AARIYA_NOTIFICATION_ACTIONABILITY_FACTORY=
AARIYA_NOTIFICATION_WORKER_POLL_SECONDS=
AARIYA_NOTIFICATION_WORKER_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES=
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_BATCH_SIZE=
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_LEASE_SECONDS=
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_BASE_SECONDS=
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_OUTBOX_RETRY_MAX_SECONDS=
The two factory variables must be trusted module:function references in the actual project. The token repository must re-check account ownership, device trust, token status, push permission, consent and incident access at send time, and must revoke a token conditionally by token version when FCM reports UNREGISTERED. The actionability service must read the current incident/policy state. Neither factory has a production default.

FCM API acceptance is recorded as provider_accepted, not as confirmed phone delivery. The authenticated Flutter app may report app_received through delivery_receipt_api.py; that receipt proves only that the authorized app reported receiving the payload. It does not prove notification display, user attention, acknowledgement, or incident resolution.

The FCM adapter has been unit-tested using isolated HTTP-response fixtures. It has not been tested against a live Firebase project because no project, credentials, or registered real device token were supplied in this environment.
FID-based device enrollment and Flutter wiring
Current Firebase documentation recommends Firebase Installation IDs (FIDs); registration-token APIs are deprecated. The worker uses fcm_admin_fid.py and requires firebase-admin>=7.5.0,<8. See Firebase Flutter FCM setup and Firebase Admin Python release notes.

The push-installation router requires all of the following from the real application:
- The existing authenticated device-session dependency; the user_id and device_id must be derived from that server-validated context, never from request JSON.
- A verified Firebase App Check/app-attestation dependency. Do not pass an unconditional lambda: True in production.
- An implementation of FidEnrollmentRepository using Aariya's real identity/device store. It must enforce current ownership/trust, server-side proactive-push consent, FID uniqueness, same-device confirmation, per-device rate limits, challenge expiry/one-time use, and audit logging.
- A FirebaseAdminFidChallengeSender using the initialized Firebase Admin app to send a short-lived data challenge to the submitted FID. Do not mark a submitted FID active until the authenticated device receives and confirms that challenge.
Example route registration in the existing FastAPI app, after constructing the real dependencies:
from server.proactive.push_installation_api import create_push_installation_router
from server.proactive.delivery_receipt_api import create_delivery_receipt_router

app.include_router(create_push_installation_router(
    authenticated_device_dependency=existing_authenticated_device_dependency,
    verified_app_attestation_dependency=existing_verified_app_check_dependency,
    enrollment_service=existing_fid_enrollment_service,
))

app.include_router(create_delivery_receipt_router(
    outbox=existing_notification_outbox,
    authenticated_device_dependency=existing_authenticated_device_dependency,
    authorization=existing_delivery_receipt_authorization,
))
All existing_* names above are integration points, not known symbols from the unavailable live repository. Implement them by adapting the current Aariya services; do not create parallel user/device data. The FID enrollment TTL must be explicitly configured and passed when constructing FidEnrollmentService.

In Flutter, initialize Firebase once in the existing application bootstrap. Create ProactivePushService only after the real user session is authenticated and the existing preference service says proactive notifications are enabled. Implement AariyaAuthenticatedApi in proactive_push_backend_adapter.dart with Aariya's current authenticated HTTP client, then pass the adapter to AariyaProactivePushBackendAdapter. Call refreshRegistration() from the existing app-resume handler if APNs registration was pending. Wire onOpenIncident to existing authenticated navigation and incident fetching; do not trust push data as full incident content.

The Flutter service currently reports app receipt while the app is foregrounded or when a notification is opened. It does not claim an OS-level delivery receipt while the app is fully terminated, and the app-received event is not a human acknowledgement.



{
  "$schema": "https://aariya.local/schemas/capability-manifest.schema.json",
  "id": "aariya-proactive-communication",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "description": "Proactive event ingestion, push notification, call invitation and existing Aariya voice-runtime bridge.",
  "entrypoint": "server/proactive/readiness_api.py",
  "criticality": "optional",
  "dependencies": [],
  "healthCheck": {
    "type": "http",
    "urlEnv": "AARIYA_PROACTIVE_HEALTH_URL"
  }
}




Startup Integrity Architecture and Operations
Goals
1. Produce live terminal events and JSONL/JSON diagnostics from actual checks.
2. Preflight the brain before npm run dev starts children and preflight Electron before opening its window.
3. Preserve process ownership: the combined launcher owns brain + Vite; the GUI starts neither; the persistent service remains launchd-managed.
4. Discover modules and configuration drift without executing untrusted discovered code.
5. Require real health contracts before declaring individual capabilities ready.
6. Avoid unsafe repair, duplicate launchers, secret leakage, and silent baseline trust.
Data flow
npm launch command
  -> profile-specific preflight
  -> config/runtime/dependency checks
  -> source scan + approved-baseline comparison
  -> capability-manifest validation
  -> dependency graph + ordered health probes
  -> policy decision (READY / DEGRADED / BLOCKED)
  -> launch only when policy allows
  -> runtime monitor emits state transitions
  -> .aariya/diagnostics/*.jsonl and *.json
The terminal renderer is fed by actual emitted events. It does not simulate progress percentages. npm run gui uses scripts/gui.mjs so the full CLI preflight runs once before Electron is spawned; Electron rechecks a successful HTTP response from the Vite page before creating the window. JSONL is append-only per run; a summary JSON is written when preflight completes or monitoring stops.
Capability manifest example
Create capabilities/memory.capability.json only after confirming that the entrypoint and health URL exist in the actual project:
{
  "id": "memory",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "description": "Aariya memory API readiness",
  "entrypoint": "server/main.py",
  "criticality": "core",
  "dependencies": [],
  "healthCheck": {
    "type": "http",
    "urlEnv": "AARIYA_MEMORY_HEALTH_URL"
  }
}
For an HTTP contract, the endpoint must return a successful HTTP status only when the specific capability is healthy. For TCP, configure hostEnv and portEnv; that proves socket reachability only. For python-import, use a dotted Python module name; that proves importability only. Do not define an always-200 endpoint that ignores internal dependency state.
Required proactive subsystem health gate
The proactive overlay supplies a fail-closed FastAPI readiness router at /health/proactive/ready. It returns HTTP 200 only when the real event store, source authorization, policy store, trusted device registry, notification transport, call store, invitation transport, existing Aariya voice-runtime bridge, and required supervised workers report ready. The probe functions must call Aariya's actual adapters; module imports, configuration names, or a listening port are not a substitute.

After the readiness router is registered inside the existing FastAPI app and its real probes are wired, configure the protected runtime environment (or an approved project .env file):
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_HEALTH_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000/health/proactive/ready
AARIYA_REQUIRE_PROACTIVE_HEALTH=1
AARIYA_PROACTIVE_READY_TIMEOUT_MS=60000
With AARIYA_REQUIRE_PROACTIVE_HEALTH=1, a missing URL or failing external endpoint blocks preflight. If the URL belongs to the local brain that the current launcher will start, preflight reports the check as deferred; npm run dev, npm run dev:brain, and npm run backend:start then wait for HTTP 200 and stop/block if the endpoint never becomes ready. npm run gui checks the existing backend endpoint before opening Electron. Runtime monitoring keeps probing the proactive endpoint and treats it as critical while required. Do not enable the flag until the route has been connected to the production app—an absent route should correctly prevent launch under this policy.
Dependency graph rules
- Missing dependency ID: core capability fails readiness; optional capability remains unverified/degraded.
- Dependency cycle: involved capabilities are not health-checked and cannot be marked ready.
- Dependency health not PASS: dependent capability is not health-checked.
- Entry points are resolved and checked against the project root; symlinks escaping the root are rejected.
- New modules are candidates only. Discovery does not register, import, execute or grant permissions to them.
Process locks
process-lock.mjs uses exclusive file creation to coordinate brain-8000, ui-5173 and backend-lifecycle. Locks include PID, command, timestamp and ownership token. A lock can only be removed by its owner during normal release. Stale/malformed locks block new launches and require operator verification; the checker does not automatically remove them because PID reuse or concurrent recovery could be unsafe.

The bash persistent-backend lifecycle uses the same backend-lifecycle.lock file name. status and health are read-only and do not acquire the lifecycle lock.
Approved integrity baseline
The first reviewed baseline is created by npm run startup:baseline:approve. It stores SHA-256 digests of tracked source/configuration file paths. It never stores source content or environment-variable values. Baseline comparison reports added, changed and removed files. The default is DEGRADED on drift; AARIYA_BLOCK_UNAPPROVED_DRIFT=1 turns missing/changed baseline into a blocking condition. Approving a baseline is a human review operation, not an automatic repair.
Safe repair registry
repair-registry.mjs contains an explicit allowlist. The built-in ensure-diagnostics-directory repair may create the local diagnostics directory if absent. It refuses symlinks, non-directory paths and paths resolving outside the project root. Arbitrary command execution, package installs, source rewrites, database resets and policy changes are not repairable through this adapter.
Runtime monitoring
npm run dev monitors the brain and UI after both ports become reachable. The monitor also probes configured provider and manifest endpoints and reports state transitions, not a repeated line every poll. For a persistent launchd brain, run npm run startup:monitor in a separate terminal. Ctrl+C stops monitoring, not the persistent service. npm run startup:monitor:dev can monitor brain/UI in a separate terminal.

Monitoring proves only the configured probes. A TCP listener is not equivalent to application readiness. Configure AARIYA_HEALTH_URL and capability-specific health contracts for real application state. During monitoring, the system rechecks the manually approved SHA-256 integrity baseline and reports unapproved drift without auto-approving it. Runtime dependency impacts are derived from registered capability contracts. Monitoring reports failures; it does not automatically restart unowned processes.
Current safe-repair scope
Built-in automated repair is intentionally narrow: ensure the diagnostics directory exists. Other recovery actions must be added one by one with documented preconditions, side effects, timeout, audit event, post-repair checks and rollback behavior. Until that is done, retries and diagnosis are enabled but general automatic repair is not.
Verification
Run npm run test:startup to exercise graph ordering/missing/cyclic dependency detection, process lock exclusivity/stale-lock handling, approved baseline drift and secret-content exclusion, safe repair allowlisting, and an end-to-end preflight where a failed core HTTP health contract blocks a dependent core capability.


#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

OVERLAY_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
TARGET_INPUT="${1:-}"
if [[ -z "$TARGET_INPUT" || ! -d "$TARGET_INPUT" ]]; then
  echo "Usage: bash apply-overlay.sh /absolute/or/relative/path/to/Aariya-repository" >&2
  exit 2
fi
ROOT="$(cd "$TARGET_INPUT" && pwd)"
if [[ ! -f "$ROOT/package.json" || ! -f "$ROOT/scripts/dev.mjs" || ! -f "$ROOT/scripts/dev-brain.mjs" || ! -f "$ROOT/scripts/backend.sh" || ! -f "$ROOT/main.js" ]]; then
  echo "Target does not look like the Aariya project supplied for this patch." >&2
  echo "Expected package.json, scripts/dev.mjs, scripts/dev-brain.mjs, scripts/backend.sh and main.js at the target root." >&2
  exit 2
fi

STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
BACKUP_DIR="$ROOT/.aariya/startup-integrity-backups/$STAMP"
mkdir -p "$BACKUP_DIR"

backup_if_present() {
  local rel="$1"
  if [[ -f "$ROOT/$rel" ]]; then
    mkdir -p "$BACKUP_DIR/$(dirname "$rel")"
    cp -p "$ROOT/$rel" "$BACKUP_DIR/$rel"
  fi
}

# Make reversible backups before replacing any existing project files.
for rel in \
  package.json \
  scripts/dev.mjs \
  scripts/dev-brain.mjs \
  scripts/backend.sh \
  main.js \
  scripts/gui.mjs \
  scripts/dev-ui.mjs \
  scripts/startup/engine.mjs \
  scripts/startup/preflight.mjs \
  scripts/startup/capability-manifest.schema.json \
  scripts/startup/dependency-graph.mjs \
  scripts/startup/process-lock.mjs \
  scripts/startup/process-inspector.mjs \
  scripts/startup/approved-baseline.mjs \
  scripts/startup/repair-registry.mjs \
  scripts/startup/baseline.mjs \
  scripts/startup/locks.mjs \
  scripts/startup/monitor.mjs \
  scripts/startup/await-proactive.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/dependency-graph.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/process-lock.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/process-inspector.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/http-readiness.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/security-config.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/approved-baseline.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/repair-registry.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/health-contracts.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/proactive-health.test.mjs \
  docs/startup-integrity.md; do
  backup_if_present "$rel"
done

# Merge only launch/check scripts into the project's current package.json.
# Existing dependencies, metadata and unrelated scripts are kept intact.
node - "$ROOT/package.json" "$OVERLAY_DIR/package.json" <<'NODE'
const fs = require('node:fs');
const [targetPath, overlayPath] = process.argv.slice(2);
const current = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(targetPath, 'utf8'));
const overlay = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(overlayPath, 'utf8'));
current.scripts ||= {};
for (const name of ['dev:ui', 'gui', 'startup:check', 'startup:check:brain', 'startup:check:ui', 'startup:check:gui', 'startup:check:persistent', 'startup:baseline:approve', 'startup:baseline:status', 'startup:locks', 'startup:monitor', 'startup:monitor:dev', 'test:startup']) {
  if (typeof overlay.scripts?.[name] === 'string') current.scripts[name] = overlay.scripts[name];
}
fs.writeFileSync(targetPath, `${JSON.stringify(current, null, 2)}\n`, 'utf8');
NODE

for rel in \
  scripts/dev.mjs \
  scripts/dev-brain.mjs \
  scripts/backend.sh \
  main.js \
  scripts/gui.mjs \
  scripts/dev-ui.mjs \
  scripts/startup/engine.mjs \
  scripts/startup/preflight.mjs \
  scripts/startup/capability-manifest.schema.json \
  scripts/startup/dependency-graph.mjs \
  scripts/startup/process-lock.mjs \
  scripts/startup/process-inspector.mjs \
  scripts/startup/approved-baseline.mjs \
  scripts/startup/repair-registry.mjs \
  scripts/startup/baseline.mjs \
  scripts/startup/locks.mjs \
  scripts/startup/monitor.mjs \
  scripts/startup/await-proactive.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/dependency-graph.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/process-lock.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/process-inspector.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/http-readiness.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/security-config.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/approved-baseline.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/repair-registry.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/health-contracts.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/proactive-health.test.mjs \
  docs/startup-integrity.md; do
  mkdir -p "$ROOT/$(dirname "$rel")"
  cp "$OVERLAY_DIR/$rel" "$ROOT/$rel"
done

# Ignore generated runtime diagnostics and rollback backups, without replacing
# any existing .gitignore content.
GITIGNORE="$ROOT/.gitignore"
[[ -f "$GITIGNORE" ]] || : > "$GITIGNORE"
for rule in '.aariya/diagnostics/' '.aariya/locks/' '.aariya/startup-integrity/' '.aariya/startup-integrity-backups/'; do
  if ! grep -Fqx "$rule" "$GITIGNORE"; then printf '\n%s\n' "$rule" >> "$GITIGNORE"; fi
done

# Validate syntax only; this deliberately does not start Aariya or call providers.
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/engine.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/preflight.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/dependency-graph.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/process-lock.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/process-inspector.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/approved-baseline.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/repair-registry.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/baseline.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/locks.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/monitor.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/await-proactive.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/dev.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/dev-brain.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/dev-ui.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/main.js"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/gui.mjs"
bash -n "$ROOT/scripts/backend.sh"
node --test "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/dependency-graph.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/process-lock.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/process-inspector.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/http-readiness.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/security-config.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/approved-baseline.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/repair-registry.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/health-contracts.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/proactive-health.test.mjs"
node -e "JSON.parse(require('node:fs').readFileSync(process.argv[1], 'utf8'))" "$ROOT/package.json"

printf '\nAariya startup-integrity overlay applied.\n'
printf 'Backups: %s\n' "$BACKUP_DIR"
printf 'Next verification command: cd %q && npm run startup:check\n' "$ROOT"


#!/usr/bin/env bash
# scripts/backend.sh — control the persistent launchd-managed Aariya brain.
#
# The backend normally runs as a foreground child of `npm run dev` (dev.mjs)
# and dies with the terminal. For a persistent, always-on brain that survives
# reboots it runs under macOS launchd as the `com.aariya.backend` agent
# (plist: ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.aariya.backend.plist).
#
# This script bootstraps ("start") and boots out ("stop") that agent, and
# recreates the plist if it has been removed. macOS only (launchd).
#
# Usage:
#   bash scripts/backend.sh [start|stop|restart|status|health]   (default: status)
#   AARIYA_PORT=8000 bash scripts/backend.sh start        (override port)
#
# `health` also checks the weekly dependency-scan agent (com.aariya.depscan,
# scripts/scan_deps.sh) — run it via `npm run health`.
#
# For the hot-reload dev loop use `npm run dev` or `npm run dev:brain` instead.

set -euo pipefail

# ── Paths / config ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
LABEL="com.aariya.backend"
PORT="${AARIYA_PORT:-8000}"
PLIST="${AARIYA_PLIST:-$HOME/Library/LaunchAgents/$LABEL.plist}"
# Use the same candidate environments as preflight, preferring the server venv
# for the persistent launchd process. AARIYA_PYTHON is an explicit override.
if [[ -n "${AARIYA_PYTHON:-}" ]]; then
  PYTHON="$AARIYA_PYTHON"
elif [[ -x "$ROOT/server/.venv/bin/python" ]]; then
  PYTHON="$ROOT/server/.venv/bin/python"
elif [[ -x "$ROOT/venv/bin/python" ]]; then
  PYTHON="$ROOT/venv/bin/python"
elif [[ -x "$ROOT/.venv/bin/python" ]]; then
  PYTHON="$ROOT/.venv/bin/python"
else
  PYTHON="$(command -v python3 || command -v python || true)"
fi
LOG_OUT="/tmp/aariya_backend.log"
LOG_ERR="/tmp/aariya_backend.err.log"

GREEN='\033[32m'; YELLOW='\033[33m'; RED='\033[31m'; RESET='\033[0m'
ok()   { printf '%b%s%b\n' "$GREEN" "✅ $1" "$RESET"; }
warn() { printf '%b%s%b\n' "$YELLOW" "⚠️  $1" "$RESET"; }
fail() { printf '%b%s%b\n' "$RED" "❌ $1" "$RESET"; }

# Shared lifecycle intent lock. It coordinates start/stop/restart operations with
# `npm run dev` / `npm run dev:brain`, which hold the matching Node lock while
# they own the development brain. Stale locks are never deleted automatically.
LOCK_ROOT="$ROOT/.aariya/locks"
LIFECYCLE_LOCK="$LOCK_ROOT/backend-lifecycle.lock"
LIFECYCLE_TOKEN="backend-shell-$$"
LIFECYCLE_LOCK_HELD=0
acquire_lifecycle_lock() {
  if [[ -L "$ROOT/.aariya" || -L "$LOCK_ROOT" ]]; then
    fail "Refusing lifecycle lock because .aariya or its locks directory is a symlink. Inspect the path before retrying."
    return 1
  fi
  if ! mkdir -p "$LOCK_ROOT"; then
    fail "Could not create lifecycle lock directory: $LOCK_ROOT"
    return 1
  fi
  chmod 700 "$ROOT/.aariya" "$LOCK_ROOT" 2>/dev/null || true
  if ( set -o noclobber; printf '{\n  "schemaVersion": 1,\n  "name": "backend-lifecycle",\n  "pid": %s,\n  "ownerToken": "%s",\n  "acquiredAt": "%s",\n  "command": "backend.sh %s"\n}\n' "$$" "$LIFECYCLE_TOKEN" "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" "${1:-unknown}" > "$LIFECYCLE_LOCK" ) 2>/dev/null; then
    chmod 600 "$LIFECYCLE_LOCK" 2>/dev/null || true
    LIFECYCLE_LOCK_HELD=1
    trap release_lifecycle_lock EXIT
    return 0
  fi
  local owner_pid
  owner_pid="$(sed -n 's/^[[:space:]]*"pid"[[:space:]]*:[[:space:]]*\([0-9][0-9]*\),*.*/\1/p' "$LIFECYCLE_LOCK" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
  if [ -n "$owner_pid" ] && kill -0 "$owner_pid" 2>/dev/null; then
    fail "Another startup/lifecycle operation is active (PID $owner_pid). Refusing concurrent backend operation."
  else
    fail "A stale or malformed lifecycle lock exists at $LIFECYCLE_LOCK. Verify the listed process is gone, then remove the lock manually; it is not removed automatically."
  fi
  return 1
}
release_lifecycle_lock() {
  [ "$LIFECYCLE_LOCK_HELD" = 1 ] || return 0
  if grep -Fq "\"ownerToken\": \"$LIFECYCLE_TOKEN\"" "$LIFECYCLE_LOCK" 2>/dev/null; then
    rm -f "$LIFECYCLE_LOCK"
  fi
  LIFECYCLE_LOCK_HELD=0
}

# ── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
UID_NUM="$(id -u)"
# Authoritative state check: `launchctl print` is exact, whereas grepping the
# `launchctl list` table has proved flaky in some environments.
is_loaded() { launchctl print "gui/$UID_NUM/$LABEL" >/dev/null 2>&1; }
# The weekly dependency-scan agent (scripts/scan_deps.sh) — separate service.
depscan_scheduled() {
  launchctl print "gui/$UID_NUM/com.aariya.depscan" >/dev/null 2>&1
}
# Non-fatal reminder shown after start/restart when the dep scan isn't scheduled.
warn_depscan_if_unscheduled() {
  if ! depscan_scheduled; then
    warn "dependency scan (com.aariya.depscan) NOT scheduled — install: bash scripts/scan_deps.sh install"
  fi
}
# Shared JavaScript preflight keeps persistent startup aligned with npm run dev.
# It does not print environment values; only statuses/key names are reported.
run_startup_preflight() {
  if ! command -v node >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    fail "Node.js is required to run the Aariya startup integrity preflight."
    return 1
  fi
  node "$ROOT/scripts/startup/preflight.mjs" --profile persistent-brain
}
verify_required_proactive_health() {
  if ! command -v node >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    fail "Node.js is required to verify required proactive subsystem readiness."
    return 1
  fi
  node "$ROOT/scripts/startup/await-proactive.mjs"
}
# Any HTTP response counts as "up" (the brain answers 404 on "/" — never
# use curl -f here, it would treat that as failure).
is_up()     { curl -sS -o /dev/null --max-time 2 "http://127.0.0.1:$PORT/"; }
agent_pid() {
  # Anchored to the exact field so `ppid =` / `process type =` lines can't
  # hijack the match.
  launchctl print "gui/$UID_NUM/$LABEL" 2>/dev/null |
    awk -F' = ' '$1 ~ /pid$/ {print $2; exit}'
}

# (Re)create the plist from the current paths — the agent is self-describing
# so the script stays the single source of truth even if the plist is deleted.
write_plist() {
  mkdir -p "$(dirname "$PLIST")"
  cat > "$PLIST" <<EOF
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!DOCTYPE plist PUBLIC "-//Apple//DTD PLIST 1.0//EN" "http://www.apple.com/DTDs/PropertyList-1.0.dtd">
<plist version="1.0">
<dict>
  <key>Label</key><string>$LABEL</string>
  <key>ProgramArguments</key>
  <array>
    <string>$PYTHON</string>
    <string>-m</string>
    <string>uvicorn</string>
    <string>server.main:app</string>
    <string>--host</string>
    <string>0.0.0.0</string>
    <string>--port</string>
    <string>$PORT</string>
  </array>
  <key>WorkingDirectory</key>
  <string>$ROOT</string>
  <key>RunAtLoad</key><true/>
  <key>KeepAlive</key><true/>
  <key>StandardOutPath</key><string>$LOG_OUT</string>
  <key>StandardErrorPath</key><string>$LOG_ERR</string>
</dict>
</plist>
EOF
}

# Bootstrap into the GUI launchd domain, tolerating "already loaded". The
# bootstrap error is kept for the caller to surface when startup fails.
bootstrap_agent() {
  if is_loaded; then return 0; fi
  # A booted-out instance may still be de-registering — bootstrapping the
  # same label too early can succeed without actually starting anything.
  local waited=0
  while is_loaded && [ "$waited" -lt 30 ]; do
    sleep 1
    waited=$((waited + 1))
  done
  if launchctl bootstrap "gui/$UID_NUM" "$PLIST" 2>/tmp/backend_boot.err; then
    return 0
  fi
  # Teardown races are transient — retry once before falling back.
  sleep 2
  launchctl bootstrap "gui/$UID_NUM" "$PLIST" 2>>/tmp/backend_boot.err && return 0
  # macOS predates bootstrap/bootout — legacy load path.
  launchctl load -w "$PLIST" 2>/dev/null
}

# Boot the agent out of the domain; best-effort legacy unload fallback.
# Returns immediately; `stop` separately waits for the port to free up.
bootout_agent() {
  launchctl bootout "gui/$UID_NUM/$LABEL" 2>/dev/null || true
  launchctl unload "$PLIST" 2>/dev/null || true
}

# ── Commands ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
start() {
  if is_up; then
    if ! verify_required_proactive_health; then
      fail "Brain is answering, but required proactive subsystem readiness did not pass."
      return 1
    fi
    if is_loaded; then
      ok "Aariya brain already answering on :$PORT — nothing to do."
      warn_depscan_if_unscheduled
    else
      # Something else holds the port (e.g. a dev-mode uvicorn) — the
      # launchd agent is NOT managed by this script right now.
      warn ":$PORT is answering but the launchd agent is not loaded — use: npm run dev -- --clean"
    fi
    return 0
  fi
  if [ ! -f "$PLIST" ]; then
    fail "launchd agent plist not found ($PLIST)."
    fail "Aariya auto-start is intentionally DISABLED on this Mac — the launchd agent was removed so the server never starts at login, after restart, or after shutdown."
    fail "To run it manually from a terminal (dies with the terminal):"
    fail "    npm run dev"
    fail "    # or directly:"
    fail "    \"$ROOT/venv/bin/uvicorn\" server.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port $PORT"
    return 1
  fi
  if ! run_startup_preflight; then
    fail "Persistent brain startup blocked by preflight. Resolve the reported critical failures and retry."
    return 1
  fi
  # A just-stopped instance drains gracefully and can leave the socket bound
  # (refusing connections) for a while. Let it go before bootstrapping, or
  # the fresh process crash-loops on "address already in use".
  local drain=0
  while lsof -nP -i ":$PORT" -sTCP:LISTEN >/dev/null 2>&1 && [ "$drain" -lt 60 ]; do
    sleep 1
    drain=$((drain + 1))
  done
  if ! bootstrap_agent; then
    fail "could not load launchd agent $LABEL — $(tail -1 /tmp/backend_boot.err 2>/dev/null)"
    return 1
  fi
  # The brain boots in ~10s; 120s also absorbs a slow port release right
  # after `stop` (uvicorn drains gracefully and launchd throttles restarts
  # while the old socket lingers), without false failure.
  local waited=0
  until is_up || [ "$waited" -ge 120 ]; do sleep 1; waited=$((waited + 1)); done
  if is_up; then
    if ! verify_required_proactive_health; then
      fail "Brain started, but required proactive subsystem readiness failed. Review the health route and runtime dependencies."
      return 1
    fi
    ok "Aariya brain is UP on :$PORT (launchd $LABEL)."
    warn_depscan_if_unscheduled
  else
    fail "brain did not answer on :$PORT within 120s — check $LOG_ERR"
    return 1
  fi
}

stop() {
  if ! is_loaded && ! is_up; then
    warn "agent $LABEL is not loaded — nothing to stop."
    return 0
  fi
  bootout_agent
  # launchd de-registers the booted-out service asynchronously and uvicorn
  # drains its socket gracefully. Wait for BOTH before returning — re-
  # bootstrapping the same label while the old registration lingers can
  # silently no-op.
  local waited=0
  until { ! is_loaded && ! is_up; } || [ "$waited" -ge 60 ]; do
    sleep 1
    waited=$((waited + 1))
  done
  if is_loaded || is_up; then
    warn "agent still present after bootout — a stray process may hold :$PORT (use: npm run dev -- --clean)."
  else
    ok "Aariya brain stopped (agent $LABEL booted out)."
  fi
}

status() {
  if is_loaded; then
    local pid
    pid="$(agent_pid)"
    if is_up; then
      ok "RUNNING — $LABEL (pid ${pid:-?}) answering on :$PORT."
    else
      warn "LOADED but not answering on :$PORT (pid ${pid:-?}) — check $LOG_ERR."
    fi
  else
    warn "NOT LOADED — brain is not running. Start it with: bash scripts/backend.sh start"
  fi
}

restart() { stop; start; }

# launchd only exists on macOS. health() stays truthful (and CI-friendly)
# on other hosts: report n/a and pass — the real CI gates are the backend
# suite, pip-audit and the live CVE scan, not local macOS services.
# Set AARIYA_NO_LAUNCHD=1 to force the n/a branch (used by the CI workflow).
launchd_available() {
  [ "${AARIYA_NO_LAUNCHD:-0}" = "1" ] && return 1
  command -v launchctl >/dev/null 2>&1
}

# Combined health check: the brain AND the weekly dependency-scan agent.
# Non-zero exit if either is down / not scheduled — CI- and cron-friendly.
health() {
  local rc=0
  echo "── Aariya services ──"
  if ! launchd_available; then
    warn "brain    — n/a (no launchd on this host — macOS only)."
    warn "depscan  — n/a (no launchd on this host — macOS only)."
    echo "  (health is a macOS local-ops check — CI gates: backend suite + pip-audit + live CVE scan)"
    return 0
  fi
  if is_loaded; then
    local pid
    pid="$(agent_pid)"
    if is_up; then
      ok "brain    — RUNNING (pid ${pid:-?}) answering on :$PORT."
    else
      warn "brain    — LOADED but not answering on :$PORT."
      rc=1
    fi
  else
    warn "brain    — NOT LOADED. Start: bash scripts/backend.sh start"
    rc=1
  fi
  if launchctl print "gui/$UID_NUM/com.aariya.depscan" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    ok "depscan  — SCHEDULED (weekly Monday 06:00)."
  else
    warn "depscan  — NOT SCHEDULED. Install: bash scripts/scan_deps.sh install"
    rc=1
  fi
  # Last scan outcome (log written by scripts/scan_deps.sh run). The grep
  # pipelines may legitimately find nothing — the || true guards keep them
  # from aborting under set -e.
  local last_run last_health
  last_run="$(grep '== Dependency scan' /tmp/aariya_depscan.log 2>/dev/null | tail -1 \
    | sed 's/== Dependency scan — //; s/ ==//' || true)"
  last_health="$(grep -E 'Dependency scan (healthy|reported)' /tmp/aariya_depscan.log 2>/dev/null | tail -1 || true)"
  if [ -n "$last_run" ]; then
    echo "  last depscan: $last_run  —  ${last_health:-see log for details}"
  else
    echo "  last depscan: none yet (first run Monday 06:00, or: bash scripts/scan_deps.sh run)"
  fi
  return "$rc"
}

COMMAND="${1:-status}"
case "$COMMAND" in
  start|stop|restart)
    acquire_lifecycle_lock "$COMMAND" || exit 1
    ;;
esac

case "$COMMAND" in
  start)   start ;;
  stop)    stop ;;
  restart) restart ;;
  status)  status ;;
  health)  health ;;
  *)
    echo "Usage: bash scripts/backend.sh [start|stop|restart|status|health]   (default: status)" >&2
    exit 2
    ;;
esac


#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

OVERLAY_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
TARGET_INPUT="${1:-}"
if [[ -z "$TARGET_INPUT" || ! -d "$TARGET_INPUT" ]]; then
  echo "Usage: bash apply-overlay.sh /absolute/or/relative/path/to/Aariya-repository" >&2
  exit 2
fi
ROOT="$(cd "$TARGET_INPUT" && pwd)"
if [[ ! -f "$ROOT/package.json" || ! -f "$ROOT/scripts/dev.mjs" || ! -f "$ROOT/scripts/dev-brain.mjs" || ! -f "$ROOT/scripts/backend.sh" || ! -f "$ROOT/main.js" ]]; then
  echo "Target does not look like the Aariya project supplied for this patch." >&2
  echo "Expected package.json, scripts/dev.mjs, scripts/dev-brain.mjs, scripts/backend.sh and main.js at the target root." >&2
  exit 2
fi

STAMP="$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
BACKUP_DIR="$ROOT/.aariya/startup-integrity-backups/$STAMP"
mkdir -p "$BACKUP_DIR"

backup_if_present() {
  local rel="$1"
  if [[ -f "$ROOT/$rel" ]]; then
    mkdir -p "$BACKUP_DIR/$(dirname "$rel")"
    cp -p "$ROOT/$rel" "$BACKUP_DIR/$rel"
  fi
}

# Make reversible backups before replacing any existing project files.
for rel in \
  package.json \
  scripts/dev.mjs \
  scripts/dev-brain.mjs \
  scripts/backend.sh \
  main.js \
  scripts/gui.mjs \
  scripts/dev-ui.mjs \
  scripts/startup/engine.mjs \
  scripts/startup/preflight.mjs \
  scripts/startup/capability-manifest.schema.json \
  scripts/startup/dependency-graph.mjs \
  scripts/startup/process-lock.mjs \
  scripts/startup/process-inspector.mjs \
  scripts/startup/approved-baseline.mjs \
  scripts/startup/repair-registry.mjs \
  scripts/startup/baseline.mjs \
  scripts/startup/locks.mjs \
  scripts/startup/monitor.mjs \
  scripts/startup/await-proactive.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/dependency-graph.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/process-lock.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/process-inspector.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/http-readiness.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/security-config.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/approved-baseline.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/repair-registry.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/health-contracts.test.mjs \
  scripts/startup/tests/proactive-health.test.mjs \
  docs/startup-integrity.md; do
  backup_if_present "$rel"
done

# Merge only launch/check scripts into the project's current package.json.
# Existing dependencies, metadata and unrelated scripts are kept intact.
node - "$ROOT/package.json" "$OVERLAY_DIR/package.json" <<'NODE'
const fs = require('node:fs');
const [targetPath, overlayPath] = process.argv.slice(2);
const current = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(targetPath, 'utf8'));
const overlay = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(overlayPath, 'utf8'));
current.scripts ||= {};
for (const name of ['dev:ui', 'gui', 'startup:check', 'startup:check:brain', 'startup:check:ui', 'startup:check:gui', 'startup:check:persistent', 'startup:baseline:approve', 'startup:baseline:status', 'startup:locks', 'startup:monitor', 'startup:monitor:dev', 'test:startup']) {
  if (typeof overlay.scripts?.[name] === 'string') current.scripts[name] = overlay.scripts[name];
}
fs.writeFileSync(targetPath, `${JSON.stringify(current, null, 2)}\n`, 'utf8');
NODE

for rel in \
  scripts/dev.mjs \
  scripts/dev-brain.mjs \
  scripts/backend.sh \
  main.js \
  scripts/gui.mjs \
  scripts/dev-ui.mjs \
  scripts/startup/engine.mjs \
  scripts/startup/preflight.mjs \
  scripts/startup/capability-manifest.schema.json \
  scripts/startup/dependency-graph.mjs \
  scripts/startup/process-lock.mjs \
  scripts/startup/process-inspector.mjs \
  scripts/startup/approved-baseline.mjs \
  scripts/startup/repair-registry.mjs \

  scripts/startup/locks.mjs \
  scripts/startup/monitor.mjs \

  docs/startup-integrity.md; do

  cp "$OVERLAY_DIR/$rel" "$ROOT/$rel"
done

# Ignore generated runtime diagnostics and rollback backups, without replacing
# any existing .gitignore content.
GITIGNORE="$ROOT/.gitignore"
[[ -f "$GITIGNORE" ]] || : > "$GITIGNORE"
for rule in '.aariya/diagnostics/' '.aariya/locks/' '.aariya/startup-integrity/' '.aariya/startup-integrity-backups/'; do
  if ! grep -Fqx "$rule" "$GITIGNORE"; then printf '\n%s\n' "$rule" >> "$GITIGNORE"; fi

# Validate syntax only; this deliberately does not start Aariya or call providers.
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/startup/engine.mjs"
