node --check "$ROOT/scripts/dev-brain.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/dev-ui.mjs"
node --check "$ROOT/main.js"
node --check "$ROOT/scripts/gui.mjs"
bash -n "$ROOT/scripts/backend.sh"
node --test "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/dependency-graph.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/process-lock.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/process-inspector.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/http-readiness.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/security-config.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/approved-baseline.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/repair-registry.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/health-contracts.test.mjs" "$ROOT/scripts/startup/tests/proactive-health.test.mjs"
node -e "JSON.parse(require('node:fs').readFileSync(process.argv[1], 'utf8'))" "$ROOT/package.json"

printf '\nAariya startup-integrity overlay applied.\n'
printf 'Backups: %s\n' "$BACKUP_DIR"
printf 'Next verification command: cd %q && npm run startup:check\n' "$ROOT"

2. What remains to complete Aariya
The major gaps are now less about adding independent modules and more about connecting and validating the whole system.

1. Integrate the real Aariya repository

Register the proactive routes and readiness checks in the existing FastAPI server. Connect the actual authentication, shared database, consent records, policy service, and worker lifecycle. The actual server source was not available in the reviewed overlay, so this integration is not yet verified.



2. Complete real incoming calls

Integrate iOS PushKit and CallKit, Android native call presentation, the Flutter LiveKit session, and the bridge into Aariya’s existing voice and conversation runtime. Verify that answering the call continues the same conversation rather than starting another AI session.



3. Complete security and recovery

Validate real device authorization, revocation, approval gates for sensitive actions, encryption/key management, audit history, database recovery, and multi-instance operation.



4. Verify Guardian using real infrastructure

Deploy it outside Aariya’s failure domain, configure two independent alert routes, and test outages. The current implementation supports HTTP/HTTPS and local SQLite probes plus generic webhook notifications; direct probes for other databases and dedicated SMS, email, or voice-call providers still need real integrations.



My recommendation
Stop adding standalone modules temporarily and move to live integration. The overlay has a substantial set of tested components, but passing unit tests does not prove that Aariya can successfully detect an emergency, notify your device, initiate a call, preserve conversation context, and recover from failure.

The next milestone should be an end-to-end integration that exercises that entire sequence against the actual Aariya backend and Flutter application, using real services and test devices. That is the work needed to move from an integration-ready design to a working system.

Aariya Guardian Watchdog
Purpose and failure boundary
guardian_watchdog/ is a standalone monitor. It imports no Aariya application modules and has its own persistent SQLite database. Run it as a separate process with a separately provisioned notification route. For real fault isolation, deploy it on a different host or managed runtime with a different disk/credential boundary from Aariya. Merely running another process on the same host does not protect against host, disk, network, or power failure.

The Guardian checks only targets explicitly supplied in AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PROBES_JSON. It has no fabricated default service names or successful default probes. Supported real probes are:
- http: a configured HTTP(S) health endpoint; remote endpoints must use HTTPS, query parameters are rejected, and redirects are not followed.
- sqlite: a configured absolute SQLite file path, tested read-only with PRAGMA quick_check(1). The Guardian's own state database must be a different file. This is useful for local deployments; it does not provide remote database reachability from another host unless the deployment explicitly grants the Guardian read access to that file.
For PostgreSQL, Redis, cloud databases, telephony, or an external messaging provider, add a probe that uses the real driver and least-privilege credentials. Do not count an application HTTP 200 as proof every underlying provider works.
State and alert semantics
- Defaults: poll every 10 seconds, mark a target down after 3 consecutive failures, and mark it recovered after 2 consecutive successes.
- State changes and alert-outbox entries are committed in the Guardian-owned database. An interrupted process resumes outstanding deliveries after restart.
- Each configured alert route has a separate durable delivery state, retry schedule, attempt counter, and lease. One unavailable route does not prevent a second route from being attempted.
- Alert payloads contain only schema version, opaque alert/incident identifiers, configured target ID, transition kind, a normalized reason code, and timestamp. No user details, request payloads, raw response bodies, or credentials are sent.
- Delivery is at-least-once. External alert receivers should honor Idempotency-Key to avoid duplicate operator notifications.
- The configured alert routes must use distinct hostnames. They should also be operationally independent (for example separate providers or different out-of-band channels); different subdomains of the same failed service may still share a failure domain.
- Alert delivery failure is written to process output as a sanitized event. Capture that output with the service manager and alert on sustained failures independently.
Configuration
Required environment variables:
- AARIYA_GUARDIAN_DB_PATH: absolute path for the Guardian-owned database, on a persistent writable volume.
- AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PROBES_JSON: non-empty JSON array of explicit probes.
- AARIYA_GUARDIAN_ALERT_SINKS_JSON: at least two routes, with distinct hostnames.
- Each route's token_env names a separate environment variable holding that endpoint's bearer credential. Secrets themselves must not be put in JSON, source code, logs, or Git.
Example shape only (replace every host, path, probe, and secret variable with verified deployment values):
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_DB_PATH=/var/lib/aariya-guardian/guardian.sqlite3
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_INTERVAL_SECONDS=10
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_FAILURE_THRESHOLD=3
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_RECOVERY_THRESHOLD=2
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_MAX_RETRY_SECONDS=900
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_ALERT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=5
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PROBES_JSON=[{"id":"aariya_core_liveness","type":"http","url":"https://aariya-internal.example/health/live"},{"id":"aariya_proactive_readiness","type":"http","url":"https://aariya-internal.example/health/proactive/ready"}]
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_ALERT_SINKS_JSON=[{"id":"primary_ops","url":"https://notify-provider-one.example/guardian-events","token_env":"AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PRIMARY_TOKEN"},{"id":"secondary_ops","url":"https://notify-provider-two.example/guardian-events","token_env":"AARIYA_GUARDIAN_SECONDARY_TOKEN"}]
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PRIMARY_TOKEN=replace-with-secret-from-secret-manager
AARIYA_GUARDIAN_SECONDARY_TOKEN=replace-with-different-secret-from-secret-manager
The .example hostnames and replace-with... values above are documentation placeholders. They are not valid live endpoints, and the Guardian will not have useful alert delivery until real providers, credentials, and health URLs are configured.
Run
From the source root, after installing Python dependencies already used by the overlay:
python -m guardian_watchdog
The process exits with code 2 if its configuration is missing or invalid. It handles SIGINT/SIGTERM and retries failed checks/deliveries. The service manager must restart it after unexpected exits, monitor its process logs, and keep the Guardian state database persistent. Do not start this process inside Aariya's own startup lifecycle; that would couple the monitor to the system it must observe.
Production rollout checklist
1. Run Guardian under a dedicated, unprivileged OS/service identity.
2. Put the Guardian database on its own persistent volume and deny write access to Aariya's database; the SQLite probe is read-only.
3. Give it only outbound access to explicitly configured health endpoints and alert providers. Restrict outbound network traffic at the host/firewall layer too.
4. Use a secret manager for separate credentials; rotate them and test revocation.
5. Confirm each route reaches an externally managed channel, not Aariya's communication gateway. Test one route while the other is intentionally unavailable.
6. Simulate Core HTTP failure, proactive readiness failure, database failure, a dead notification route, an expired delivery lease, and Guardian process restart.
7. Verify alert receiver idempotency and recovery notices. Never interpret provider acceptance as proof a human saw the alert.
8. For real high availability, deploy a second independent Guardian monitor or managed external uptime monitor. A Guardian cannot alert when its own host, own network path, or all alert routes have failed.
Current scope boundary
This implementation includes HTTP and local SQLite probes plus authenticated bearer-webhook delivery. It does not yet include a direct PostgreSQL/Redis probe, SMS vendor integration, email delivery, push delivery through Apple/Google, or a managed secret-store adapter. Those must be added against the actual production services and credentials; do not simulate their health in production.



from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import math
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Iterable, Mapping

from .models import AlertMessage, ProbeOutcome, ProbeTarget
from .sinks import AlertSink, PermanentDeliveryError, TransientDeliveryError
from .store import GuardianStore


class GuardianMonitor:
    """Independent monitor with thresholded transitions and durable alerts."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        store: GuardianStore,
        targets: Iterable[ProbeTarget],
        alert_sink: AlertSink | None = None,
        alert_sinks: Mapping[str, AlertSink] | None = None,
        interval_seconds: float = 10,
        failure_threshold: int = 3,
        recovery_threshold: int = 2,
        alert_worker_id: str = "guardian-alert-delivery",
        max_retry_seconds: int = 900,
    ) -> None:
        target_list = tuple(targets)
        ids = [target.target_id for target in target_list]
        if not target_list:
            raise ValueError("Guardian must have at least one configured target")
        if (alert_sink is None) == (alert_sinks is None):
            raise ValueError("provide exactly one of alert_sink or alert_sinks")
        if len(ids) != len(set(ids)):
            raise ValueError("Guardian target IDs must be unique")
        if not 1 <= interval_seconds <= 3600:
            raise ValueError("interval_seconds must be from 1 to 3600")
        if not 1 <= failure_threshold <= 100 or not 1 <= recovery_threshold <= 100:
            raise ValueError("health thresholds must be from 1 to 100")
        if not 1 <= max_retry_seconds <= 86400:
            raise ValueError("max_retry_seconds must be from 1 to 86400")
        self.store = store
        self.targets = target_list
        if alert_sinks is not None:
            sinks = dict(alert_sinks)
        else:
            sink_id = getattr(alert_sink, "sink_id", "primary")
            sinks = {sink_id: alert_sink}
        if not sinks or any(not callable(getattr(sink, "send", None)) for sink in sinks.values()):
            raise ValueError("at least one configured alert sink with send() is required")
        if len(sinks) != len(set(sinks)):
            raise ValueError("alert sink IDs must be unique")
        for sink_id, sink in sinks.items():
            import re
            if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]{0,63}", sink_id):
                raise ValueError("alert sink IDs must be safe lowercase identifiers")
            configured_id = getattr(sink, "sink_id", sink_id)
            if configured_id != sink_id:
                raise ValueError("alert sink mapping key must match the sink's sink_id")
        self.alert_sinks = sinks
        self.store.register_sinks(tuple(sinks.keys()))
        self.interval_seconds = interval_seconds
        self.failure_threshold = failure_threshold
        self.recovery_threshold = recovery_threshold
        self.alert_worker_id = alert_worker_id
        self.max_retry_seconds = max_retry_seconds

    async def _check_target(self, target: ProbeTarget) -> ProbeOutcome:
        try:
            outcome = await asyncio.to_thread(target.probe.check, target.target_id)
            if not isinstance(outcome, ProbeOutcome) or outcome.target_id != target.target_id:
                return ProbeOutcome(
                    target_id=target.target_id,
                    healthy=False,
                    reason_code="invalid_probe_result",
                    checked_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc),
                )
            return outcome
        except TimeoutError:
            return ProbeOutcome(target.target_id, False, "probe_timeout", datetime.now(timezone.utc))
        except Exception:
            # Exception messages are omitted to prevent accidental secret leakage.
            return ProbeOutcome(target.target_id, False, "probe_exception", datetime.now(timezone.utc))

    async def _send_due_alerts(self) -> dict[str, int]:
        counts = {"delivered": 0, "retrying": 0, "permanent_failure": 0}
        for sink_id, sink in self.alert_sinks.items():
            due = self.store.claim_due_deliveries(
                worker_id=self.alert_worker_id,
                sink_id=sink_id,
                limit=50,
                lease_seconds=60,
            )
            for alert in due:
                try:
                    await asyncio.to_thread(sink.send, alert)
                except PermanentDeliveryError as exc:
                    retry = min(self.max_retry_seconds, 3600)
                    self.store.finish_delivery(
                        alert_id=alert.alert_id, sink_id=sink_id,
                        worker_id=self.alert_worker_id, delivered=False,
                        retry_delay_seconds=retry,
                        error_code=str(exc).split(":", 1)[0].replace("-", "_").lower(),
                    )
                    counts["permanent_failure"] += 1
                except Exception as exc:
                    attempt = max(1, alert.attempt)
                    delay = min(self.max_retry_seconds, int(math.pow(2, min(attempt, 20))))
                    self.store.finish_delivery(
                        alert_id=alert.alert_id, sink_id=sink_id,
                        worker_id=self.alert_worker_id, delivered=False,
                        retry_delay_seconds=delay,
                        error_code=self._safe_error_code(exc),
                    )
                    counts["retrying"] += 1
                else:
                    self.store.finish_delivery(
                        alert_id=alert.alert_id, sink_id=sink_id,
                        worker_id=self.alert_worker_id, delivered=True,
                    )
                    counts["delivered"] += 1
        return counts

    @staticmethod
    def _safe_error_code(exc: Exception) -> str:
        if isinstance(exc, TransientDeliveryError):
            code = str(exc)
            if code in {"provider_connection_error", "provider_http_429", "provider_http_500", "provider_http_502", "provider_http_503", "provider_http_504"}:
                return code
        return "delivery_failed"

    async def run_once(self) -> dict[str, object]:
        outcomes = await asyncio.gather(*(self._check_target(target) for target in self.targets))
        reports: list[dict[str, object]] = []
        for outcome in outcomes:
            report = self.store.record_observation(
                outcome,
                failure_threshold=self.failure_threshold,
                recovery_threshold=self.recovery_threshold,
            )
            reports.append({
                "target_id": report["target_id"],
                "state": report["state"],
                "reason_code": report["reason_code"],
                "latency_ms": outcome.latency_ms,
                "transition_created": report["transition"] is not None,
            })
        delivery = await self._send_due_alerts()
        return {
            "checked_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
            "targets": reports,
            "alert_delivery": delivery,
        }

    async def run_forever(self, stop_event: asyncio.Event) -> None:
        while not stop_event.is_set():
            await self.run_once()
            try:
                await asyncio.wait_for(stop_event.wait(), timeout=self.interval_seconds)
            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                continue



from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import uuid
from contextlib import closing
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from .models import AlertMessage, ProbeOutcome, ProbeState


def _iso(value: datetime) -> str:
    if value.tzinfo is None or value.utcoffset() is None:
        raise ValueError("datetime must include a timezone")
    return value.astimezone(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="microseconds")


def _parse_dt(value: str) -> datetime:
    return datetime.fromisoformat(value).astimezone(timezone.utc)


class GuardianStore:
    """Guardian-owned persistent state and retryable alert outbox.

    Keep this database outside Aariya's application DB. A process on another
    host with an independent volume is stronger fault isolation than a separate
    SQLite file on the same disk.
    """

    def __init__(self, database_path: Path):
        if not database_path.is_absolute() or str(database_path) == ":memory:":
            raise ValueError("Guardian database path must be absolute and file-backed")
        self.database_path = database_path.resolve()
        self.database_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            db.executescript("""
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS guardian_target_state (
                    target_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
                    state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('unknown','up','down')),
                    failures INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                    successes INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                    last_reason TEXT NOT NULL,
                    last_check_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    active_incident_id TEXT,
                    updated_at TEXT NOT NULL
                );
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS guardian_transition_events (
                    transition_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
                    incident_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    target_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    event_kind TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(event_kind IN ('down','recovered')),
                    reason_code TEXT NOT NULL,
                    occurred_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    previous_state TEXT NOT NULL,
                    new_state TEXT NOT NULL
                );
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS guardian_configured_sinks (
                    sink_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY
                );
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS guardian_alert_outbox (
                    alert_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
                    incident_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    target_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    event_kind TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(event_kind IN ('down','recovered')),
                    reason_code TEXT NOT NULL,
                    occurred_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('pending','in_flight','delivered')),
                    attempts INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                    next_attempt_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    lease_owner TEXT,
                    lease_until TEXT,
                    last_error_code TEXT
                );
                CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_guardian_outbox_due
                    ON guardian_alert_outbox(state, next_attempt_at, lease_until);
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS guardian_alert_deliveries (
                    alert_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    sink_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                    state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('pending','in_flight','delivered')),
                    attempts INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                    next_attempt_at TEXT NOT NULL,
                    lease_owner TEXT,
                    lease_until TEXT,
                    last_error_code TEXT,
                    PRIMARY KEY(alert_id, sink_id),
                    FOREIGN KEY(alert_id) REFERENCES guardian_alert_outbox(alert_id)
                );
                CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_guardian_deliveries_due
                    ON guardian_alert_deliveries(sink_id, state, next_attempt_at, lease_until);
            """)

    def _connect(self) -> sqlite3.Connection:
        db = sqlite3.connect(self.database_path, timeout=5, isolation_level=None)
        db.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        db.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000")
        db.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
        db.execute("PRAGMA synchronous=FULL")
        return db

    def record_observation(
        self,
        outcome: ProbeOutcome,
        *,
        failure_threshold: int,
        recovery_threshold: int,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        if failure_threshold < 1 or recovery_threshold < 1:
            raise ValueError("thresholds must be positive")
        now = outcome.checked_at.astimezone(timezone.utc)
        now_iso = _iso(now)
        created_alert: dict[str, Any] | None = None
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            try:
                db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
                row = db.execute(
                    "SELECT * FROM guardian_target_state WHERE target_id=?",
                    (outcome.target_id,),
                ).fetchone()
                previous_state: ProbeState = row["state"] if row else "unknown"
                failures = int(row["failures"]) if row else 0
                successes = int(row["successes"]) if row else 0
                active_incident = row["active_incident_id"] if row else None
                state: ProbeState = previous_state
                transition: tuple[str, str, str] | None = None

                if outcome.healthy:
                    successes += 1
                    failures = 0
                    if previous_state == "down" and successes >= recovery_threshold:
                        state = "up"
                        transition = ("recovered", active_incident or str(uuid.uuid4()), outcome.reason_code)
                        active_incident = None
                    elif previous_state == "unknown" and successes >= recovery_threshold:
                        state = "up"
                else:
                    failures += 1
                    successes = 0
                    if previous_state != "down" and failures >= failure_threshold:
                        state = "down"
                        incident_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
                        active_incident = incident_id
                        transition = ("down", incident_id, outcome.reason_code)

                db.execute("""INSERT INTO guardian_target_state
                    (target_id,state,failures,successes,last_reason,last_check_at,active_incident_id,updated_at)
                    VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?)
                    ON CONFLICT(target_id) DO UPDATE SET
                        state=excluded.state, failures=excluded.failures,
                        successes=excluded.successes, last_reason=excluded.last_reason,
                        last_check_at=excluded.last_check_at,
                        active_incident_id=excluded.active_incident_id, updated_at=excluded.updated_at""",
                    (outcome.target_id, state, failures, successes, outcome.reason_code,
                     now_iso, active_incident, now_iso),
                )

                if transition is not None:
                    kind, incident_id, reason_code = transition
                    transition_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
                    alert_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
                    db.execute("""INSERT INTO guardian_transition_events
                        (transition_id,incident_id,target_id,event_kind,reason_code,occurred_at,previous_state,new_state)
                        VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?)""",
                        (transition_id, incident_id, outcome.target_id, kind, reason_code,
                         now_iso, previous_state, state),
                    )
                    db.execute("""INSERT INTO guardian_alert_outbox
                        (alert_id,incident_id,target_id,event_kind,reason_code,occurred_at,state,attempts,next_attempt_at)
                        VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,'pending',0,?)""",
                        (alert_id, incident_id, outcome.target_id, kind, reason_code, now_iso, now_iso),
                    )
                    configured = db.execute(
                        "SELECT sink_id FROM guardian_configured_sinks ORDER BY sink_id"
                    ).fetchall()
                    sink_ids = [entry["sink_id"] for entry in configured] or ["primary"]
                    for sink_id in sink_ids:
                        db.execute("""INSERT INTO guardian_alert_deliveries
                            (alert_id,sink_id,state,attempts,next_attempt_at)
                            VALUES (?,?,'pending',0,?)""", (alert_id, sink_id, now_iso))
                    created_alert = {
                        "alert_id": alert_id,
                        "incident_id": incident_id,
                        "target_id": outcome.target_id,
                        "event_kind": kind,
                        "reason_code": reason_code,
                        "occurred_at": now_iso,
                    }
                db.execute("COMMIT")
            except Exception:
                if db.in_transaction:
                    db.execute("ROLLBACK")
                raise

        return {
            "target_id": outcome.target_id,
            "state": state,
            "failures": failures,
            "successes": successes,
            "reason_code": outcome.reason_code,
            "checked_at": now_iso,
            "transition": created_alert,
        }

    def register_sinks(self, sink_ids: list[str] | tuple[str, ...]) -> None:
        """Register real delivery routes and backfill undelivered events for newly added routes."""
        import re

        ids = tuple(sink_ids)
        if not ids or len(set(ids)) != len(ids):
            raise ValueError("at least one unique alert sink ID is required")
        if any(not isinstance(sink_id, str) or not re.fullmatch(r"[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]{0,63}", sink_id) for sink_id in ids):
            raise ValueError("sink IDs must be safe lowercase identifiers")
        now_iso = _iso(datetime.now(timezone.utc))
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
            try:
                existing = {row["sink_id"] for row in db.execute("SELECT sink_id FROM guardian_configured_sinks")}
                desired = set(ids)
                for retired_sink in existing - desired:
                    pending = db.execute("""SELECT COUNT(*) AS count FROM guardian_alert_deliveries
                        WHERE sink_id=? AND state!='delivered'""", (retired_sink,)).fetchone()["count"]
                    if int(pending) > 0:
                        raise ValueError("cannot remove an alert route with pending deliveries; resolve or preserve that route first")
                    db.execute("DELETE FROM guardian_configured_sinks WHERE sink_id=?", (retired_sink,))
                for sink_id in ids:
                    db.execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO guardian_configured_sinks(sink_id) VALUES(?)", (sink_id,))
                    if sink_id not in existing:
                        rows = db.execute("SELECT alert_id FROM guardian_alert_outbox WHERE state!='delivered'").fetchall()
                        for row in rows:
                            db.execute("""INSERT OR IGNORE INTO guardian_alert_deliveries
                                (alert_id,sink_id,state,attempts,next_attempt_at)
                                VALUES (?,?,'pending',0,?)""", (row["alert_id"], sink_id, now_iso))
                db.execute("COMMIT")
            except Exception:
                if db.in_transaction:
                    db.execute("ROLLBACK")
                raise

    def claim_due_deliveries(
        self,
        *,
        worker_id: str,
        sink_id: str,
        limit: int = 20,
        lease_seconds: int = 30,
        now: datetime | None = None,
    ) -> list[AlertMessage]:
        if not worker_id or len(worker_id) > 128:
            raise ValueError("worker_id must be a non-empty identifier")
        if not 1 <= limit <= 500 or not 1 <= lease_seconds <= 3600:
            raise ValueError("invalid outbox claim limits")
        current = now or datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        current_iso = _iso(current)
        lease_until = _iso(current + timedelta(seconds=lease_seconds))
        results: list[AlertMessage] = []
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            try:
                db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
                rows = db.execute("""SELECT o.*, d.attempts AS delivery_attempts
                    FROM guardian_alert_deliveries d
                    JOIN guardian_alert_outbox o ON o.alert_id=d.alert_id
                    WHERE d.sink_id=? AND (
                        (d.state='pending' AND d.next_attempt_at<=?)
                        OR (d.state='in_flight' AND d.lease_until<=?)
                    ) ORDER BY o.occurred_at LIMIT ?""",
                    (sink_id, current_iso, current_iso, limit),
                ).fetchall()
                for row in rows:
                    db.execute("""UPDATE guardian_alert_deliveries SET state='in_flight',
                        lease_owner=?,lease_until=?,attempts=attempts+1 WHERE alert_id=? AND sink_id=?""",
                        (worker_id, lease_until, row["alert_id"], sink_id),
                    )
                    results.append(AlertMessage(
                        alert_id=row["alert_id"], incident_id=row["incident_id"],
                        target_id=row["target_id"], kind=row["event_kind"],
                        reason_code=row["reason_code"], occurred_at=_parse_dt(row["occurred_at"]),
                        attempt=int(row["delivery_attempts"]) + 1, sink_id=sink_id,
                    ))
                db.execute("COMMIT")
            except Exception:
                if db.in_transaction:
                    db.execute("ROLLBACK")
                raise
        return results

    def claim_due_alerts(
        self,
        *,
        worker_id: str,
        limit: int = 20,
        lease_seconds: int = 30,
        now: datetime | None = None,
    ) -> list[AlertMessage]:
        """Compatibility helper for a single sink named primary."""
        return self.claim_due_deliveries(
            worker_id=worker_id, sink_id="primary", limit=limit,
            lease_seconds=lease_seconds, now=now,
        )

    def finish_delivery(
        self,
        *,
        alert_id: str,
        sink_id: str,
        worker_id: str,
        delivered: bool,
        retry_delay_seconds: int = 30,
        error_code: str | None = None,
        now: datetime | None = None,
    ) -> bool:
        current = now or datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        next_attempt = _iso(current + timedelta(seconds=max(1, min(retry_delay_seconds, 86400))))
        if error_code is not None:
            import re
            if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]{0,63}", error_code):
                error_code = "delivery_failed"
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            db.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
            try:
                row = db.execute("""SELECT * FROM guardian_alert_deliveries
                    WHERE alert_id=? AND sink_id=?""", (alert_id, sink_id)).fetchone()
                if row is None or row["state"] != "in_flight" or row["lease_owner"] != worker_id:
                    db.execute("ROLLBACK")
                    return False
                if delivered:
                    db.execute("""UPDATE guardian_alert_deliveries SET state='delivered',
                        lease_owner=NULL,lease_until=NULL,last_error_code=NULL
                        WHERE alert_id=? AND sink_id=?""", (alert_id, sink_id))
                else:
                    db.execute("""UPDATE guardian_alert_deliveries SET state='pending',next_attempt_at=?,
                        lease_owner=NULL,lease_until=NULL,last_error_code=? WHERE alert_id=? AND sink_id=?""",
                        (next_attempt, error_code or "delivery_failed", alert_id, sink_id),
                    )
                remaining = db.execute("""SELECT COUNT(*) AS count FROM guardian_alert_deliveries
                    WHERE alert_id=? AND state!='delivered'""", (alert_id,)).fetchone()["count"]
                db.execute("""UPDATE guardian_alert_outbox SET state=? WHERE alert_id=?""",
                    ("delivered" if int(remaining) == 0 else "pending", alert_id),
                )
                db.execute("COMMIT")
                return True
            except Exception:
                if db.in_transaction:
                    db.execute("ROLLBACK")
                raise

    def finish_alert(
        self,
        *,
        alert_id: str,
        worker_id: str,
        delivered: bool,
        retry_delay_seconds: int = 30,
        error_code: str | None = None,
        now: datetime | None = None,
    ) -> bool:
        """Compatibility helper for a single sink named primary."""
        return self.finish_delivery(
            alert_id=alert_id, sink_id="primary", worker_id=worker_id,
            delivered=delivered, retry_delay_seconds=retry_delay_seconds,
            error_code=error_code, now=now,
        )

    def get_target_state(self, target_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            row = db.execute("SELECT * FROM guardian_target_state WHERE target_id=?", (target_id,)).fetchone()
        return dict(row) if row else None

    def list_outbox(self, *, limit: int = 100) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        if not 1 <= limit <= 1000:
            raise ValueError("limit must be from 1 to 1000")
        with closing(self._connect()) as db:
            rows = db.execute("SELECT * FROM guardian_alert_outbox ORDER BY occurred_at DESC LIMIT ?", (limit,)).fetchall()
        return [dict(row) for row in rows]



from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from urllib.error import HTTPError, URLError
from urllib.parse import urlparse
from urllib.request import HTTPRedirectHandler, Request, build_opener
import ipaddress
import socket

from .models import ProbeOutcome


def _is_loopback(hostname: str | None) -> bool:
    if not hostname:
        return False
    if hostname.lower() == "localhost":
        return True
    try:
        return ipaddress.ip_address(hostname).is_loopback
    except ValueError:
        return False


def validate_probe_url(url: str) -> None:
    parsed = urlparse(url)
    if parsed.scheme not in {"http", "https"} or not parsed.hostname:
        raise ValueError("probe URL must be an absolute HTTP(S) URL")
    if parsed.username or parsed.password or parsed.fragment or parsed.query:
        raise ValueError("probe URL must not include credentials, query parameters, or a fragment")
    if parsed.scheme != "https" and not _is_loopback(parsed.hostname):
        raise ValueError("non-loopback probe URLs must use HTTPS")


class _NoRedirect(HTTPRedirectHandler):
    def redirect_request(self, req, fp, code, msg, headers, newurl):
        return None


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class HttpProbe:
    """Probe a configured health URL; never forwards an event-supplied URL."""

    url: str
    timeout_seconds: float = 3.0
    expected_statuses: tuple[int, ...] = (200,)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        validate_probe_url(self.url)
        if not 0.1 <= self.timeout_seconds <= 30:
            raise ValueError("timeout_seconds must be between 0.1 and 30")
        if not self.expected_statuses or any(
            type(status) is not int or status < 100 or status > 599
            for status in self.expected_statuses
        ):
            raise ValueError("expected_statuses must contain HTTP status codes")

    def check(self, target_id: str) -> ProbeOutcome:
        started = time.monotonic()
        checked_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        req = Request(self.url, headers={"Accept": "application/json", "User-Agent": "Aariya-Guardian/1"}, method="GET")
        code: int | None = None
        try:
            opener = build_opener(_NoRedirect())
            with opener.open(req, timeout=self.timeout_seconds) as response:
                code = int(response.status)
                # Do not read or log the response body; it may contain sensitive data.
        except HTTPError as exc:
            code = int(exc.code)
        except TimeoutError:
            reason = "probe_timeout"
            return ProbeOutcome(target_id, False, reason, checked_at, int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000))
        except (URLError, socket.timeout, OSError):
            return ProbeOutcome(target_id, False, "connection_error", checked_at, int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000))
        latency = int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000)
        if code in self.expected_statuses:
            return ProbeOutcome(target_id, True, "healthy", checked_at, latency)
        return ProbeOutcome(target_id, False, f"http_status_{code}", checked_at, latency)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class SqliteDatabaseProbe:
    """Read-only SQLite health check that does not mutate application tables."""

    database_path: Path
    timeout_seconds: float = 1.0

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.database_path.is_absolute() or str(self.database_path) == ":memory:":
            raise ValueError("SQLite probe path must be an absolute file path")
        if not 0.1 <= self.timeout_seconds <= 30:
            raise ValueError("timeout_seconds must be between 0.1 and 30")

    def check(self, target_id: str) -> ProbeOutcome:
        checked_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        started = time.monotonic()
        if not self.database_path.is_file():
            return ProbeOutcome(target_id, False, "database_file_missing", checked_at, 0)
        connection: sqlite3.Connection | None = None
        try:
            # URI mode=ro prevents the Guardian from creating or modifying the target DB.
            uri = self.database_path.as_uri() + "?mode=ro"
            connection = sqlite3.connect(uri, uri=True, timeout=self.timeout_seconds)
            row = connection.execute("PRAGMA quick_check(1)").fetchone()
            healthy = bool(row and row[0] == "ok")
        except sqlite3.Error:
            healthy = False
        finally:
            if connection is not None:
                connection.close()
        reason = "healthy" if healthy else "database_check_failed"
        return ProbeOutcome(target_id, healthy, reason, checked_at, int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000))



from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass
from urllib.error import HTTPError, URLError
from urllib.parse import urlparse
from urllib.request import HTTPRedirectHandler, Request, build_opener
import ipaddress

from .models import AlertMessage


def _is_loopback(hostname: str | None) -> bool:
    if not hostname:
        return False
    if hostname.lower() == "localhost":
        return True
    try:
        return ipaddress.ip_address(hostname).is_loopback
    except ValueError:
        return False


class _NoRedirect(HTTPRedirectHandler):
    def redirect_request(self, req, fp, code, msg, headers, newurl):
        return None


class AlertSink:
    def send(self, alert: AlertMessage) -> None:
        raise NotImplementedError


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class WebhookAlertSink(AlertSink):
    """Send minimal transition notifications to an operator-controlled endpoint."""

    url: str
    bearer_token: str
    timeout_seconds: float = 5.0
    sink_id: str = "primary"

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]{0,63}", self.sink_id):
            raise ValueError("sink_id must be a safe lowercase identifier")
        parsed = urlparse(self.url)
        if parsed.scheme not in {"http", "https"} or not parsed.hostname:
            raise ValueError("alert webhook URL must be an absolute HTTP(S) URL")
        if parsed.username or parsed.password or parsed.fragment or parsed.query:
            raise ValueError("alert webhook URL must not contain credentials, query parameters, or a fragment")
        if parsed.scheme != "https" and not _is_loopback(parsed.hostname):
            raise ValueError("non-loopback alert webhooks must use HTTPS")
        if not self.bearer_token or len(self.bearer_token) < 24:
            raise ValueError("alert webhook bearer token must be configured and at least 24 characters")
        if not 0.1 <= self.timeout_seconds <= 30:
            raise ValueError("timeout_seconds must be between 0.1 and 30")

    def send(self, alert: AlertMessage) -> None:
        body = json.dumps(alert.public_payload(), separators=(",", ":"), allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8")
        request = Request(
            self.url,
            data=body,
            headers={
                "Authorization": f"Bearer {self.bearer_token}",
                "Content-Type": "application/json",
                "Accept": "application/json",
                "User-Agent": "Aariya-Guardian/1",
                "Idempotency-Key": alert.alert_id,
            },
            method="POST",
        )
        try:
            opener = build_opener(_NoRedirect())
            with opener.open(request, timeout=self.timeout_seconds) as response:
                if not 200 <= int(response.status) < 300:
                    raise RuntimeError("provider_rejected")
                # Do not read or log provider response content.
        except HTTPError as exc:
            # No response body or reason string is included in the exception.
            if exc.code in {400, 401, 403, 404, 422}:
                raise PermanentDeliveryError(f"provider_rejected_{exc.code}") from None
            raise TransientDeliveryError(f"provider_http_{exc.code}") from None
        except (URLError, TimeoutError, OSError):
            raise TransientDeliveryError("provider_connection_error") from None


class TransientDeliveryError(RuntimeError):
    pass


class PermanentDeliveryError(RuntimeError):
    pass


from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Mapping
from urllib.parse import urlparse

from .models import ProbeTarget
from .probes import HttpProbe, SqliteDatabaseProbe
from .sinks import WebhookAlertSink


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class GuardianSettings:
    database_path: Path
    targets: tuple[ProbeTarget, ...]
    alert_sinks: Mapping[str, WebhookAlertSink]
    interval_seconds: float = 10
    failure_threshold: int = 3
    recovery_threshold: int = 2
    max_retry_seconds: int = 900

    @classmethod
    def from_env(cls, env: Mapping[str, str] | None = None) -> "GuardianSettings":
        source = os.environ if env is None else env
        db_raw = source.get("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_DB_PATH", "").strip()
        if not db_raw:
            raise ValueError("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_DB_PATH is required")
        db_path = Path(db_raw).expanduser()
        if not db_path.is_absolute():
            raise ValueError("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_DB_PATH must be absolute")
        db_path = db_path.resolve()

        raw_targets = source.get("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PROBES_JSON", "").strip()
        if not raw_targets:
            raise ValueError("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PROBES_JSON must define at least one real target")
        try:
            configs = json.loads(raw_targets)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            raise ValueError("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PROBES_JSON is invalid JSON") from None
        if not isinstance(configs, list) or not configs:
            raise ValueError("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_PROBES_JSON must be a non-empty array")

        targets: list[ProbeTarget] = []
        sqlite_paths: set[Path] = set()
        for item in configs:
            if not isinstance(item, dict):
                raise ValueError("each Guardian probe config must be an object")
            target_id = item.get("id")
            kind = item.get("type")
            if not isinstance(target_id, str) or not isinstance(kind, str):
                raise ValueError("each Guardian probe requires string id and type")
            if kind == "http":
                allowed = {"id", "type", "url", "timeout_seconds", "expected_statuses"}
                if set(item) - allowed or not isinstance(item.get("url"), str):
                    raise ValueError("HTTP probe config contains unknown keys or lacks url")
                timeout = _number(item.get("timeout_seconds", 3.0), "timeout_seconds")
                statuses_value = item.get("expected_statuses", [200])
                if not isinstance(statuses_value, list) or any(type(x) is not int for x in statuses_value):
                    raise ValueError("expected_statuses must be an integer array")
                probe = HttpProbe(item["url"], timeout_seconds=timeout, expected_statuses=tuple(statuses_value))
            elif kind == "sqlite":
                allowed = {"id", "type", "path", "timeout_seconds"}
                if set(item) - allowed or not isinstance(item.get("path"), str):
                    raise ValueError("SQLite probe config contains unknown keys or lacks path")
                path = Path(item["path"]).expanduser()
                if not path.is_absolute():
                    raise ValueError("SQLite probe path must be absolute")
                path = path.resolve()
                sqlite_paths.add(path)
                timeout = _number(item.get("timeout_seconds", 1.0), "timeout_seconds")
                probe = SqliteDatabaseProbe(path, timeout_seconds=timeout)
            else:
                raise ValueError("Guardian probe type must be http or sqlite")
            targets.append(ProbeTarget(target_id, probe))

        if len({target.target_id for target in targets}) != len(targets):
            raise ValueError("Guardian probe IDs must be unique")
        if db_path in sqlite_paths:
            raise ValueError("Guardian database must not be the same file as a monitored SQLite database")

        raw_sinks = source.get("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_ALERT_SINKS_JSON", "").strip()
        if not raw_sinks:
            raise ValueError("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_ALERT_SINKS_JSON must configure independent alert routes")
        try:
            sink_configs = json.loads(raw_sinks)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            raise ValueError("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_ALERT_SINKS_JSON is invalid JSON") from None
        if not isinstance(sink_configs, list) or len(sink_configs) < 2:
            raise ValueError("Guardian requires at least two independent alert routes")

        alert_sinks: dict[str, WebhookAlertSink] = {}
        hosts: set[str] = set()
        for item in sink_configs:
            if not isinstance(item, dict) or set(item) != {"id", "url", "token_env"}:
                raise ValueError("each alert route must contain exactly id, url, and token_env")
            sink_id, url, token_env = item["id"], item["url"], item["token_env"]
            if not all(isinstance(value, str) and value for value in (sink_id, url, token_env)):
                raise ValueError("alert route fields must be non-empty strings")
            if not re.fullmatch(r"[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,127}", token_env):
                raise ValueError("token_env must name an environment variable")
            token = source.get(token_env, "")
            if not token:
                raise ValueError(f"credential environment variable {token_env} is required")
            parsed = urlparse(url)
            if not parsed.hostname:
                raise ValueError("alert route URL must include a hostname")
            hostname = parsed.hostname.lower()
            if hostname in hosts:
                raise ValueError("alert routes must use distinct hostnames for failure isolation")
            hosts.add(hostname)
            if sink_id in alert_sinks:
                raise ValueError("alert sink IDs must be unique")
            alert_sinks[sink_id] = WebhookAlertSink(
                url=url,
                bearer_token=token,
                timeout_seconds=_number(source.get("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_ALERT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS", "5"), "alert timeout"),
                sink_id=sink_id,
            )

        interval = _number(source.get("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_INTERVAL_SECONDS", "10"), "interval")
        failure_threshold = _integer(source.get("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_FAILURE_THRESHOLD", "3"), "failure threshold")
        recovery_threshold = _integer(source.get("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_RECOVERY_THRESHOLD", "2"), "recovery threshold")
        max_retry_seconds = _integer(source.get("AARIYA_GUARDIAN_MAX_RETRY_SECONDS", "900"), "maximum retry")
        if not 1 <= interval <= 3600:
            raise ValueError("Guardian interval must be between 1 and 3600 seconds")
        if not 1 <= failure_threshold <= 100 or not 1 <= recovery_threshold <= 100:
            raise ValueError("Guardian thresholds must be between 1 and 100")
        if not 1 <= max_retry_seconds <= 86400:
            raise ValueError("Guardian maximum retry delay must be between 1 and 86400 seconds")

        return cls(
            database_path=db_path,
            targets=tuple(targets),
            alert_sinks=alert_sinks,
            interval_seconds=interval,
            failure_threshold=failure_threshold,
            recovery_threshold=recovery_threshold,
            max_retry_seconds=max_retry_seconds,
        )


def _number(value: object, name: str) -> float:
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be numeric")
    try:
        number = float(value)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be numeric") from None
    if number != number or number in {float("inf"), float("-inf")}:
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be finite")
    return number


def _integer(value: object, name: str) -> int:
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be an integer")
    try:
        number = int(value)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be an integer") from None
    if str(value).strip() != str(number):
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be an integer")
    return number


from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import json
import logging
import signal
import sys

from .config import GuardianSettings
from .monitor import GuardianMonitor
from .store import GuardianStore


def _configure_logging() -> None:
    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format="%(message)s", stream=sys.stdout)


async def _main() -> None:
    _configure_logging()
    settings = GuardianSettings.from_env()
    store = GuardianStore(settings.database_path)
    monitor = GuardianMonitor(
        store=store,
        targets=settings.targets,
        alert_sinks=settings.alert_sinks,
        interval_seconds=settings.interval_seconds,
        failure_threshold=settings.failure_threshold,
        recovery_threshold=settings.recovery_threshold,
        max_retry_seconds=settings.max_retry_seconds,
    )
    stop_event = asyncio.Event()
    loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
    for sig in (signal.SIGINT, signal.SIGTERM):
        try:
            loop.add_signal_handler(sig, stop_event.set)
        except NotImplementedError:
            signal.signal(sig, lambda *_: loop.call_soon_threadsafe(stop_event.set))
    logging.info(json.dumps({"event": "guardian_started", "targets": [t.target_id for t in settings.targets]}))
    try:
        while not stop_event.is_set():
            try:
                report = await monitor.run_once()
                logging.info(json.dumps({"event": "guardian_iteration", "report": report}, separators=(",", ":")))
            except Exception as exc:
                # Keep diagnostic output stable and avoid logging possibly sensitive exception text.
                logging.error(json.dumps({"event": "guardian_iteration_failed", "error_type": type(exc).__name__}))
            try:
                await asyncio.wait_for(stop_event.wait(), timeout=settings.interval_seconds)
            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                pass
    finally:
        logging.info(json.dumps({"event": "guardian_stopped"}))


def main() -> None:
    try:
        asyncio.run(_main())
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        pass
    except Exception as exc:
        # Config errors must stop launch. Do not disclose URLs, tokens, or raw exception text.
        logging.error(json.dumps({"event": "guardian_startup_failed", "error_type": type(exc).__name__}))
        raise SystemExit(2) from None


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
